Company - Management

C5711 questions
C-5558C-5658C-5770C-57b58C-5842C-5954C-6046C-6154C-6250C-6358C-6445

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Management is the case's strongest single foundation and its clearest concentration risk at once. A founder-operator with a proven playbook, 8.56 percent of the equity and a record of fast action has reset the strategy to the one thing the business can control (margin), rebuilt the second tier with operators, and structured the German entry well; the mid-term record under his chairmanship is of a company that outperformed in easy conditions and missed in hard ones; communication is clear on direction and silent on mechanism; capital allocation has been good at buying and poor at pacing; governance is sound with founder-shaped edges. For the model, the right weight is to trust the margin direction and the pace of action, to discount the 8 percent timeline by a year against the founder's urgency (the sell-side's Swedish path of 7.2 percent in 2027 and 7.6 percent in 2028 is the sober version), and to carry key-person and culture risk as the main non-financial variance.

C-55

Size of ambition or vision, particularly against peers.

C58

Question asked How big is the company's ambition or vision (a force: growth as a priority, outcompeting, innovating, adapting, pushing), particularly in relation to peers?

Judgement: ambitious on paper (10 percent growth a year through the cycle, 8 percent margin, a German market five times the Nordics, a "European platform"), and for the next two years deliberately subordinated to repair; against peers the ambition is mid-ranked: larger than Burkhalter's domestic Swiss compounding and Bravida's "margin over volume" posture of 2024 to 2025, smaller than Nimlas's "4-40-4" (SEK 40bn of sales and SEK 4bn of EBITA by 2031, entering four new European markets), Bravida's 2026 pivot to data-centre growth, or SPIE's EUR 1bn of EBITA by 2028. The founder's personal ambition is demonstrated elsewhere (Green Landscaping's 26 percent CAGR; Eltel's turnaround) and here it is expressed as "margin, margin and margin" first.

  • The force the question asks about (growth as a priority, outcompeting, pushing) is present in the German design and in the organic start-ups and absent, for now, from the Nordic acquisition line. The sell-side notes that "without its primary growth engine running, Instalco cannot justify elevated multiples".
C-56

Quality of strategy: right and bold priorities; widening the moat; growth as a priority; dynamic across environments.

C58

Question asked How great and smart is the strategy, with right and bold strategic priorities (an enabler): widening the moat over the mid term, growth as a priority, and a dynamic strategy that can capitalise on a plausible range of environments?

Judgement: a good strategy for the environment it is in, not a great one. It is right (margin before volume, cash before acquisitions, a baseline over hands-off autonomy, a staged German platform rather than a greenfield), it is adaptive (the 2024 cuts and the 2025 pause show the model can shrink), and it preserves the one advantage the company has (local entrepreneurship). It is not bold on the moat: nothing in it widens pricing power, closes the digital gap or builds a specialised capability (data centres, grids, automation at scale) of the kind that has re-rated Bravida and SPIE; Inmatiq and Intec are the nearest things and they are small. Growth is a stated priority and a paused practice.

  • Dynamic across environments: the strategy worked in 2017 to 2021 (low rates, rising volumes, cheap targets) and failed in 2022 to 2025 (inflation, falling volumes, leverage); the 2.0 version adds the controls that would have caught the second environment earlier. Whether it works in a third environment (recovery with rising rates) is the test now running.
C-57

The chief executive, judged by actions.

C70

Question asked How great is the chief executive, judged by actions rather than conjecture (an enabler, not an ossifier)?

Judgement: a strong operator by action, with two blemishes. Actions for: co-founded the company in 2014 and took it from SEK 570m to SEK 8,890m of sales by 2021 at an 8.2 percent adjusted EBITA margin and 1.8x leverage, listing it in 2017; returned on 1 August 2025 and within one quarter the margin went from 6.0 to 7.2 percent, leverage from 3.3x to 2.8x, the dividend was cut, Swedish acquisitions paused, thirteen business areas collapsed to three countries, eight subsidiaries closed; bought shares in the open market in Q4 2025; takes no warrants; chaired Green Landscaping from 2015 to 2026 through a 26 percent revenue CAGR at 4 to 6x multiples and Eltel through eleven consecutive quarters of margin improvement. Actions against: he chaired Instalco from 2021 while the margin fell from 8.2 to 5.9 percent and leverage rose to 3.3x, which makes his return a correction of something done on his watch; he held six external chairmanships at the time, two to three times the Swedish norm, and still holds Eltel and Knowit; and his interim pay ran through a consultancy company under a board derogation. He is an enabler, not an ossifier: the research's "animal CEO" archetype fits and the counter-case (that his return "may entrench legacy acquisition strategies and block fresh external ideas") is noted and not evidenced.

C-57b

Hiring well as a priority, and the quality of the leadership team built.

C58

Question asked How high a priority is hiring well for the chief executive, and how great is the leadership team the chief executive has built (an enabler)?

Judgement: hiring and placing operators is visibly a priority (country managers and COOs appointed within a month of his return; a subsidiary founder with SEK 42m of stock put in charge of Sweden; Fabri's CEO brought into group management), and the team is credible but thin at the top. CFO Christina Kassberg (since November 2021; Addtech, Resurs, Medivir) has delivered the cash and working-capital record (108 percent conversion, working capital 2.8 to 1.6 percent of sales) and is the financial anchor; Johan Larsson ran Dalab at a 9 percent average EBIT margin before taking Sweden; Patrik Persson (COO Sweden, with the group since 2018), Roger Aksnes (Norway, a former pipe-layer), Jukka-Pekka Kujala (Finland, logistics and aviation), Anders Lundin (Intec). Against: the Head of M&A left in July 2025 and was not visibly replaced, the Extended Management Team was dismantled, and the group has no deputy to the founder, so key-person risk is concentrated. No external star hires; the model's bench is the subsidiary CEOs, whose retention is the risk.

C-58

How well resourced and invested is the whole business (a buffer; built yesterday for resilience)?

❌ D42

Question asked How well resourced and invested is the whole business (a buffer; an ant colony: built yesterday for resilience)?

Judgement: lean rather than resilient. The business is asset-light by design (capex 0.7 percent of sales; a central team of a handful of functions; no inventory), which is efficient and is not a buffer. Its balance sheet is the opposite of a buffer (leverage 2.6x, goodwill 145 percent of equity, SEK 1,050m of facility headroom); its digital investment lags Bravida and SPIE; its people investment is the Instalco Academy and warrant programmes for subsidiary managers, with turnover at 15.8 percent; its apprentice pipeline is not disclosed (Burkhalter trains 19 percent of its workforce as apprentices and SPIE 504 in Germany alone). The resilience the company showed in 2024 to 2025 came from the variable cost base and the founder, not from reserves built in advance. The "ant colony" test is failed on capital and passed on culture.

C-59

Holistic and congruent culture across the whole system.

C54

Question asked How holistic and congruent is the culture, with alignment across the total system (as at Lifco, Avanza or Ryanair)? Such that the goals, strategy, culture, execution, decisions are congruent, holistic and symbiotic?

Judgement: more congruent than most roll-ups and less congruent than the Lifco archetype it resembles. The pieces fit: a decentralised model of founder-run subsidiaries, a founder-CEO with 8.56 percent, earn-outs and lock-ups tying acquired owners to results, warrants that managers pay for at market value and that pay off only above 115 percent of the measurement price, eNPS of 31 against Bravida's 14, a strategy (margin) that subsidiary owners understand because it is their own economics. The frictions are real: the baseline and country COOs are "a notable cultural tightening" of a system built on being left alone; turnover rose from 13.1 to 15.8 percent in two years; lock-ups expire at 36 months; the 2023/2026 warrants are far out of the money (SEK 64.90 against a SEK 42.52 price); and the research's experts flag that founders leave after earn-outs. Congruence between goals, strategy and execution has improved since August 2025; congruence between the control system and the culture is the open question.

  • Lifco contrast from the Leadership digest: Lifco avoids standardised reviews; Instalco now runs them. That is a deliberate choice to trade some autonomy for control, and it is the correct choice after 2024, but the Consumer should not model the culture as self-reinforcing.
C-60

Clarity and helpfulness of communication, on strategy and on the driver transmission.

❌ D46

Question asked How clear, straightforward and helpful is the company's communication, including on the strategy and the driver transmission mechanism?

Judgement: clear on direction, unhelpful on mechanism. The messages are short and consistent ("margin, margin and margin"; "discipline over volume"; "pull not push"), segment reporting was restructured to countries with two years of restated history (24 March 2026), and the Q2 2026 call explained the cash shortfall plainly (late invoicing, overdue receivables unchanged). But there is no numerical guidance, no dated bridge from 6.4 to 8 percent, no disclosure of backlog duration, fixed-price share, subsidiary write-downs, the ABC programme's effect or Fabri's option formulas; the transmission mechanism in the model is the broker's (bad debt, revenue per employee, volume), not the company's; and from Q3 2026 Finland and Germany report as one segment with a one-month lag, which reduces visibility of both. The research's phrase, that the model is "inherently unanalysable with high precision", is as much a comment on disclosure as on structure.

C-61

Record of acting, not talking: recent delivery and mid-term track record, whatever the environment.

C54

Question asked How strong is the company's record of acting, not just talking: a recent record of huge delivery and action, and a mid-term track record of delivery, not misses or justifications, whatever the environment (outperforming in tougher ones is even better), beyond its records against guidance and estimates?

Judgement: the recent record (fifteen months) is one of large, fast, visible action: the closures and headcount cuts, the dividend cut, the pause, the reorganisation, the German step, a 120 basis point margin recovery in one quarter and 70 basis points year on year in Q2 2026, leverage from 3.3x to 2.6x in three quarters, two consecutive quarterly beats. The mid-term record (five years) is one of a company that delivered in an easy environment (31 percent sales CAGR 2016 to 2020 at a 7.8 percent margin; above 8 percent every year 2017 to 2021) and did not in a hard one (margin 8.2 to 5.9 percent; organic -6.5 percent in 2024; all three financial targets missed in FY2025; write-downs in Norway in 2021 and on Northvolt in 2025; leverage breaching target in 2024 and 2025). The outperformance-in-tough-environments test is failed for 2022 to 2025 and is being retaken now.

  • Against guidance and estimates specifically, see C-69; the pattern there is the same: beats in recoveries, misses in downturns.
C-62

Outlier standards: driving higher, faster, better, with a record of out-executing competitors.

C50

Question asked To what extent does the company hold outlier standards, driving higher, faster and better, with a record of out-executing competitors?

Judgement: historically yes, currently being re-established. The 8 percent-plus margin of 2017 to 2021 against a Nordic peer average of 5.5 percent, the Finnish segment's 12 percent at full utilisation, receivables days 27 percent better than Bravida's, eNPS double Bravida's and turnover a quarter lower are outlier standards. But in 2025 privately owned Nimlas and Assemblin Caverion out-earned Instalco Sweden, Bravida managed utilisation swings better on revenue per employee, and Instalco's Swedish margin (7.0 percent) still trails Assemblin Caverion's group 7.9 percent. The standard being set now (a baseline, self-assessments, deviations caught early) is a floor-raising exercise, not an outlier-setting one.

C-63

Shareholders first, ahead of management; prudence of governance; the evidence.

C58

Question asked Explain, with the evidence, the extent to which the company puts shareholders first, ahead of management, and how prudent its governance is (scaffolding put in place even where it was not needed): what does the evidence show, and why?

Judgement: broadly shareholder-first with founder-shaped edges. The evidence for: a founder-CEO with 8.56 percent who buys in the open market and takes no warrants; a single share class with equal votes and no control rights; six of seven directors independent, including a former Bravida CFO and construction and facilities-management executives; warrant programmes that managers pay for at market value, that pay off only above 115 percent of the measurement price, and that cap dilution at 0.7 to 0.9 percent each; variable pay capped at 50 percent of base (Bravida 125 to 200 percent); a dividend cut taken promptly rather than funded with debt; a staged German entry with founders locked in rather than a bid for control; audit committee attendance 100 percent; a change-of-control clause in the facility that protects lenders, not management. The evidence against: the founder is also a director (the sole non-independent), his interim pay ran through a consultancy under a derogation (SEK 1.94m plus TSEK 335), he was over-boarded, the auditor has served since 2015, shares were issued for acquisitions in the past, and the board's own holdings (161,421 shares across six non-executives) are small. No scaffolding of the kind the question praises (an internal audit function, say) is mentioned; the decentralised model relies on quarterly subsidiary control reports and an externally run whistleblowing channel. Prudent, not exemplary.

C-64

Enlightened capital allocator and dynamic repurchaser, with a record of high-return deployment.

❌ D45

Question asked To what extent is the company an enlightened capital allocator and a dynamic repurchaser of its shares, not just an operator or generator, with a record of allocating capital at a high return on new invested capital?

Judgement: a capable acquirer in the good years and a cautious repairer now; not a dynamic repurchaser and not, on the record, an enlightened allocator through a cycle. For: more than 150 acquisitions at 3 to 6x EV/EBITA building a business with 18 percent ROCE in 2021; the German structure; the willingness to stop buying and to cut the dividend. Against: the pace was maintained through 2022 to 2023 while cash conversion fell and leverage reached 3.3x, so capital was deployed at the top of the market with debt and equity; ROCE fell to 9.6 percent; shares have been issued to fund deals (SEK 138m for Fabri step 1) where Lifco and Addtech do not; the buyback mandate has never been used; the FY2024 dividend was paid at a 49.7 percent payout and then cut; and Fabri step 2 was funded with debt at 2.6x leverage. The test of enlightenment is what happens when leverage passes below 2.5x: a return to SEK 800m a year of bolt-ons at 3 to 6x would re-establish the record; buybacks at 12 to 13x earnings are not on the agenda and should not be modelled.

Risks

❌ D394 questions
C-8064C-8142C-8236C-8334

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The risks are of the roll-up kind and they are correlated: a write-down or a customer failure weakens cash, weak cash keeps leverage above 2.5x, leverage above target keeps the engine off and the multiple in its engine-off band, and in a regime refusing leverage a share that has re-rated ahead of its earnings gives back 25 percent on the multiple alone. None is the modal path; the research's own winning hypothesis for the bear is the "false friend" (headline growth masking cash strain), and it is answered quarter by quarter by the cash line. The perception risks are the more immediate (a Bravida or Fasadgruppen read-across, a Swedish rate sell-off, the lead broker's call) because the price has done in nine months what the earnings will take two years to do. The model should carry a recurring SEK 50m to 75m of write-downs and items in the reported case, a one-in-three chance that cash conversion stays below 100 percent through 2027, and a trough multiple of about 10x for the downside branch.

C-80

Has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months; is revenue growth below the company's normal rate; is the input change force slowing?

C64

Question asked To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

Judgement: the organic engine has accelerated, not decelerated (organic from -0.3 percent in FY2025 to +8.4 percent in Q2 2026); the acquired engine, which was the company's normal growth engine (acquired growth 14.3 percent in FY2023; total revenue growth 25 to 36 percent a year in 2019 to 2022), has been switched off (0.7 percent in FY2025, 1.1 percent in Q2 2026, SEK 56m of consideration in H1 2026) and is being restarted by Fabri alone. Total revenue growth (+10.3 percent in Q2 2026; consensus +11.7 percent FY2026) is therefore well below the company's historical normal and in line with its through-cycle target. The input force (the Swedish cycle) is early and not slowing; the input to the acquired engine (leverage below 2.5x) is not yet present.

  • For the model: do not treat the +8.4 percent organic rate as the company's normal; its through-cycle organic rate is low-to-mid single digits (4.6 percent in FY2023, -6.5 percent in FY2024). Treat the acquired rate as zero outside Germany until leverage prints below 2.5x, then 5 to 8 percent a year.
C-81

Fundamental risks that are sudden, high-magnitude and low-probability; plausibility and size of each.

❌ D42

Question asked What fundamental risks are there that are sudden, high-magnitude and low-probability, and how plausible and how large is each?

Judgement: five, in order of plausibility times magnitude.

  1. A large fixed-price project write-down or a cluster of them (the sector's characteristic failure; Instalco's own in Norway 2021; Caverion EUR 59m, Eltel EUR 55m, Sweco SEK 120m). Plausibility medium-high (43 percent fixed-price, 150 autonomous units, a 2027 wage round on un-indexed Swedish contracts, metals volatile); magnitude SEK 50m to 150m, or 5 to 15 percent of a year's adjusted EBITA, and 15 to 40 percent of a quarter's. Leading indicator: accrued costs outrunning percentage-of-completion revenue in monthly reviews; not visible from outside.
  2. A customer insolvency (construction companies 48 percent of sales; Northvolt cost SEK 64m; Nordic construction bankruptcies elevated; 8.5 percent of Nordic firms in distress). Plausibility medium; magnitude SEK 50m to 100m. Leading indicator: receivables ageing past three months above SEK 95m.
  3. A dilutive equity raise, triggered by (1) or (2) coinciding with weak cash and goodwill at 145 percent of equity pushing leverage toward covenants. Plausibility low-to-medium (the research's recurring "other side"; the memo gives the working-capital build not reversing about one in three and the raise as its consequence); magnitude 20 to 40 percent dilution on the Fasadgruppen precedent. Leading indicator: rolling cash conversion below 90 percent at Q4 2026 with leverage not below 2.5x.
  4. The Fabri option ladder costing more than expected or Fabri's margin halving (German heat-pump demand is "extremely sensitive" to subsidy changes; option formulas undisclosed). Plausibility low; magnitude a few percent of group EBITA plus an unknown cash call in 2029 to 2033.
  5. Founder incapacity or departure (sole executive director, no named successor, ties his tenure to the 8 percent). Plausibility low; magnitude large on the multiple (the multiple is company-driven, five-year range 7.86x to 34.13x). Currency is a slow risk, not a sudden one (a 20 percent NOK and EUR shock is SEK 0.14 of EPS, about 6 percent).
C-82

Perception- or sentiment-driven risks that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere.

❌ D36

Question asked What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

Judgement: six, and they are more plausible than the fundamental ones because the share has already re-rated 63 percent in twelve months on a recovery the earnings have not yet delivered, in a regime that is "refusing re-ratings" and leverage.

  1. A governance event at any decentralised Nordic installer read across (Bravida fell 10 percent in a day on municipal over-invoicing allegations; convictions followed; public clients are 9 to 20 percent of Instalco's sales). Plausibility medium over three years; magnitude 10 to 20 percent.
  2. A Bravida de-rating (the anchor for the "compounder" multiple branch; its data-centre contracts carry principal-contractor risk and its co-location customers are externally financed inside the artificial-intelligence credit crack). Plausibility medium; magnitude the loss of the 16x branch, 15 to 25 percent of the price.
  3. A Riksbank hiking cycle and a Swedish rate-sensitivity sell-off (Sweden downgraded to Neutral in the weekly digest; six Swedish memos note the country tilt). Plausibility high; magnitude 10 to 15 percent for a levered Swedish small cap.
  4. A rights-issue precedent in the cohort (Fasadgruppen -52 percent in twelve months) read across on any cash miss. Plausibility medium; magnitude 15 to 25 percent on a weak Q3 or Q4 cash print.
  5. Single-broker dependence: DNB Carnegie dominates coverage, Nordea moved to Hold on 19 October 2026; a downgrade by the lead house on a crowded register (USD 2.9m a day of turnover; relative-strength percentile 91) would be amplified. Plausibility medium; magnitude 10 to 15 percent.
  6. The Q3 report itself on 23 October 2026 against a price that has moved 10 percent in three months: a Swedish margin below 7.5 percent or operating cash flow not recovering toward SEK 200m would hit a share priced for both. Plausibility one in three to one in two on each; magnitude 10 to 20 percent.
C-83

How far could the shares fall to a fair trough multiple?

❌ D34

Judgement: 23 to 30 percent to the stock's own ten-year trough band, about 40 percent to its five-year low, with the earnings at risk on top. On consensus EPS 24 months forward of SEK 3.28, the ten-year 10th percentile of 10.1x gives SEK 33 (-22 percent); the five-year 10th percentile of 9.38x gives SEK 31 (-28 percent); the five-year low of 7.86x gives SEK 26 (-40 percent). DNB Carnegie's bear case (0 percent organic growth, margin capped at 6.5 percent, 9x EV/EBITA) and the Change Forces digest's severe scenario (2027 EPS SEK 1.80) put the trough earnings a third below consensus; at 10x on SEK 1.80 to 2.20 the price is SEK 18 to 22, the 52-week low region. The multiple is company-driven (five-year range 7.86x to 34.13x) and the stock sat at 7.86x in early 2026, so the trough is recent, not theoretical. Mark-to-market downside with earnings intact: about 25 percent; with earnings and multiple both at trough: about 50 percent.

  • Current reference: SEK 42.52; 24-month-forward P/E 12.9x at the 46th percentile of ten years and the 90th of two years (two-year median 10.26x); peers' median about 11.7x; Bravida 16.5x; the memo's blend 14.0x; the memo's buy level about SEK 37.

Guidance

❌ D436 questions
C-6850C-7038C-7140C-7232C-8554C-7344

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Guidance is the weakest of the company's communication tools and the Consumer should not build from it: five undated through-cycle targets, no annual guidance, no bridge, no restructuring arithmetic, a dividend that was cut, and a margin target the brokers have placed in 2028. What the company does say informally is consistent, candid on the risks and confirmatory of the direction the numbers already show; what it does not say (how, by when, with what building blocks) the lead broker says for it. Treat the 8 percent as the top of the cone on a two-year view and consensus's 2028 as the modal date; treat the Q3 and Q4 2026 cash prints as the only guidance events that matter.

C-68

Company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this year and next, and why.

C50

Question asked To what extent is company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

Judgement: the company gives no annual guidance, so the comparison is between its undated through-cycle targets and the three brokers' numbers. On margin the target (at least 8 percent EBITA) sits above consensus for FY2026 (about 7.0 percent) and FY2027 (about 7.8 percent) and is met by consensus only in FY2028 (about 8.0 percent); on growth the target (at least 10 percent a year) sits below consensus for FY2026 and FY2027 (+11.7 and +13.0 percent, carried by Fabri) and above it for FY2028 (+4.0 percent, no acquisitions modelled); on leverage the target (2.5x) is below the current 2.6x and consensus assumes it is reached by end-2026; on cash conversion the target (100 percent) is above the current 90 percent and consensus assumes it from FY2027. The "why" is that the brokers have decided to believe the founder on direction and to discount him on speed: they put the 8 percent two years out, where he puts it as urgent. The record's own path is below consensus by 3, 7 and 8 percent on EPS, almost all margin.

  • The sub-target that matters: DNB Carnegie's Swedish segment path is 7.2 percent in 2027 and 7.6 percent in 2028, "which remains below the company's target". The group reaches 8 percent in consensus only because Finland, Norway and Germany are modelled above the Swedish segment.
  • One basis caution for FY2026: the three brokers' EPS ranges from SEK 1.79 (Pareto) to SEK 2.30 (DNB Carnegie), a 28 percent spread that is partly definitional (amortisation treatment) and partly Pareto's lower margin; the FactSet median is SEK 2.28 and the mean SEK 2.16.
C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance.

❌ D38

Question asked Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

Judgement: there is no record of upgrading, because there is no guidance to upgrade; the targets have been the same five numbers for years and the only recent numerical signal (the dividend) went down. The founder's behaviour is to state an ambition and tie his tenure to it rather than to date it; the Change Forces digest's label is "over-promiser tendencies during downturns" (FY2025 organic -1.0 percent against a 10 percent target, margin 5.9 against 8, leverage 2.8x against 2.5x) and "shifting from over-promising to disciplined execution tracking" since his return. The sell-side does the upgrading on his behalf: DNB Carnegie's target went SEK 50 (2025) to SEK 40 (mid-2025 management change) to SEK 42.5 to SEK 42 to SEK 47 (July 2026); Nordea sits at Hold SEK 45 (19 October 2026). The Consumer should expect no upward guidance event; the upgrade events are prints.

C-71

Conservatism of guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five, adjusted for the difference in change forces.

❌ D40

Question asked How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five years, adjusted for the difference in input change forces versus the past?

Judgement: not conservative; ambitious against the last five years and plausible against the last ten. Against FY2021 to FY2025 (margin 8.2 to 5.9 percent, organic +4.6 to -6.5 percent, leverage 1.8x to 3.3x), the targets are a return to the best year. Adjusted for change forces, they become reachable but not conservative: the forces that produced 2017 to 2021 (low rates, rising volumes, SEK 800m a year of bolt-ons at 3 to 6x) are only partly back (volumes rising, rates rising, bolt-ons off), and the forces that produced 2022 to 2025 (inflation on fixed-price work, a hands-off control model) are only partly gone (the 2027 wage round is coming; the control model is being rebuilt). The 8 percent is therefore the top of the cone rather than the middle of it on a two-year view, and consensus has it right to place it in 2028; the 10 percent growth target is reachable only with the engine on; the 2.5x and 100 percent are reachable in 2026 to 2027 if the cash reverses as the company says.

C-72

Specificity of guidance: stated building blocks, or vague?

❌ D32

Question asked How specific is the guidance: built from stated building blocks, or vague about how results come about?

Judgement: vague. The company states destinations and methods and no building blocks: there is no figure for the bad-debt normalisation, the revenue-per-employee gain, the legacy-contract roll-off, the Finland or Norway margin path, the Fabri accretion, the restructuring saving (contrast Bravida's SEK 65m on SEK 90m to 110m of charges) or the timing of any of them. The bridge in use by the market (lower bad debt, 5 percent revenue per employee, volume) is DNB Carnegie's. Segment disclosure improved in 2026 (country reporting with restated history) and backlog is reported quarterly with organic and currency splits; backlog duration, the fixed-price share of backlog, subsidiary-level write-downs and contingent consideration by segment are not disclosed. The research's data-gap sections list these as requiring "direct CFO questioning during upcoming Q3 and Q4 earnings calls".

C-85

Informal or qualitative commentary above or below formal guidance and consensus, and why.

C54

Question asked To what extent is informal/qualitative company commentary above or below formal guidance and sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

Judgement: above formal guidance on urgency and tone, roughly in line with consensus on substance, and candid on the two things that could go wrong. The CEO's informal line is more bullish than the targets imply: "margin, margin and margin"; the internal improvements "are taking deep root"; the Q2 margin expansion with "significant ground still to cover"; artificial intelligence "one of the most powerful efficiency drivers" of his career; Fabri as "a long-term growth engine" in a market five times the Nordics; Finland's 12 percent as "exceptionally high" and bound to fluctuate (candour); Swedish legacy low-margin contracts "diminishing over the next several quarters" (a dated qualitative commitment); the Q2 cash shortfall as timing with overdue receivables unchanged (a testable claim); and "customer decision-making remains slow" with recovery "highly uneven" (caution). The CFO: leverage "stable" with "a comfortable cushion" to covenants; the Fabri step funded without equity. Why above formal: the founder's personal stake and tenure commitment make urgency his signal; why in line with consensus: nothing in the commentary dates the 8 percent earlier than the brokers' 2028, and management has not contradicted the broker's Swedish path. The Consumer should read the commentary as confirming direction and the 2027 margin step, and as giving no licence to model 8 percent before FY2028.

C-73

Anything else material or uncommon about guidance or investor relations.

❌ D44

Question asked What else is material or uncommon about the company's guidance or investor relations?

Judgement: five things. (1) Coverage is thin and concentrated: three brokers in FactSet, DNB Carnegie dominant with several named analysts, Nordea at Hold since 19 October 2026, no expert-call coverage found in the last two results windows; a single house's model is effectively the market's model. (2) Fabri reports with a one-month lag and inside a merged Finland and Germany segment from Q3 2026, so the two most volatile margin lines in the group are reported together and late. (3) The company publishes restated segment history promptly (24 March 2026) and clear organic and currency splits, which is better than Assemblin Caverion (private) and most of the cohort. (4) There is no capital markets day on record and no numerical guidance, which is uncommon for a listed roll-up with five financial targets. (5) The next report is tomorrow (23 October 2026), the same day as Bravida's Q3, and the Q4 report is in January 2027; the sell-side's thresholds for the print are specific (Sweden margin above 7.5 percent; operating cash flow toward SEK 200m; leverage toward 2.5x; Fabri above 10 percent in its first months), so the print will be read as a pass or fail rather than as a distribution.

Sell-side estimates

C513 questions
C-6750C-6950C-7455

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The three brokers have modelled the company the founder is restoring, with the 8 percent margin in 2028, Fabri in the revenue, the acquisition engine out of it, and a financing line that assumes a world the Riksbank is leaving. The operating gap to history is credible (a recovery to the company's own best, two to three years out, on utilisation and mix); the financing gap is not; the acquisition line is the upside no one has written down. The record says the company beats in recoveries and misses in downturns, the cut cycle ended in Q1 2026, and the revision momentum is now positive on every line and propped by targets and ratings as much as by earnings. For the model: adopt consensus revenue and the FY2027 margin, haircut the FY2028 margin by about 20 to 40 basis points, hold net financial items flat rather than falling, carry tax at 26 to 28 percent and minorities rising, and carry the M&A restart as a dated scenario rather than the base.

C-67

Sell-side forecasts for the next one to three years against the last one to five, line by line, and the credibility of the gap.

C50

Question asked How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items in particular and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

Judgement: credible on revenue, plausible on EBITDA and margin for 2027, optimistic on 2028 margin and on the lines below operating profit; the record's own path is 3, 7 and 8 percent below consensus EPS on these grounds.

Line by line (brokers' basis for consensus; history as stated):

  • Revenue: SEK 13,599m (FY2025) to SEK 15,193m, 17,171m, 17,863m (+11.7, +13.0, +4.0 percent) against +29, +25, +25, +36, +18, -4, -1 percent for FY2019 to FY2025. Credible on the whole, with one demanding piece: FY2026 and FY2027 are the Swedish recovery plus Fabri's SEK 1.3bn annualised, and stripping Fabri out leaves implied organic growth of about 8 percent in FY2026 (fair, on trough comparatives) and 6 to 8 percent in FY2027 (demanding, against comparatives that harden from Q1 2027; the segment consensus has Sweden +6.5 percent and Norway +8.5 percent); FY2028's +4.0 percent carries no acquisitions, which is conservative if the engine restarts and right if it does not. Sweden FY2026 consensus SEK 10,416m to SEK 11,578m by FY2028 (+5.5 percent a year); Finland SEK 2,296m to SEK 3,403m (+48 percent, the Fabri effect); Norway SEK 2,469m to SEK 2,817m.
  • EBITDA (brokers' basis): SEK 1,229m to SEK 1,495m, 1,770m, 1,895m (+21.6, +18.4, +7.1 percent); margin 9.0 percent to 9.8, 10.3, 10.6 percent, against a FactSet operating EBITDA margin that was 10.2 percent in FY2019 to FY2020 and 8.0 percent in FY2025 (not comparable in level, comparable in shape). Credible for FY2027 (utilisation, bad debt, Fabri mix); FY2028 assumes the full 8 percent EBITA.
  • EBIT: SEK 936m, 1,198m, 1,303m (6.2, 7.0, 7.3 percent); EBITA about 7.0, 7.8, 8.0 percent after adding back about SEK 135m of amortisation. The FY2027 step (+28 percent EBIT on +13 percent revenue) is the margin recovery and Fabri; the FY2028 step (+9 percent) is margin only.
  • Depreciation and amortisation: SEK 402m, 358m, 348m, falling as acquired intangibles roll off; a reported-EPS tailwind the adjusted line does not show.
  • Net financial items (pre-tax less EBIT): about SEK 234m, 171m, 133m, from SEK 167m in FY2025 and a Q2 2026 run-rate above SEK 240m. This is the least credible line: it needs leverage to fall to the broker's 1.6x by 2027 and rates not to rise, and the Riksbank is signalling the opposite. A flat SEK 220m instead of SEK 133m in FY2028 is about SEK 0.24 of EPS, 7 percent.
  • Tax and minorities: implied about 25 percent of pre-tax profit combined; FY2025's tax rate alone was 28.1 percent, and minorities rise with Fabri (49 percent of a growing German profit). A further 2 to 3 percent of EPS at risk.
  • EPS: SEK 1.76 to 2.28, 3.01, 3.42 (+29, +32, +14 percent), against SEK 2.67 (FY2023 peak) and SEK 1.81 (FY2024). Range among the three brokers: FY2026 SEK 1.79 to 2.30; FY2027 SEK 2.55 to 3.01; FY2028 SEK 3.14 to 3.42. The FY2027 figure (SEK 3.01) is 13 percent above the FY2023 peak on 20 percent more revenue; it is a recovery number, not a growth number.
  • Cash and balance sheet: operating cash flow SEK 1,048m, 1,370m, 1,477m; free cash flow SEK 672m, 1,101m, 1,210m; capex SEK 118m to 136m (0.8 percent); dividends SEK 0.70, 0.90, 0.90 (about 30 percent payout); equity SEK 3,821m to 5,130m (retained earnings, no buybacks). The FY2027 cash step (+31 percent operating cash flow) assumes conversion back at 100 percent; the Q2 2026 reading was 90 percent.
  • Granular items the brokers use: bad-debt normalisation and revenue per employee +5 percent (the margin bridge); a Swedish segment at 7.2 and 7.6 percent (2027, 2028); Fabri "mid-single-digit" accretion to 2027 EBITA; a base-case M&A restart of SEK 800m a year from 2027 in DNB Carnegie's scenario work that is not in the FactSet revenue median.

Credibility of the gap overall: the operating lines are consistent with the change forces and foundations found above (utilisation on a pruned base, bad debt normalising, Fabri's mix), with the 2028 margin at the top of the cone; the financing lines are not consistent with the rate regime; the acquisition line is absent and is the upside. Revision dynamics support the operating view: FY2027 EPS +8 percent and FY2028 +8 percent over six months; FY2026 -4.8 percent over six months and -14 percent over twelve (a basis and timing cut, now reversing: +2.0 percent over three months).

C-69

Record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and estimates over one, three and five years, including in comparable conditions.

C50

Question asked What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

Judgement: a cyclical record: beats in recoveries, misses in downturns, and the current phase is the recovery. Last year: Q3 2025 missed (DNB Carnegie cut adjusted earnings 11 percent on "weak summer project performance"); Q4 2025 beat (margin 7.2 percent, "founder back with a bang"); Q1 2026 beat at group level (sales +3.1 percent, EBITA +3.0 percent against consensus; organic 4.9 percent against 1.6 percent) with Sweden 14 percent below the lead broker's EBITA and Finland 130 percent above; Q2 2026 beat (sales +5 to 6 percent, EBITA +8 percent, organic 8.4 against 4.1 percent) with Finland below consensus and cash well below. Three years: FY2023 at the top (EPS SEK 2.67, brokers' basis), FY2024 a miss (EPS -32 percent, restructuring, 110 jobs), FY2025 all three financial targets missed (organic -1.0 against 10 percent, margin 5.9 against 8, leverage 2.8x against 2.5x) with cash conversion beaten (108 against 100). Five years: FY2021's 8.2 percent margin and FY2022's 36 percent growth met or beat; the subsequent slide missed by widening amounts; FY2026 consensus EPS fell 15 percent over twelve months from a high of SEK 3.31 to a low of SEK 2.07 before stabilising. Comparable conditions: the last recovery (2017 to 2018 and 2020 to 2021) was delivered with margins above 8 percent; the last downturn (2023 to 2025) was not managed. The model should expect beats on the operating lines through the easy-comparative quarters and should not extrapolate them past Q1 2027.

C-74

Have estimates been cut deeply and fully across all analysts; has guidance been rebased lower, kitchen-sinked or sandbagged?

C55

Question asked To what extent have sell-side estimates been cut deeply and fully across all analysts, and has company guidance recently been rebased lower, or more aggressively kitchen-sinked or sandbagged?

Judgement: the cut cycle happened and has ended. FY2026 consensus EPS was cut 15 percent over twelve months (range SEK 2.07 to 3.24) and FY2025 by 10 percent, across the covering houses (DNB Carnegie's target fell from SEK 50 to SEK 40 in mid-2025; Pareto's FY2026 EPS of SEK 1.79 is the low mark); FY2027 was never cut as deeply (+1.6 percent over twelve months) and FY2028 is being raised. The company rebased only the dividend (SEK 0.68 to 0.50) and kept every target, so there was no kitchen-sink and no sandbag; the recurring "non-recurring" items (SEK 65m, 29m, 64m, 11m, 75m) are the nearest thing to a cleared deck, and they are restructuring and bad debt rather than discretionary provisions. The register of estimate momentum is now positive on every line (EPS +2, +4, +8 percent over one, three and six months; EBITDA +11 percent and sales +11 percent over six months; target price +24 percent over six months; buy share 86 percent, up 14 points), which means the downside from here is from new cuts, not from the completion of old ones. The Wisdom Library's warning applies: the composite is propped by target-price and rating moves (the dashboard pins sell-side momentum at A) while the FY2026 earnings line was still being cut six months ago; decompose before trusting.

Stock market narrative

C521 question
C-2254

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The narrative is a restoration story the market has already told itself: founder back, margin recovering, Sweden turning, Germany as the next decade. Its next chapters are dated and confirmatory (the Q3 and Q4 prints, the year-end cash release, leverage below 2.5x) and its one novel chapter (a German platform that compounds at 12.7 percent margins) is the least disclosed and the furthest out. The share has moved from the bottom of its own range to the middle on the first chapter, so the staircase from here is one the company must climb to stand still, with the acquisition restart as the step that would re-rate it and the cash line as the step that would de-rate it.

C-22

The staircase of novel positive developments ahead: within 6 months, months 6 to 18, months 18 to 36; novelty; foundation.

C54

Question asked What staircase of novel positive developments lies ahead: which new, dated things can the market look forward to within the next 6 months, in months 6 to 18, and in months 18 to 36; how novel is each; and how well-founded?

Judgement: the staircase is dated and visible, and most of its steps are confirmations of a restoration rather than novelties; the one genuinely novel step (Germany as a platform) is the furthest out and the least founded in disclosure. The market has paid for the first flight of steps already (shares +63 percent in twelve months to the own-history median multiple); the next flight is where the earnings arrive.

Within 6 months (to April 2027):

  • 23 October 2026, Q3 2026 report (tomorrow): the seasonally strong Swedish quarter; the sell-side's thresholds are a Swedish segment margin of 7.5 percent or more (consensus about 7.2 percent for 2027 implies the quarter should print above 7), Swedish organic growth near 12 percent on easy comparatives, operating cash flow recovering toward SEK 200m, receivables down from SEK 2,009m, leverage toward 2.5x, and Fabri's first two months above a 10 percent margin inside the new Finland and Germany segment. Novelty: low (a confirmation). Foundation: high (dated, measurable). Bravida reports the same day, which sets the sector read.
  • November 2026, the Riksbank decision: a hike is the expected outcome; the stock's reaction will show whether the market has priced the rate path into a levered Swedish small cap.
  • Late 2026, Sweden's renovation-plan implementation guidelines under the buildings directive; Villaeffekten's first quarters. Novelty: low. Foundation: medium (timing has slipped once).
  • January 2027, Q4 2026 report: the year-end working-capital release (Q4 2025 released SEK 451m), rolling cash conversion at or above 100 percent, company net debt to EBITDA below 2.5x, the FY2026 dividend proposal (consensus SEK 0.70 against SEK 0.50), and any statement on resuming acquisitions. Novelty: medium (the first clean year-end since the founder's return). Foundation: high.
  • Late 2026 to early 2027: German bolt-ons through Fabri, if leverage allows; the first would be the signal that the engine is restarting.

Months 6 to 18 (April 2027 to April 2028):

  • Spring 2027, the Swedish collective wage round (electrical and plumbing and ventilation agreements; agreed minimum wages up about 8 percent per SEB; actual pay about 3 percent per Nordea and Swedbank): a negative step for fixed-price work priced in 2026, and the first real test of pricing discipline under the new regime.
  • Q1 and Q2 2027 reports: the comparatives harden (Q1 2026 +4.9 percent, Q2 2026 +8.4 percent organic), so this is where the underlying demand slope shows; the Swedish segment should be tracking toward the broker's 7.2 percent for the year; FY2027 consensus EPS SEK 3.01 is the number the price now rests on.
  • H1 2027, the acquisition engine's restart at the historical SEK 800m a year (the sell-side's base case; "implied late 2026, no explicit management commitment"): this is the step that would move the multiple from the engine-off band (10 to 12x) toward the engine-on band (13 to 14.5x), and the memo puts its odds around one in two if leverage is below 2.5x.
  • May 2027 AGM: the 2026/2029 warrant programme's first year; any change to the dividend policy or a buyback mandate being used (not expected).
  • Fabri's first full consolidated year, with the Finland and Germany segment's margin as the readable output; any German bolt-on at 7 to 9x.
  • A possible capital markets day or target refresh: none is announced; the five targets have stood for years; the founder's commitment to stay until 8 percent is "sustainably reached" makes a dated plan plausible once Sweden is above 7.5 percent.

Months 18 to 36 (April 2028 to October 2029):

  • FY2028 delivery of the 8 percent group margin (consensus EPS SEK 3.42; the record's path 7.9 percent); the Swedish segment at 7.6 percent per the broker; Inmatiq's contribution if it scales (50 to 100 basis points by 2028 is the outlier call).
  • 2029 at the earliest, Fabri step 3 (a further 17 percent, conditional on earnings hurdles not disclosed); the option formulas for 2030 to 2033.
  • The buildings directive's 2030 deadlines coming into tender pipelines from 2028; Norwegian hospital capital plans 2027 to 2030; the Swedish defence build-out to 3.1 percent of GDP by 2028.
  • Normalisation of Nordic installation volumes from about 15 percent below normal, if the broker's estimate is right, which is the long tail of the utilisation story.

How novel and how founded, together: the next two reports are the best-founded and least novel steps; the acquisition restart is moderately founded and moderately novel; Germany is the only new chapter and its economics (option prices, bolt-on multiples, the mix of the merged segment) are the least disclosed. Against the staircase stands a price that already discounts the first flight: the dashboard's valuation reading is mid-range (46th percentile of ten years) and the memo's modal return from here is 9.3 percent a year, so the narrative's positive steps have to be delivered merely to hold the price, and the market-moving novelty for the next twelve months is cash and the engine, not margin.

The Investment Analyst's closing notes for this layer

Closing note for the Consumer

Three things to carry from this set into the operating and financial model, because they decide most of the variance.

  1. The margin recovery is the case and it is the company's own history re-run: build Sweden (71 percent of sales) from 7.0 percent toward the broker's 7.2 and 7.6 percent in 2027 and 2028 on utilisation, bad-debt normalisation and legacy roll-off, with no real price and with the spring 2027 wage round as a step in cost; let Finland normalise to 8 to 10 percent; blend Fabri at 12.7 percent with 49 percent to minorities.
  2. The chain below EBITA is where consensus is most generous: amortisation rolling off helps the reported line, but net financial items (SEK 234m to 133m in consensus) assume deleveraging into a rate path the Riksbank is reversing, the tax rate was 28.1 percent in FY2025 against 25 percent assumed, and minorities rise with Germany; hold these flat-to-worse and the FY2028 EPS is 8 to 10 percent below the SEK 3.42 median.
  3. Cash is the gate: conversion at or above 100 percent at Q4 2026 and company leverage below 2.5x restart the acquisition engine (SEK 800m a year, SEK 56m of EBITA a year, and the engine-on multiple); conversion below 90 percent keeps it off and opens the roll-up's failure path. Tomorrow's print is the first of the two that decide it.
Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 2

Research requests for the next set

Company: Instalco AB (INSTAL-SE, Nasdaq Stockholm): a Nordic technical installation group of about 150 decentralised subsidiaries in electrical, heating and plumbing, ventilation, industrial installation and technical consulting, reporting country segments for Sweden (about 71 percent of FY2025 net sales of SEK 13,598 million), Norway and, from Q3 2026, Finland and Germany (the German platform Fabri AG consolidated at 51 percent from 1 August 2026), with sales split between service (about 35 percent), renovation (about 33 percent) and new production (about 30 percent), run since 1 August 2025 by returning founder Per Sjöstrand under the "Instalco 2.0" margin-over-volume programme toward the company's long-term targets of at least 8 percent EBITA margin, at least 10 percent annual growth over a cycle, net debt below 2.5x EBITDA and 100 percent cash conversion.

Request: T-P1

Peers: Bravida Holding AB (BRAV-SE; the closest listed Nordic installer, with a disclosed cost base of about 90 percent variable, a 4 percent headcount cut, and a Swedish three-into-one reorganisation targeting SEK 65 million of annual savings on SEK 90 to 100 million of charges, company, February 2026); Assemblin Caverion Group (private, Triton-owned; the Nordic number one at about SEK 41 billion of revenue with a merger synergy programme and a reported 7.9 percent adjusted EBITA margin in 2025); SPIE SA (SPIE-FR; the European multi-technical leader whose margin rose from 6.7 percent in 2023 to 7.6 percent in 2025 through selectivity, mix and acquisitions rather than volume); Nimlas Group (private, Swedish private-equity-backed consolidator of over 140 companies, a direct competitor in Swedish mid-sized tenders); Fasadgruppen Group AB (FG-SE; a decentralised Nordic contractor that showed negative operating leverage in the 2026 winter, a 0.5 percent adjusted EBITA margin in Q1 2026 on organic sales down 11.7 percent); Burkhalter Holding AG (BRKN-CH; a decentralised Swiss installer with 83 autonomous units and a centralised back office, EBIT margin 6.1 percent in 2025); InstallatørGruppen (Copenhagen-listed since June 2026; a Danish roll-up of 48 acquisitions since 2023 competing for the same Nordic targets); Caverion and Eltel as precedents of large-project write-downs (EUR 59 million and EUR 55 million). Inputs: What is Instalco's cost base split between materials and purchased services (46.9 percent of net sales in FY2025 per the annual report), own employee costs (36.8 percent) and subcontractors (about 16 percent of costs), and how much of each is fixed within a year under Swedish labour law; how have revenue per employee and backlog per employee moved from 2019 to Q2 2026 and what did they do to margin (the group earned above 8 percent EBITA every year from 2017 to 2021 and 5.9 percent reported in 2025); what incremental EBITA margin did Instalco earn on returning volume in Q4 2025 and Q2 2026 (adjusted EBITA SEK 276 million on net sales SEK 3,874 million in Q2 2026, against SEK 238 million on SEK 3,512 million a year earlier, company) and in its 2017 to 2018 and 2020 to 2021 up-cycles, and how long elevated incremental margins lasted; what did the late-2024 programme (eight subsidiaries closed or merged, 110 employees and 100 subcontractors terminated, SEK 65 million of non-recurring costs, a stated SEK 30 million EBITA drag removed) actually deliver and by when; how much of the margin bridge from the 2025 level toward the 8 percent target is bad-debt normalisation (receivable impairments of SEK 64 million in Q1 2025 on Northvolt and SEK 75 million of items affecting comparability in FY2025) versus utilisation versus the roll-off of low-margin Swedish contracts signed in 2024 and 2025 that management said in April 2026 were still working through the backlog; why did Finland's segment margin move from 2.0 percent in Q1 2025 to 12.1 percent in Q1 2026 and 8.1 percent in Q2 2026, and what is sustainable; how did Instalco's Swedish segment margin (7.0 percent in Q2 2026; DNB Carnegie's published forecasts of 7.2 percent in 2027 and 7.6 percent in 2028, 29 September 2026) compare with Bravida Sweden and with the privately owned Swedish operations of Nimlas and Assemblin Caverion in 2025 and 2026, and why; what will the spring 2027 Swedish collective agreements for electrical and for plumbing and ventilation installers (agreed minimum wages up about 8 percent per SEB, August 2026; actual pay rises about 3 percent per Nordea and Swedbank) do to fixed-price contracts signed in 2026 without automatic indexation, and how did the 2023 and 2025 rounds affect Instalco's and Bravida's margins; what back-office or procurement consolidation is feasible across 150 subsidiaries without breaking the decentralised model, and what did Burkhalter's Abacus migration and Bravida's Swedish merger deliver; what cost did the country-based organisation (three country managers and chief operating officers from September 2025) add; what is the fixed-cost share and margin behaviour of Fabri AG (EUR 86 million of 2025 sales at a 12.7 percent EBITA margin, company, July 2026) and of German installers generally through a volume cycle. Focus: Whether Instalco's Swedish segment can move from 7.0 percent toward the 8 percent group target by 2028 on utilisation, bad-debt normalisation and legacy-contract roll-off alone, without price and through the 2027 wage round, and what precedents say about how long such a recovery holds once volumes normalise; and which cost lines, if any, beyond those already cut in 2024 can be reduced in a federation of 150 autonomous subsidiaries without the entrepreneur departures the model depends on avoiding.

Request: T-P2

Peers: Bravida Holding AB (BRAV-SE; the same Swedish tender markets, a stated margin-over-volume policy that management admitted compromising in the downturn, and about 48 percent service revenue); Assemblin Caverion Group (private; the largest Nordic competitor in the same tenders); SPIE SA (SPIE-FR; indexation clauses and daily pricing tools across multi-year framework contracts, the benchmark for pass-through); Burkhalter Holding AG (BRKN-CH; dynamic pass-through of metal costs in a labour-scarce Swiss market); Fasadgruppen Group AB (FG-SE; a fixed-price renovation contractor exposed to material inflation and to newbuild contractors crowding into renovation); Nimlas Group (private; competing for the same mid-sized Swedish work); InstallatørGruppen (Copenhagen-listed; Danish contracts under the AB18 framework with automatic indexation above 12 months, a contrast to Swedish contracts). Operating conditions: A Swedish construction recovery that began in 2026 (building starts up 8.0 percent year on year in July 2026 per DNB Carnegie) coinciding with a Riksbank hiking cycle (a November 2026 hike expected by Nordea and Barclays; Deutsche Bank projects 1.75 percent to 2.50 percent by mid-2027) and Norges Bank at 4.50 percent after September 2026; the spring 2027 Swedish collective wage round on contracts priced in 2026 without automatic indexation; Swedish installation tenders attracting about fifteen bidders against a historical five (expert call, ARE Sverige, February 2025) and public buyers selecting on lowest price; price pressure concentrated in southern Sweden and in private residential and commercial work; copper and aluminium price spikes (Deutsche Bank forecasts of USD 12,800 and USD 3,400 a tonne, spring 2026) inside the 43 percent of Instalco's sales on fixed-price contracts; Nordic wholesaler concentration (Ahlsell about 30 percent of Swedish installation distribution) with distributors currently absorbing cost (DNB Carnegie on Solar, August 2026); competitors' capacity absorbed by large data-centre contracts (Bravida's order intake up 44 percent in Q2 2026); the German market entered through Fabri AG; renewal of service and maintenance agreements (about 35 percent of Instalco's sales) and multi-year partnering and framework contracts (Skanska 2026 to 2032, VAROPreem 2026 to 2029). Inputs: What share of Instalco's revenue is fixed-price, cost-plus, partnering and index-linked, by country and by segment (the company reports 57 percent cost-plus or variable and 43 percent fixed-price for FY2025, from 46 percent fixed-price in 2021), and how do Sweden, Norway, Finland and Germany differ on indexation; how long does cost pass-through take in practice on Swedish installation contracts and what happened to Instalco's margins through the 2021 to 2023 inflation (adjusted EBITA margin 8.2 percent in 2021 to 7.6 percent in 2023 and 5.9 percent reported in 2025, company); is there evidence of Nordic installers achieving and holding price increases since 2022 and of price given back in 2024 and 2025; can Instalco or anyone separate price from volume in its 8.4 percent organic growth in Q2 2026 (management has said it cannot extract volume metrics from about 6,000 projects); what has the ABC customer-prioritisation programme and the "value-added pricing" training launched under Instalco 2.0 in late 2025 measurably done to tender margins and win rates; how many bidders now compete on Swedish mid-sized installation tenders in metropolitan and southern Sweden, and are small firms still bidding at zero margin as the sector's distress figures (8.5 percent of Nordic companies in financial distress, Alvarez & Marsal 2026) suggest; are newbuild contractors still pivoting into renovation and public work, and when does that reverse; how sticky are Instalco's service agreements and what churn do customers report; what do the large Nordic contractors (NCC, Skanska, Peab) and public buyers say about installers' ability to pass on the 2027 wage round and metal costs; what price increases have equipment makers and wholesalers announced for 2026 and 2027 (Systemair, Lindab, Ahlsell, Rexel, Onninen); what contract structures and indexation does Fabri AG use in Germany and how did German installers' margins behave through the 2022 to 2024 inflation and the German heat-pump subsidy swings. Focus: Whether Instalco can hold its Swedish tender margins through the 2027 wage round and a metals spike on un-indexed fixed-price work, and whether the pricing relief it reported in 2026 is its own (selectivity, service mix, partnering) or borrowed from competitors' capacity being absorbed elsewhere and therefore reversible; and whether any part of its 2026 organic growth is price rather than volume.

Request: T-P3

Why requested: The case turns on a single chain of dependent dynamics, cash conversion setting leverage, leverage gating the acquisition engine, the engine setting growth and the multiple, and backlog growth consuming the working capital that feeds the chain, so the model needs to know whether these loops are reinforcing or balancing before it can set 2027 and 2028. Peers: Bravida Holding AB (BRAV-SE; company-basis cash conversion that moved from 112 percent in mid-2024 to 77 percent in 2026 as customer advances on large contracts were consumed, and a resumed buyback and bolt-on programme at 1.3x leverage); SPIE SA (SPIE-FR; a self-funded serial acquirer on structurally negative working capital of 34 days, the model where the cash loop reinforces growth); Fasadgruppen Group AB (FG-SE; the precedent where the loop ran in reverse, acquired growth with leverage to 5.3x, organic growth of about 1 percent, a SEK 504 million rights issue in 2026); Assemblin Caverion Group (private; deleveraging from 4.0x to 3.3x post-merger); Nimlas Group and InstallatørGruppen (private-equity-backed and newly listed consolidators competing for the same Nordic targets and operating under different leverage thresholds); Lifco and Addtech (Nordic serial acquirers that fund acquisitions without equity issuance, as the contrast the sell-side draws with Instalco); Green Landscaping Group (the platform Instalco's founder chaired from 2015 to April 2026, as a precedent for his acquisition cadence). Inputs: A reinforcing loop in capital: does cash conversion at or above 100 percent bring company net debt to EBITDA below the 2.5x target (2.6x at Q2 2026, company) and does that restart bolt-on acquisitions at the historical pace of about SEK 800 million a year (DNB Carnegie's figure; SEK 56 million of acquisition consideration in H1 2026 per the interim report), which add acquired growth and lift the listed multiple toward the level Nordic serial acquirers trade at when their engines run, which in turn lowers the cost of equity-funded deals; a balancing loop in working capital: does backlog growth (order backlog up 17 percent to SEK 10,937 million at Q2 2026) on 43 percent fixed-price work consume working capital (contract assets SEK 808 million at Q2 2026 from SEK 551 million at Q4 2025; accounts receivable SEK 2,009 million) and depress cash conversion (90 percent rolling at Q2 2026 from 108 percent at Q4 2025) exactly when the recovery is strongest, and how did the same loop run at Bravida and SPIE; a balancing loop in pricing: does a recovering market fill the fifteen-bidder tail's idle capacity and end price-dumping, and would a stalled recovery into Riksbank hikes restart it; a balancing loop in people: do the three-year lock-ups on acquired founders expiring, employee turnover rising to 15.8 percent in 2025 from 13.1 percent in 2023 (company), and the Instalco 2.0 baseline's tighter controls produce subsidiary managing-director departures that erode the local relationships behind the 80 percent repeat-customer share; a reinforcing loop in Germany: does Fabri's platform (23 subsidiaries, founders locked in through options to 2033) attract German founder-sellers and bolt-ons at Nordic-style multiples, or do private-equity bidders (Brookfield's 15 German technical installers since 2023) inflate them; a reflexive loop in the share price: how does a share up about 63 percent in twelve months change the cost and the acceptability of equity-funded acquisitions (the SEK 138 million targeted issue for the first Fabri step in 2025) and the competition for targets; a loop in customer credit: how would the financing of Nordic data-centre developers and of construction companies (48 percent of Instalco's sales) respond to tighter credit, and how did the Northvolt receivable write-down of SEK 64 million in Q1 2025 arise and propagate. Focus: Whether the cash-to-leverage-to-acquisition loop closes positively within 2027 for Instalco (the sell-side's base case) or whether backlog-driven working capital consumption and lock-up departures keep it open, and what precedents say about how long a Nordic serial acquirer takes to restart its engine after a leverage breach and what happens to its multiple while it waits.

Request: T-P4

Why requested: The gap between Instalco's adjusted EBITA and its reported earnings per share is large and made of several lines that consensus treats generously, so the earnings path must be built from each input rather than taken from a three-broker median whose definitions differ. Peers: Bravida Holding AB (BRAV-SE; a Nordic installer with disclosed one-offs, a 100 percent floating SEK debt book and a stated dividend policy, the closest bridge comparison); SPIE SA (SPIE-FR; disclosed drop-through, normative tax rate, share-count and convertible effects, and a 40 percent payout policy); Burkhalter Holding AG (BRKN-CH; a near-90-percent-payout installer with goodwill written off against equity, for the accounting contrast); Fasadgruppen Group AB (FG-SE; the Nordic precedent for earn-out liabilities, call and put options on subsidiary shares and a rights issue inside the bridge); Assemblin Caverion Group (private; bond reporting on interest cost and leverage for a Nordic installer at 3.3x); Green Landscaping Group and Eltel (platforms chaired by Instalco's founder, for his handling of minorities, earn-outs and below-the-line items). Inputs: The treatment by each covering broker (DNB Carnegie, Pareto Securities, Nordea) of amortisation of acquired intangibles (SEK 132 million in FY2025, SEK 189 million in FY2024, company) and of items affecting comparability (SEK 75 million in FY2025; SEK 65 million of restructuring and SEK 29 million of goodwill impairment in Q4 2024; SEK 64 million Northvolt in Q1 2025) in their adjusted EPS, given the FY2026 range of SEK 1.79 to SEK 2.30 among them (FactSet, 21 October 2026); the split of FY2025 net financial items (net interest expense SEK 167 million per FactSet; SEK 63 million in Q2 2026 per the interim report) between bank debt, lease interest (SEK 26 million in FY2025) and other items, the fixed-or-floating mix of the SEK 3.4 billion facility extended in June 2026 to at least 2028, its leverage-based margin step-up grid, and the effect of the Riksbank and Norges Bank paths on 2027 and 2028 interest cost; why the reported tax rate rose to 28.1 percent in FY2025 from 25.1 percent in FY2024 and 20 to 22 percent in 2019 to 2023 (FactSet), and what rate the company and brokers expect; the size and path of minority interests after consolidating 51 percent of Fabri AG (2025 sales EUR 86 million, EUR 117 million pro forma, EBITA margin 12.7 percent, company), the EBITA hurdles for the 17 percent step in 2029 and the valuation formula for the 2030 to 2033 options, and any other put and call options and contingent consideration on subsidiary shares across the group by segment; the share count path (268.4 million at FY2025 per FactSet) including the 2023/2026, 2024/2027 and 2026/2029 warrant programmes (2,350,000, 2,350,000 and up to 1,950,000 warrants at SEK 64.90, SEK 44.32 and a price to be set), the unused buyback mandate, and the company's record of share issuance for acquisitions; the dividend policy (about 30 percent payout; SEK 0.50 proposed for FY2025 against SEK 0.68 for FY2024) against FactSet consensus dividends of SEK 0.70, 0.90 and 0.90 for FY2026 to FY2028; the sensitivity of reported earnings to currency (the annual report's translation reserve sensitivities of SEK 35 million per 10 percent EUR move and SEK 48 million per 10 percent NOK move; the board's policy not to hedge) now that German euro earnings are consolidated; the phasing of Fabri's consolidation (from 1 August 2026 with a one-month reporting lag) and the cost and cash timing of its later steps; the normal run-rate of receivable impairments and project write-downs the company and brokers assume once the 2024 and 2025 items are excluded; lease liabilities (SEK 505 million) and their treatment in each broker's net debt and in the bank covenant definition, which DNB Carnegie says excludes contingent earn-outs. Focus: Pin down the FY2026 to FY2028 bridge from adjusted EBITA to reported and adjusted earnings per share, line by line (amortisation, items affecting comparability, net financial items under a rising-rate path, tax, minorities including Fabri's 49 percent, share count, currency and dividends), and establish the cash cost and timing of Fabri's option steps, so that the earnings path can be built from the company's own inputs rather than from a three-broker consensus whose near-year adjusted EPS estimates range from SEK 1.79 to SEK 2.30 (FactSet, 21 October 2026).

Layer 2

Translating the foundational components into an operating and financial model for the next 18 months, and 18 months to 3 years out

9 questions in 1 group · 13,153 words · written 22 October 2026, 23:06 to 23:27 UK, Fable, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years, based on the first set's answers, a base of the company's historical financial and operating performance, and how businesses in the sector and industry outperform or underperform, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a trustworthy judgement regarding the predictability of delivery of the financial forecasts over the next 18 months.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the likely effect on the company's financial forecasts is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes for this layer

The Investment Analyst's title
Instalco (INSTAL-SE): second set of questions, the operating and financial model for the next 18 months to four years

Written 22 October 2026 by the Investment Analyst, for the Analyst answering the third and fourth sets (the predictability of the forecasts, and how far our view is reflected in guidance, consensus and the narrative).

Read this first: the basis of every figure below.

  • Everything is pre-Q3: the Q3 2026 report lands tomorrow, 23 October 2026. Where the print would change a judgement, the threshold is named.
  • Money is SEK millions unless stated. The profit basis I build from is the company's adjusted EBITA (FY2025 SEK 875m, 6.4 percent; reported EBITA SEK 800m, 5.9 percent). The forecast-inputs form and consensus are on the brokers' basis: EBITDA before amortisation of acquired intangibles and before items affecting comparability (FY2025 SEK 1,229m, 9.0 percent), which equals adjusted EBITA plus about SEK 354m of depreciation (2.6 percent of sales). Brokers' EPS (FY2025 SEK 1.76; consensus FY2026 SEK 2.28, FY2027 SEK 3.01, FY2028 SEK 3.42, three brokers) is before amortisation of acquired intangibles, after tax and minorities. Reported basic EPS FY2025 was SEK 1.28. I write "the three brokers" where it matters.
  • My base case in one line: revenue SEK 15,053m, 16,860m, 17,995m, 19,195m, 20,496m for FY2026 to FY2030; adjusted EBITA margin 6.9, 7.4, 7.7, 7.9, 8.0 percent; adjusted EPS SEK 2.07, 2.45, 2.79, 3.21, 3.59; free cash flow after leases SEK 550m, 650m, 800m, 895m, 960m. Against the three brokers that is EPS 9, 19 and 18 percent below for FY2026 to FY2028. The full build, segment by segment and line by line, is in P-10 and in the forecast-inputs form.
  • Where I differ from the first set, I say so in the answer concerned. The five material differences are: the tax rate (I use 23 to 24 percent, not 26 to 28); the reading of the consensus depreciation line (amortisation of acquired intangibles rises with Fabri, it does not roll off); the year-end 2026 leverage gate (the reported print is likely to be 2.6 to 2.7x, not below 2.5x, because five months of Fabri EBITDA meet twelve months of its debt); the distance from consensus (my FY2027 EPS is 19 percent below, not 7, because four small shortfalls compound); and minorities (already SEK 36m in H1 2026 before Fabri, so SEK 125m to 135m a year from 2027, which consensus only partly carries).

Financial forecasts

C529 questions
P-0162P-0242P-0355P-0440P-0555P-0650P-0840P-0957P-1055

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The forecasts for the next 18 months to four years describe a company earning its way back to its own 2017 to 2021 margin at a slower pace than its founder implies and than its three brokers model, on a revenue line that grows more slowly than its history and more quickly than consensus assumes after 2027. The operating lines are the dependable part: utilisation on a pruned labour base, bad-debt normalisation, legacy-contract roll-off and Fabri's mix take the adjusted EBITA margin from 6.4 to 7.4 percent in two years and to 8.0 percent in four, with price contributing nothing to margin and about 3 points a year to nominal revenue, with the 2027 wage round as the one dated negative, and with operating leverage fading from 2028 as the backlog is staffed. The chain below EBITA is the undependable part and it is where consensus is most exposed: financial items flat rather than falling, tax at 23 to 24 percent, minorities at SEK 125m to 135m, amortisation rising with Fabri, warrants adding 1 to 2 million shares a year, no buybacks; together they hold adjusted EPS growth to about 1.1 to 1.2x EBITA growth and put FY2027 EPS at SEK 2.45 against the brokers' SEK 3.01, which is my bull case. The feedback loops close later and smaller than the sell-side's base: the cash-to-leverage link works during 2027, the acquisition restart is at 60 percent of the old pace from H2 2027, and working capital consumes 11 to 12 percent of every krona of growth, so free cash flow after leases rises from SEK 550m to SEK 960m but never funds the engine plus the dividend after 2028 and the balance sheet deleverages by ratio, not in money. The record says to expect beats on volume for two more quarters and to stop extrapolating them at Q1 2027, when comparatives harden and wages land. For the next Analyst: the operating forecasts are predictable within about a point of margin and 2 points of growth; the EPS forecasts are predictable only once the four below-EBITA items are modelled from the company's components rather than from consensus; and the dates that decide the case are tomorrow's Swedish margin and cash print, the February 2027 year-end cash release and leverage on both bases, the spring 2027 wage settlements, and Fabri's first full quarter.

P-01

Fixed-cost operating leverage: how it shapes the main line items over the next 18 months to four years, against the last four to six years

C62

Question asked After assessing the fixed-cost operating leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, against the last four to six years?

Judgement: the operating leverage is real, worth an incremental adjusted EBITA margin of 10 to 14 percent on organic revenue for roughly four to six more quarters, and it is the single largest reason adjusted EBITA grows faster than revenue in FY2026 and FY2027 (EBITA +18 and +20 percent on revenue +11 and +12 percent in my base). It is a utilisation effect on a semi-fixed labour base rather than textbook fixed-cost absorption, so it fades to the group margin once capacity is rebuilt (from 2028), it reverses with a two-quarter lag if volumes fall, and it cannot by itself carry the margin from 6.4 to 8 percent. Against the last four to six years it runs the same mechanism in the opposite direction: FY2022 to FY2025 was negative operating leverage (revenue per employee down 11 percent from the Q3 2023 peak while personnel cost per employee rose 18 percent since 2019, margin 8.2 to 5.9 percent); FY2026 to FY2028 is the recovery of that same utilisation.

How the leverage actually works, which matters for the shape of the forecast:

  • The cost base is not "92 percent variable" as the first set and the company's presentation imply. The accounting split (materials and purchased services 46.9 percent of sales, employees 36.8 percent, subcontractors about 16 percent of costs) overstates flexibility. The better description is Morgan Stanley's Bravida framework: about 42 percent of costs fully flexible (materials, subcontractors), about 47 percent semi-flexible (operative staff on three-to-six-month notice), the rest fixed (salaried project managers, local management, premises, systems). Materials flex with activity but give no margin on fixed-price work already in progress; subcontractors are the shock absorber; own technicians are the semi-fixed layer that produces the leverage. Instalco's average headcount was 6,042 in FY2025 against 6,139 in FY2024 and 6,298 at period-end Q2 2026, so the base is now being rebuilt, which is the start of the fade.
  • The evidence on incremental margins, cleaned: Q2 2026 group incremental EBITA margin 13.5 percent (SEK 49m on SEK 362m), Sweden 10.4 percent (SEK 28m on SEK 268m); Q4 2025 the headline 20.5 percent is an artefact of the SEK 65m restructuring charge in the Q4 2024 comparative (normalised, the margin was flat year on year); H1 2026's 25 percent is an artefact of the SEK 64m Northvolt write-down in Q1 2025. The first set's "10 to 14 percent" is the right figure and the only clean one; I carry 12 percent on Swedish organic revenue for H2 2026 and H1 2027, 10 percent for H2 2027, then the segment margin (about 7.5 percent) from 2028 as headcount catches up with backlog. Finland shows both ends of the range inside one year (2.0 percent in Q1 2025, 12.0 percent in Q1 2026, 8.1 percent in Q2 2026).
  • Why it fades: backlog per employee was SEK 1.74m at Q2 2026 (SEK 1.57m at Q4 2025, highest since Q1 2023), which is the leading indicator the lead broker uses, and it rises only while hiring lags backlog. The company has to hire to convert a backlog of SEK 10,937m (1.21x last-twelve-month installation sales); each hire restores the cost base. Historically elevated incremental margins lasted several quarters, never years, and in 2017 to 2018 and 2020 to 2021 they were blurred by acquisitions (30.7 percent acquired growth in 2017; 23.8 percent in 2021).
