Precedent for the ceiling: SPIE, which has "the absence of operating leverage" and expands margin by selectivity and mix, is where Instalco ends up once volumes normalise.
  • Why it can reverse: Fasadgruppen printed a 0.5 percent adjusted EBITA margin in Q1 2026 on organic sales -11.7 percent, then 7.6 percent in Q2, under a decentralised model very like Instalco's. The same under-absorption cost Instalco 2.3 points of margin between 2021 and 2025. A volume reversal into the Riksbank's hikes would show in margin with about a two-quarter lag.

How it shapes each line, FY2026 to FY2030 against FY2021 to FY2025:

  • Revenue: operating leverage does not drive the top line; it is driven by it. Organic growth in my base is about 9 percent in H2 2026 (trough comparatives: Q3 2025 -3.6 percent organic), 5 percent in FY2027, 3.5 percent in FY2028 and 3 percent thereafter. Reported growth adds Fabri (SEK 445m in FY2026, SEK 1,450m in FY2027) and a modest bolt-on restart from H2 2027.
  • Employee costs: from 36.8 percent of sales in FY2025 (35.9 percent in FY2024) to about 36.0 percent in FY2026 and 35.8 percent in FY2027 despite the spring 2027 wage round (P-04), then flat; the whole of the utilisation gain shows here, offset by wages.
  • Other external costs: from 8.5 to 9.0 percent of sales in late 2024 and 2025 (bad-debt provisions the main reason) back toward 7.5 to 8.0 percent by FY2027; about 40 basis points of this is bad-debt normalisation, which is a quality item rather than leverage (P-03).
  • Adjusted EBITA margin: 6.4 percent (FY2025) to 6.9, 7.4, 7.7, 7.9, 8.0 percent. Of the 130 basis points to FY2028, I attribute about 60 to utilisation (this answer), 40 to bad-debt normalisation, 30 to legacy low-margin Swedish contracts rolling off (signed 2024 to 2025, "diminishing over the next several quarters" per management in April 2026), 40 to Fabri's mix, less about 40 for the net cost of the 2027 wage round and other small items. Brokers' EBITDA margin follows at about 2.6 points above (9.5, 10.0, 10.3, 10.5, 10.6 percent against consensus 9.8, 10.3, 10.6 percent).
  • Adjusted EBITA in money: SEK 875m to 1,039m, 1,248m, 1,386m, 1,516m, 1,640m (+19, +20, +11, +9, +8 percent). The two years in which operating leverage dominates are FY2026 and FY2027; from FY2028 growth in EBITA is mostly volume and acquisitions at the group margin.
  • EPS: the gearing of EPS to organic revenue is about 1.3x in FY2026 to FY2027 (1 percent of organic revenue moves EPS about 1.3 percent, the research's own figure), rising to about 1.5x after financial leverage (P-02). Half of the FY2027 EPS cone (SEK 1.80 to 3.00 around my SEK 2.45) is operating leverage; the other half is the chain below EBITA.

Magnitude of the range it creates: a 2-point swing in Swedish organic growth is about SEK 150m of revenue at a 12 percent incremental margin, SEK 18m of EBITA, about 1.5 percent of FY2027 EPS; a 2-point swing in the incremental margin itself (10 versus 12 percent) on SEK 800m of organic revenue is SEK 16m. The larger variance is in whether utilisation gains survive the wage round and the hiring that the backlog requires, which is why I place the Swedish segment at 7.2 percent in 2027 (the lead broker's figure) rather than extrapolating Q2 2026's run-rate. The sector digest's rule is the frame: "volume returning to a pruned cost base drives earnings, not price."

Where I differ from the first set: in substance not at all; in detail I remove the "92 percent variable" description, discard the 20 and 25 percent incremental figures as comparative artefacts, and date the fade (from 2028) rather than leaving it open. The first set's instruction to treat organic growth above about 8 percent as needing acquired headcount stands and is the reason my organic rate falls to 5 percent in 2027.

P-02

Financial leverage: how it shapes earnings per share and the balance sheet over the next 18 months to four years

❌ D42

Question asked After assessing the financial leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share and the balance sheet over the next 18 months to four years?

Judgement: financial leverage amplifies the operating recovery by about 1.2x in FY2026 and FY2027 (adjusted EPS +18 and +19 percent on adjusted EBITA +19 and +20 percent is the base case once minorities are counted, so the amplification is used up by minorities and tax; without them EPS would grow about 1.4x EBITA) and it is the swing that makes consensus EPS reachable or not: the three brokers need net financial items to fall from about SEK 234m to SEK 133m by FY2028, I hold them flat at SEK 210m to 225m, and that alone is SEK 0.25 of FY2028 EPS. On the balance sheet, company-basis leverage goes from 2.6x at Q2 2026 to about 2.6 to 2.7x reported at year-end 2026 (2.4 to 2.5x pro forma for a full year of Fabri), below 2.5x during H1 2027, about 2.2x at end-2027 and about 1.9x by 2029 even with the acquisition engine restarted at SEK 500m a year; so the balance sheet supports the modest restart I model and leaves room for a faster one. The comfort is against covenants; the discomfort is against a regime that refuses leverage and a share that pays for it in the multiple.

The mechanics, with the numbers that drive the forecast:

  • The debt and its cost: interest-bearing liabilities SEK 3,565m at Q2 2026 including SEK 722m of leases, net debt SEK 3,561m (cash SEK 4m); on the company's definition (excluding leases) net debt about SEK 2,840m, 2.6x. The SEK 3.4bn facility (term loan and revolver, extended in June 2026 to 2028 with a further one-year option) carries a base rate plus a leverage-linked margin; the fixed-floating mix, the margin grid and the covenant definitions are undisclosed. External loan interest was SEK 126m in FY2025 (SEK 162m in FY2024) and SEK 29m in Q2 2026, implying about 4.1 percent on the drawn balance; lease interest SEK 26m in FY2025; "other" financial items (contingent-consideration discounting, fees) about SEK 40m a year; unrealised currency effects on intra-group loans swing between years (+SEK 33m in FY2025, -SEK 33m in H1 2026). Note the first set's SEK 167m "net interest" is FactSet's figure; the consolidated net financial expense was SEK 145m. I build from SEK 145m.
  • The path I model for net financial items: FY2026 SEK -225m (H1 -124 including the FX loss; H2 about -100 with Fabri's step-2 debt from August and the first Riksbank hike), FY2027 -220m, FY2028 -210m, FY2029 -210m, FY2030 -215m. The components in FY2027: about SEK 143m of external interest (average net debt about SEK 3.1bn at about 4.6 percent, which is today's 4.1 percent plus 50 basis points of Riksbank), SEK 30m of lease interest, SEK 40m of other items, no FX. Deleveraging reduces the base by about SEK 100m a year from 2027 and the margin ratchet should step down 25 basis points when leverage prints below 2.5x (about SEK 7m), but the rate path (Riksbank to 2.0 to 2.5 percent by mid-2027; Norges Bank at 4.50 percent with a tightening bias) and the bolt-on restart absorb it. Net: flat.
  • Why consensus's falling line is improbable: SEK 133m in FY2028 requires either net debt of about SEK 2.2bn at today's rates (that is, no acquisitions at all and 100 percent cash conversion for three years) or a 150 basis point fall in rates. Neither is the modal path. The first set's steer to hold the line flat is adopted and quantified: a flat SEK 210m instead of SEK 133m is SEK 77m pre-tax, about SEK 0.22 to 0.25 of FY2028 EPS, 7 percent.
  • Sensitivities the Consumer should carry: 100 basis points on about SEK 3.1bn of floating debt is SEK 31m pre-tax, SEK 0.09 of EPS, about 3.5 percent of FY2027 EPS; 0.5x of leverage at current rates is about SEK 25m, SEK 0.07; a 10 percent NOK and EUR translation shock is immaterial to interest but SEK 92m of equity reserves.

How it shapes EPS, FY2026 to FY2030 against FY2021 to FY2025:

  • In FY2021 net financial items absorbed about 4 percent of EBITA (SEK 29m on SEK 748m); in FY2025 about 17 percent (SEK 145m on SEK 875m), and the quarterly peak was about 20 percent. In my base they absorb 22 percent in FY2026 (Fabri debt, rates), 18 percent in FY2027, 15 percent in FY2028, 14 percent in FY2029 and 13 percent in FY2030. So the chain below EBITA eases slowly, by about 1 point of EBITA a year, not the 5 points a year consensus has.
  • Adjusted EPS in my base: SEK 2.07, 2.45, 2.79, 3.21, 3.59 (FY2025 SEK 1.76). The gearing of EPS to EBITA across the five years is 1.1 to 1.2x. If I used consensus's financial line, FY2028 EPS would be SEK 3.03; if I used my financial line but consensus's margin and tax, it would be SEK 3.15. Financial leverage is therefore the second-largest of four reasons I sit below consensus, behind the margin and ahead of tax and minorities (P-08).
  • The reverse case: a stalled recovery into the hikes (organic 0 percent, margin 6.5 percent, rates +100 basis points) gives FY2027 EPS about SEK 1.80; leverage turns a 13 percent EBITA shortfall into a 27 percent EPS shortfall. That asymmetry is why the score is below 50 despite a benign base case.

How it shapes the balance sheet:

  • Net debt (company basis, excluding leases): about SEK 2,840m at Q2 2026; about SEK 3,100m at end-2026 (H2 free cash flow of about SEK 400m after leases, less Fabri step 2 SEK 393m, earn-outs and minority buy-outs about SEK 100m, plus Fabri's own net debt consolidated, which is undisclosed and which I put at about SEK 150m); about SEK 3,040m at end-2027; about SEK 3,000m at end-2028; rising to about SEK 3,300m by end-2030 as the acquisition line (SEK 550m to 850m a year) exceeds free cash flow after dividends. Leverage on the company's definition (EBITDA before IFRS 16): about 2.7x reported at end-2026 but about 2.5x pro forma for twelve months of Fabri; 2.2x at end-2027; 2.0x end-2028; 1.9x end-2029; 1.8x end-2030. The first set expected the 2.5x gate to be passed at Q4 2026; I expect it to be ambiguous at Q4 2026 (passed pro forma, missed reported) and clear by Q2 2027. The Consumer should not treat a 2.6x print in February 2027 as a failure of the cash leg; the test is the pro forma figure and the cash conversion ratio.
  • Equity: SEK 3,487m attributable at Q2 2026, growing to about SEK 5.5bn by 2030 on retained earnings (no buybacks). Goodwill: SEK 5,210m at end-2025 (145 percent of equity) rises with Fabri's purchase price allocation and the bolt-ons to about SEK 6.5bn, still above 100 percent of equity through 2028. Gearing falls from 107 percent to about 60 percent by 2030. Minority interests on the balance sheet rise from SEK 235m to perhaps SEK 700m to 850m with Fabri (DNB Carnegie's SEK 600m to 650m plus existing). None of this is a buffer; the resilience is in the variable cost base and the facility's headroom (SEK 1,050m at Q2 2026), not in reserves.
  • The equity-raise tail: I put it at under 15 percent over 18 months. It needs two of three things at once: cash conversion below 90 percent at Q4 2026, a SEK 100m-plus write-down, and a Fabri cash call brought forward. The precedent that frames it is Fasadgruppen (SEK 504m rights issue from 5.3x in February 2026); the mitigant is the June 2026 extension to 2028 and covenants "met with a wide margin", with DNB Carnegie's stress test showing pro forma EBITDA would need to fall about 20 percent before leverage including earn-outs reached 3.35x.

What I add to the first set: the first set correctly identified the financing line as the least credible consensus line; I add that the balance sheet in my base case is stronger than the engine-off assumption implies (1.9x by 2029 with SEK 500m a year of bolt-ons), which means the constraint on the acquisition restart from 2028 is management's judgement and the price of targets, not debt capacity. That is upside to the revenue line the next Analyst can test.

P-03

Cost elimination and efficiency: how much reaches the main line items, and when

C55

Question asked After assessing the scope for cost elimination and efficiency in greater detail, how much is likely to reach the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, and when?

Judgement: little that is new, and what is new is small, slow and not in the first 18 months. The cost actions that will reach the lines are (1) bad-debt normalisation, about 40 basis points or SEK 55m to 65m, two-thirds already in the H1 2026 run-rate and the rest through FY2027; (2) the late-2024 closures, whose SEK 30m of avoided losses is fully in the base by Q4 2025 and adds nothing further; (3) the roll-off of low-margin Swedish contracts signed in 2024 and 2025, about 30 basis points, landing between Q4 2026 and Q2 2027. Beyond these, the only structural pool is back-office, finance-process, procurement-compliance and ancillary purchasing across 150 subsidiaries, worth perhaps 20 to 30 basis points over three to four years (2028 to 2030), against which the country organisation added an undisclosed cost (I carry about SEK 20m to 30m a year) and the Instalco 2.0 baseline adds control cost. Net of the two, I carry 15 basis points of structural efficiency in FY2029 to FY2030 and nothing before. Large cuts are neither planned nor feasible without breaking the model.

The items, sized and dated:

  • Bad debt (the largest "cost" item in the recovery): other external expenses rose from 7.5 to 8.0 percent of sales in 2022 to 2024 to 8.5 to 9.0 percent in late 2024 and 2025, mainly on provisions; the doubtful-debt provision rose from SEK 24m (end-2023) to SEK 72m (end-2024) before the SEK 64m Northvolt write-down in Q1 2025. DNB Carnegie's 40 basis points (about SEK 54m on FY2025 sales) is the normalisation. Timing: H1 2026 carried no new impairment and management says overdue receivables did not rise with the Q2 build, so roughly 25 to 30 basis points are in the H1 2026 run-rate already; the remainder arrives as the provision runs at a normal level through FY2027. I carry a recurring SEK 50m of "non-recurring" items in the reported case (restructuring, bad debt, executive changes recurred in every year since 2023: SEK 65m plus 29m in Q4 2024, SEK 64m in Q1 2025, SEK 11m in Q2 2025), which keeps the adjusted and reported bases SEK 0.15 of EPS apart on this item alone. A second Northvolt-type event (construction companies 48 percent of sales; Swedish construction bankruptcies at the prior year's high per Elis, July 2026) is the risk to this item: SEK 50m to 100m, 15 to 40 percent of a quarter's EBITA.
  • The 2024 programme: eight subsidiaries (SEK 250m of revenue, SEK -30m of EBITA in January to November 2024) closed or merged, 110 employees and 100 subcontractors removed, SEK 65m of charges, with the last closure completing around November 2025. The benefit is avoided losses rather than savings, phased through 2025 as backlog completed, and it is in the Q4 2025 and FY2026 base. The public record does not show the full SEK 30m in FY2025's 6.4 percent, which tells me either that the benefit was partly offset by under-utilisation elsewhere or that it arrives in FY2026; either way, nothing incremental after FY2026. Earlier capacity actions (about 400 to 500 subcontractors and 200 employees removed by mid-2024, per DNB Carnegie) are likewise in the base.
  • Legacy contract roll-off: management said in April 2026 that low-margin Swedish contracts signed in 2024 and 2025 were "still working their way through the backlog" and "diminishing over the next several quarters". With typical mid-sized projects of 12 to 18 months, the last of them complete around mid-2027. I place about 30 basis points of Swedish segment margin on this, two-thirds in FY2027. The leading indicator is the Swedish margin in the seasonally weak Q1 2027 (Q1 2026 was 5.0 percent).
  • Structural efficiency (the only new pool): each subsidiary (about 30 people, 20 to 25 installers, three to six project leaders, one or two finance and administration staff) keeps its own bookkeeping and reporting on a group-wide system; central agreements cover about 30 percent of purchases (Burkhalter's frameworks cover about 80 percent); fleet, insurance, telecom and software are bought locally. InstallatørGruppen reports DKK 16m of wholesaler savings, DKK 8m-plus on insurance, leasing, telecom and fuel, and DKK 94m of cross-sales on a much younger platform; DNB Carnegie estimates its subsidiaries get about 5 percent lower procurement prices and 3 to 8 percent of purchase price back through payment terms. Scaled to Instalco (about SEK 5.4bn of materials and services), 1 to 2 percent better procurement compliance on the locally bought 70 percent is SEK 40m to 75m, 25 to 50 basis points, but only a fraction is realistically captured in a model that lets the local project manager choose the supplier (Burkhalter's experience). Finance standardisation (chart of accounts, payables, payroll, month-end, credit monitoring) is the other credible pool, but its value comes through fewer errors and better billing rather than headcount. I carry 15 basis points in FY2029 to FY2030; the Consumer may treat up to 30 as upside with low confidence.
  • What cannot be cut: local CEOs, estimators, project leaders and customer-facing technicians (the entrepreneur-retention and tender-capacity risk the first set named; InstallatørGruppen explicitly refuses to merge local companies for this reason); project controls (the 2024 closures required write-downs on backlog because announcing a shutdown reduced execution efficiency); safety, certification and commissioning. Bravida's three-into-one Swedish merger (SEK 65m of annual savings against SEK 90m to 110m of charges, phased over 2026 and beyond, payback 1.4 to 1.7 years) shows that even the centralised peer needs multi-year phasing and that the saving reduces EPS in the implementation year. Instalco has no such programme and should not be modelled with one.
  • Cost the programme adds: three country managers, COOs, controllers and the Go-Great division from September 2025; the Extended Management Team was dissolved on 1 January 2026, which partly offsets. Undisclosed; I net SEK 20m to 30m against the efficiency pool. The financial test of the country layer is fewer write-downs rather than lower cost, which is why I treat it as part of the bad-debt and legacy-contract items rather than as overhead.

When it reaches the lines, in sequence: FY2026 (bad debt, two-thirds; the 2024 programme's tail), FY2027 (bad debt completed; legacy roll-off; the wage round takes about 40 basis points back), FY2028 (utilisation fade; Fabri mix; nothing from cost), FY2029 to FY2030 (procurement and finance standardisation, 15 basis points). Within the 18-month window the Consumer should expect essentially no cost-programme news, no restructuring charge in the base (a small one, SEK 20m to 40m, if Norway or a weak Swedish cluster is cut, is the plausible variant), and the margin story to be told entirely in utilisation, bad debt and legacy roll-off.

Where I differ from the first set: C-77 said little remains to cut, which I confirm; C-78 placed the scope at 1.5 to 2 points of group margin or SEK 220m to 300m of EBITA, which I confirm in size (my FY2028 EBITA is SEK 1,386m against SEK 875m, of which about SEK 200m is margin and the rest volume and Fabri) but I attribute less of it to cost (about 55 basis points: bad debt and legacy roll-off) and more to utilisation and mix than C-78's wording implied.

P-04

Pricing power: how price contributes to revenue and margin over the next 18 months to four years

❌ D40

Question asked After assessing the pricing power in greater detail, how is price likely to contribute to revenue and margin over the next 18 months to four years?

Judgement: price contributes nothing to margin and about 2.5 to 3 points a year to nominal revenue growth, which is cost inflation passed through with a lag on the 57 percent of the book that is cost-plus, partnering or service, and absorbed for several quarters on the 43 percent that is fixed-price. The 2026 pricing relief is about one-third the company's own (selective bidding, the ABC prioritisation, a higher service share) and two-thirds borrowed from competitors' capacity being absorbed by data-centre work, so it is reversible. The spring 2027 wage round is the one dated event where price is tested: a 3 percent actual pay rise on employee costs of 36.8 percent of sales is 1.1 percent of sales mechanically; I assume two-thirds is recovered (service repricing, cost-plus pass-through, selectivity, utilisation) and one-third is absorbed on un-indexed fixed-price work, a net 40 basis points off the FY2027 margin, concentrated in Q2 to Q4 2027. Real price, meaning margin earned from price rather than from volume, mix or execution, is zero in the base and should be written into the model only when the Swedish segment prints above 7.5 percent in a quarter in which organic growth is below 5 percent.

The evidence that fixes the sign:

  • The last cycle is the application record and it is unkind: adjusted EBITA margin 8.2 percent in 2021, 7.2 percent for the first nine months of 2022 (management attributed the fall "primarily to higher material prices" while organic growth was +7.8 percent), 7.6 percent in 2023, 6.9 percent adjusted in 2024, 6.4 percent in 2025. In the Q3 2021 call management said cost-plus arrangements let it "push much of the price increase to customers over time" while older fixed-price projects remained problematic "for several quarters". That is the whole pricing-power story: pass-through works, with a lag set by the order book, and the lag costs margin every time inflation accelerates. Bravida said the same in Q2 2023 (material prices not fully passed on, margin 5.6 percent on +14 percent sales), and Bravida estimates the lag from better market pricing to realised prices at two to six months, possibly seven.
  • The mix protects more than the headline 43 percent fixed-price share suggests but is not index-linked. Larger projects are generally partnering with transparent cost and a mark-up; service (35 to 37 percent of sales, up from about 30 percent) is billed on hours, materials and price lists and is not contractually bound, so it reprices at each job; variable components are "generally absent except for fines". The share of revenue with automatic wage or materials indexation is undisclosed; Swedish contracts lack the AB18 indexation Danish contracts carry above twelve months. So the un-protected pool is the fixed-price work signed in 2026 that runs through 2027 (Östersund to autumn 2027, Stockholm renovation to 2029, the Norwegian nursing home to 2028 and the NOK 440m contract to 2031 are the named long-duration examples).
  • The tender market is still a buyer's market in Instalco's band: public buyers award on "lowest price linked to quality"; the fifteen-bidder figure is a southern Swedish and public-tender symptom rather than a national average (the research could not verify it nationally and developers say two or three installers are invited for private work); pricing sentiment in specialised construction only turned positive in October 2025 (DNB Carnegie) after declining from May 2022; Nimlas, the closest private comparator, says volumes are returning "before prices". The upside case from procurement reform (the European Commission's October 2026 proposal for a 30 to 50 percent quality weighting) is beyond the horizon and subject to derogation.
  • The relief is partly borrowed: Bravida's data-centre backlog (SEK 4.3bn Green Mountain, SEK 2.2bn atNorth, SEK 650m EcoDataCenter, potentially SEK 8bn to 9bn in total) ties specialist capacity to 2028 and 2029; Instalco's own report says data-centre investment is "absorbing significant resources across the market and strengthening conditions in other installation projects". If those projects slip on the artificial-intelligence financing cycle (the weekly digest's crack) the capacity returns to ordinary tenders within two quarters. The company-specific part (declining low-priced work, the ABC categorisation, value-added pricing training launched in late 2025) is real but unmeasured: no win rates, tender margins or price-volume split are disclosed, and management says it cannot separate price from volume across 6,000 projects.

How price enters the model:

  • Revenue: of my 9 percent organic growth in H2 2026, about 3 points are price and inflation pass-through (Veidekke puts Swedish construction-cost inflation at 3.7 percent in September 2026, heading toward 4.6 percent before easing to 2.8 percent by late 2028; Norway 5.4 percent); of the 5 percent in FY2027, about 3 points; of the 3 to 3.5 percent in FY2028 to FY2030, about 2.5 points. In other words, real volume growth in my base is about 6 percent in H2 2026, 2 percent in FY2027 and 0.5 to 1 percent thereafter, which is consistent with Swedish volumes about 15 percent below normal recovering over three years and with hiring being the constraint. The Consumer should note that a revenue line that looks like "5 percent organic" is mostly inflation from 2027.
  • Margin: zero from price. The wage round takes about 40 basis points net in FY2027 (SEK 70m), which is the single largest negative item in the FY2027 bridge and the reason the Swedish segment goes to 7.2 percent rather than 7.8 percent. Materials: neutral in the base (wholesalers absorbing, heat-pump oversupply, copper volatile; Rexel passed 3.6 percent of price in Q2 2026 while Lindab could not fully recover its costs). A metals spike inside the fixed-price book would cost 20 to 50 basis points for two to three quarters; an 8 percent wage-floor outcome applied broadly (the stress case) would cost up to 100 basis points before offsets. I carry neither in the base.
  • Where real price is plausible and I carry 1 to 2 percent of it: Finnish industrial work at high utilisation, technical consulting (Intec), automation (Inmatiq, selected on "quality and expertise" by Region Blekinge), and renewals of service agreements. Together these are perhaps 15 to 20 percent of sales; 1.5 percent of price on them is about 25 basis points of group margin over three years, and it is inside my FY2029 to FY2030 margin of 7.9 to 8.0 percent rather than additional to it.

Peer calibration: SPIE is the upper bound (indexation in all contracts, daily pricing tools, "priced ahead of inflation", margin 6.7 to 7.6 percent in two years with about 70 percent maintenance) and Instalco has neither the tooling nor the mix; Burkhalter shows that a labour-scarce market with excess demand still "faces high price pressure" and earns little on materials; Fasadgruppen shows price given back through weaker scope economics on fixed-price renovation (margin 9.5 to 8.8 to 7.6 percent); InstallatørGruppen shows what indexation does above twelve months. Instalco sits with Bravida and Nimlas: margin defended by selectivity and mix, not by price.

What decides it, in order: the Swedish margin in Q2 to Q4 2027 against the wage settlement actually agreed (electrical and plumbing agreements in spring 2027; the 8 percent minimum-wage figure is SEB's, the 3 percent actual-pay figure Nordea's and Swedbank's); backlog per employee holding above SEK 1.7m while organic growth slows; any disclosure of award margins or the fixed-price share of backlog; and whether Bravida's and Assemblin Caverion's data-centre backlogs are executed on schedule.

Where I differ from the first set: C-17 and C-45 concluded "model zero real price", which I keep; I add the quantification (price about 3 points of nominal growth, zero of margin, wage round -40 basis points net) and the attribution of the 2026 relief (one-third own, two-thirds borrowed), which the first set left open.

P-05

Reinforcing or balancing feedback loops: how they shape the main line items over the next 18 months to four years

C55

Question asked After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, such that dynamics at play today create additional scope for further positive/negative dynamics in the near/mid-term (reinforcing feedback loops), or the opposite (balancing loops), how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

Judgement: one reinforcing loop and three balancing loops are at work, and the net of them makes the four-year revenue line higher than consensus and the four-year cash line lower. The reinforcing loop (utilisation to margin to cash to leverage to acquisitions to acquired growth and to the multiple) is half-closed: the first three links work in my base (margin 6.9 to 7.4 percent, leverage below 2.5x in H1 2027), the fourth restarts modestly rather than at the historical SEK 800m a year (SEK 300m of acquired annual sales in H2 2027, SEK 500m a year in 2028 and 2029, SEK 550m in 2030), and the fifth (the multiple) is not a financial-forecast item. The balancing loops are working capital (growth consumes about 11 to 12 percent of incremental revenue on the gross measure, so cash conversion runs 90 to 95 percent in FY2026 and only reaches about 100 percent in FY2027 to FY2028), pricing (the recovery fills the tail's idle capacity, which is reversible on a Riksbank-led stall) and people (lock-ups, 15.8 percent turnover, the baseline's tightening; no evidence yet, carried as risk). Germany is a second reinforcing loop with a 49 percent leak and a price set by private equity. Across the lines: reported revenue growth of 6.7 to 6.8 percent a year in FY2028 to FY2030 (about half organic, half acquired) against consensus's 4 percent and the company's 10 percent; adjusted EBITA margin gaining about 10 basis points a year from bolt-ons from 2028; free cash flow after leases rising from SEK 550m to SEK 960m but never covering acquisitions plus dividends from 2029, so net debt rises in money while leverage falls.

The capital loop, link by link, with what the evidence says about each:

  1. Margin to cash. Working: adjusted EBITA from SEK 875m to SEK 1,039m in FY2026. Not yet converted: H1 2026 operating cash flow SEK 338m against SEK 426m, rolling cash conversion 90 percent (108 percent at Q4 2025, 100 percent at Q1 2026), contract assets SEK 808m from SEK 551m, receivables SEK 2,009m; working capital swung SEK 208m between Q1 (release SEK 75m) and Q2 (use SEK 133m). I treat Q2 as mostly timing (management: older overdue receivables did not rise; most receivables fall due within one month) with a structural residue from long Norwegian public projects. My base: FY2026 operating cash flow about SEK 960m (H2 about SEK 620m, in line with H2 2025's SEK 584m despite higher EBITDA, because the Q4 2025 release was flattered by an unusually low percentage-of-completion balance per DNB Carnegie), FY2027 SEK 1,100m, FY2028 SEK 1,285m. Consensus has SEK 1,048m, 1,370m, 1,477m; the gap in FY2027 is consensus's assumption of zero working-capital build on 13 percent growth.
  2. Cash to leverage. Working with a lag: 3.3x at Q3 2025 to 2.6x at Q2 2026; end-2026 reported about 2.7x and pro forma about 2.5x (P-02); below 2.5x in H1 2027; 2.2x at end-2027. The feedback report's two-period test (conversion above 100 percent for two consecutive periods with contract assets stable) is the right test; on my numbers it is met in the Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 reports if the Q4 release is normal, and if it is not the restart slips to 2028.
  3. Leverage to acquisitions. The weakest link and the one I lean against the sell-side on. Management's sequence is margin, debt reduction, cash flow, then acquisitions, with no dated commitment; DNB Carnegie's SEK 800m is a five-year average, not a run-rate; H1 2026 showed one deal (TSM, 70 percent, SEK 190m of annual sales, partly paid in 310,545 treasury shares) and the research could not validate the SEK 56m H1 consideration figure the first set used (FactSet's line; the interim report records SEK 3m of acquisition costs). Precedents for restart timing after a leverage breach: Green Landscaping (the founder's other platform) is expected to hold bolt-ons until leverage is below 2.5x, with a full restart not before Q1 2027, after FCF-to-EBITA conversion of 28 percent over 2020 to 2025; Fasadgruppen needed a rights issue first; DSV reinstated a reduced buyback at 2.46x against a sub-2.0x target. The pattern is a restart at reduced scale one to two quarters after the gate is passed, which is why I model SEK 300m of acquired sales in H2 2027 and SEK 500m a year thereafter, about 60 percent of the historical pace, and treat SEK 800m as the upside branch.
  4. Acquisitions to growth and margin. At SEK 500m of acquired sales a year at a 7 percent margin and 4 to 6x EV/EBITA, each year adds about SEK 35m of EBITA, 2.5 to 3 points of growth and about 10 basis points of group margin (SPIE's rule of thumb for accretive bolt-ons). German bolt-ons through Fabri at 7 to 9x add less per krona. The acquisition line in money: FY2026 SEK -520m (H1 SEK 56m, Fabri step 2 about SEK 393m, earn-outs and minority buy-outs), FY2027 -410m, FY2028 -550m, FY2029 -785m (including Fabri step 3, about SEK 235m at the 0.9x sales architecture DNB Carnegie describes), FY2030 -850m (the first tranche of the 2030 to 2033 options plus bolt-ons). Contingent consideration carries a maximum undiscounted exposure of SEK 240m against a SEK 50m recognised liability (June 2026), and SEK 160m was paid for minority buy-outs in 2025 alone, so the "acquisitions" line is never below about SEK 150m even with no new deals.
  5. Growth to the multiple to cheaper equity. Not modelled; noted that the AGM mandate to issue up to 10 percent for acquisitions exists and that a 63 percent share-price rise makes share-funded deals (SEK 138m for Fabri step 1; treasury shares for TSM) more acceptable. I carry no share issuance for acquisitions in the base and flag it as the most likely source of a small, recurring dilution (0.5 to 1 percent a year) if the engine runs faster than cash.

The balancing loops and their size in the model:

  • Working capital: on the FactSet gross measure (receivables plus inventories less payables) the year-end balance was 13.1 percent of sales at end-2023, 13.8 percent at end-2024, 11.7 percent at end-2025 and 11.7 percent at Q2 2026; the company's own net measure was 2.8 percent (2022 peak), 1.6 percent (end-2025), 2.3 percent (Q2 2026). I hold the gross measure at 11.7 percent for end-2026 (which charges the model about SEK 170m, blending the operating build with Fabri's acquired balance), 11.4 percent end-2027, 11.3 percent end-2028, 11.2 percent thereafter: a slow improvement from the baseline's working-capital controls, not SPIE's negative 28 days. The loop is seasonal (Q2 and Q3 build, Q4 release) and the Consumer should judge it on the Q4 2026 and Q4 2027 prints, not on Q3 (tomorrow's print will show a build; receivables below SEK 1,800m or operating cash flow toward SEK 200m would be better than my base). Bravida's conversion (77 percent in H1 2026 as advances on data-centre contracts are consumed) is the sector's reminder that backlog growth and cash conversion move in opposite directions for a year.
  • Pricing: covered in P-04. In the model it is the reason organic growth falls to 5 percent in 2027 and 3 to 3.5 percent after, and the reason I give no margin to price. The reversal trigger is a stall in Swedish starts into the November 2026 and 2027 hikes; the lag to Instalco's revenue is two to four quarters, so a 2027 stall shows in H2 2027 to H1 2028 margins.
  • People: turnover 15.8 percent in 2025 (15.3 percent in 2024; the 13.1 percent for 2023 is a broker figure on a different base), eNPS flat at 31, no subsidiary-CEO departures disclosed, the 80 percent repeat-customer share not substantiated by the research, the three-year lock-up wave not evidenced. I carry nothing in the base and treat it as the main non-financial variance: one cluster of departures in a weak Swedish region would show as a SEK 30m to 50m margin and write-down event. Nimlas (eight managing-director successions in 2025) and InstallatørGruppen (no owner-manager departures, succession plans for 70 roles) show it can be institutionalised; Green Landscaping's SEK 79m of 2026 one-offs show what delayed discovery looks like.
  • Customer credit: a balancing loop that acts before default (slower starts, longer payment, higher contract assets) and then at default (Northvolt SEK 64m plus idle capacity). Not in the base beyond the SEK 50m recurring items; a second event is a 5 to 15 percent hit to a year's adjusted EBITA.

The German loop: Fabri (23 subsidiaries, about 800 employees, EUR 86m of 2025 sales, EUR 117m pro forma, 12.7 percent EBITA, EUR 69m backlog) is a platform that attracts founder-sellers because it preserves local identity, and that is exactly what Brookfield (15 German deals since 2023), VDK, Eiffage, Nordic Climate Group and potentially Nimlas and InstallatørGruppen also offer. The loop reinforces revenue (I grow Fabri 10 percent a year from bolt-ons) and leaks 49 percent of profit until 2029 (then 32 percent, then the 2030 to 2033 options). German capacity utilisation at 68 percent (August 2026) and construction PMI in contraction mean the 12.7 percent margin is a cycle-peak figure for a specialist platform; I carry 11.5 percent. Fabri is a mix and growth story for the model, not a pricing or cash story.

How it all shapes the lines over four years: revenue growth of 10.7, 12.0, 6.7, 6.7, 6.8 percent (consensus 11.7, 13.0, 4.0; the company's target 10); adjusted EBITA margin 6.9, 7.4, 7.7, 7.9, 8.0 percent with the last 20 basis points from bolt-ons; free cash flow after leases SEK 550m, 650m, 800m, 895m, 960m; acquisitions plus dividends of SEK 680m, 570m, 750m, 1,030m, 1,120m, which exceed free cash flow from 2029; net debt (company basis) therefore flat to rising in money from 2028 while leverage falls to 1.8 to 1.9x. The reinforcing loop closes in the model's numbers in 2028 and 2029, a year later than the sell-side's base case and two years later than the founder's urgency, and it closes at a slower pace than the one that built the company.

Where I differ from the first set: the first set said "model the growth engine as off until leverage prints below 2.5x", which I follow, but I date the print (H1 2027) and the restart (H2 2027) and size it (60 percent of the old pace), where the first set left it as a dated scenario; the first set also used the SEK 56m H1 acquisition figure and the 80 percent repeat-customer share, both of which the research could not validate, so I do not rely on either.

P-06

Track record of delivery: how much weight the forecasts should carry, and in which direction they should be leaned

C50

Question asked After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

Judgement: the record is cyclical and the next 18 months are the recovery phase of that cycle, so the forecasts for the operating lines should be leaned up for the next two reports and held thereafter, and the forecasts for the lines below EBITA should be leaned down throughout. Weight: the operating lines (revenue, adjusted EBITA) deserve about 60 to 65 percent confidence within plus or minus 5 percent of my base for FY2026 and FY2027; the lines below EBITA (financial items, tax, minorities, amortisation) deserve less than 50 percent confidence because the company has never disclosed them in a way that could be forecast and because three of the four are moving against the forecast; the cash line deserves about 50 percent confidence that conversion returns to 100 percent during 2027. Direction against the three brokers: lean FY2026 EPS down about 10 percent (basis differences and the chain below EBITA), FY2027 EPS down 15 to 20 percent, FY2028 EPS down 15 to 20 percent; lean revenue slightly below consensus in FY2026 and FY2027 (the 6 to 8 percent organic assumption for 2027 is demanding against hardening comparatives) and above consensus from FY2028 (consensus has no bolt-ons). Direction against the company's own targets: the 8 percent margin is reached in FY2030 in my base, a year after the first set's path and two years after consensus; the 10 percent growth target is reached only in the Fabri years (FY2026 to FY2027); 2.5x and 100 percent conversion are reached during 2027.

The record, read for what it predicts:

  • Over one year: Q3 2025 missed (DNB Carnegie cut adjusted earnings 11 percent on a weak summer), Q4 2025 beat (margin 7.2 percent), Q1 2026 beat at group level (organic 4.9 against 1.6 percent) with Sweden 14 percent below the lead broker's EBITA and Finland 130 percent above, Q2 2026 beat (organic 8.4 against 4.1 percent; EBITA +8 percent against consensus) with cash well below. Pattern: group beats on volume, segment and cash surprises both ways, which is the decentralised model's signature. Expect tomorrow's print to beat on organic growth (DNB Carnegie's 10 percent group, 12 percent Sweden, on the -3.6 percent comparative) and to be judged on the Swedish margin (7.5 percent is the market's threshold; I have 7.0 to 7.3 percent) and on cash (I expect a seasonal build; the market wants SEK 200m of operating cash flow).
  • Over three years: FY2023 at the top on the brokers' basis (EPS SEK 2.67), FY2024 a miss (EPS -32 percent, restructuring), FY2025 all three financial targets missed with cash conversion beaten. Consensus FY2026 EPS was cut 15 percent over twelve months (range SEK 2.07 to 3.24) and FY2025 by 10 percent; FY2027 was cut less and FY2028 is being raised. The cut cycle ended in Q1 2026.
  • Over five to ten years: above 8 percent every year from 2017 to 2021 with the engine running and volumes rising; never above 8 percent without both; never below 100 percent cash conversion for a full year except in the growth years with acquisitions (2021: 82 percent on FactSet's measure); write-downs in Norway (2018, 2021), southern Sweden (2024) and Northvolt (2025); leverage above target in 2024 and 2025. The company delivers in easy environments and did not in the hard one; the environment now is mixed (volumes up, rates up, wages up).
  • The founder's record elsewhere is the soft positive (Eltel eleven consecutive quarters of margin improvement; Green Landscaping's growth) and the soft negative (Green Landscaping's 2026 one-offs and 28 percent FCF conversion under the same chairman).

What the record says about each forecast line:

  • Revenue: credible. The company's organic series turns with the Swedish cycle and the backlog gives 12 to 18 months of visibility; the brokers' FY2026 (+11.7 percent) is about right (mine +10.7 percent, the difference mostly Fabri's four reported months rather than five and currency); FY2027 (+13.0 percent, implying 6 to 8 percent organic) is where the lean is down (mine +12.0 percent on 5 percent organic plus a small bolt-on); FY2028 (+4.0 percent) is where the lean is up (mine +6.7 percent with bolt-ons). Segment consensus (Sweden +5.5 percent a year to FY2028; Norway +8.5 percent; Finland and Germany +42 percent) is consistent with mine except Norway, where I have about 6 percent.
  • Adjusted EBITA margin: lean slightly down against consensus from FY2027 (7.4 against about 7.8 to 7.9 percent) because the group consensus margin and the lead broker's own Swedish path (7.2 percent in 2027, 7.6 percent in 2028) cannot both be right unless Norway, Finland and Germany average about 9.5 percent, which I do not believe (Norway 6 percent, Finland 8 percent, Fabri 11.5 percent in my base). This inconsistency inside the consensus is the most useful thing the Consumer can carry into the fourth set: the group number is being set by the sum of segment optimism rather than by one authored path.
  • Cash: lean down for FY2026 (consensus operating cash flow SEK 1,048m; mine SEK 960m) and FY2027 (SEK 1,370m against SEK 1,100m); the record of 100 percent-plus conversion is real but was achieved with flat volumes in 2024 and 2025; growth years consume working capital at this company.
  • Below EBITA: lean down on all four items (P-08). The record on tax is a 20 to 22 percent rate in 2019 to 2023, 25 and 28 percent in 2024 and 2025, 20 percent in H1 2026; on minorities a step up to SEK 36m in H1 2026 before Fabri; on amortisation SEK 189m in FY2024 and SEK 132m in FY2025 before Fabri's purchase price allocation; on financial items SEK 145m in FY2025 and SEK 124m in H1 2026 including FX.
  • Reported versus adjusted: the "non-recurring" items have recurred every year; the reported EPS will sit SEK 0.45 to 0.55 below the adjusted EPS throughout (amortisation about SEK 0.45 after tax, items about SEK 0.15), and the dividend policy (30 percent of net profit) is applied to the reported figure, which is why my dividend path (SEK 0.60 to 1.10) is below consensus (SEK 0.70, 0.90, 0.90) in FY2026 and above it later.

The weight and the lean, summarised for the Consumer: trust the direction (margin up, leverage down, volume up) at 70 percent; trust the pace at 50 percent and lean it a year slower than the founder and half a year slower than the brokers; trust the chain below EBITA least and model it from the company's disclosed components, not from consensus; expect the next two prints to beat on volume and to be scored on the Swedish margin and on cash, and do not extrapolate beats past Q1 2027, when the comparatives harden (Q1 2026 +4.9 percent organic, Q2 2026 +8.4 percent) and the wage round lands.

P-08

Below-operating-profit items and share buybacks or issuance: how they shape earnings per share, net income and free cash flow, versus the last four to six years

❌ D40

Question asked After assessing below-operating-profit cost items (e.g. tax, interest, one-offs, minority investments, etc.) and share buybacks or issuance in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share, net income and free cash flow over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

Judgement: the chain below adjusted EBITA takes a larger share of the operating recovery than the three brokers allow, and it is the main reason my adjusted EPS sits 18 to 19 percent below consensus in FY2027 and FY2028 while my EBITA sits only 7 percent below. Line by line: amortisation of acquired intangibles rises with Fabri and the bolt-ons (about SEK 150m in FY2026 to SEK 185m by FY2030) rather than rolling off, which is a reported-EPS item only; items affecting comparability recur at about SEK 50m a year in the reported case; net financial items stay flat at SEK 210m to 225m (P-02); the tax rate is 23 to 24 percent, not FY2025's 28 percent and not the lead broker's 20 percent; minorities step from SEK 32m in FY2025 to about SEK 70m in FY2026 and SEK 125m to 135m in FY2027 and FY2028 (existing subsidiary minorities already ran at SEK 36m in H1 2026, Fabri adds about SEK 55m a year), falling to about SEK 100m by 2030 as the Fabri steps are exercised; the share count rises about 1 to 2 million a year from warrants (6.55 million outstanding, two of three series at or in the money) with no buybacks and no share-funded acquisitions in the base; the dividend rebuilds from SEK 0.50 to SEK 0.60, 0.75, 0.90, 1.00, 1.10. Versus FY2021 to FY2025, the chain has become permanently heavier: interest from 4 to about 15 to 20 percent of EBITA, tax from 20 to 24 percent, minorities from negligible to 10 percent of net profit, amortisation from SEK 70m to SEK 150m-plus, and the gap between adjusted and reported EPS from about SEK 0.25 to about SEK 0.50.

Each item, with the figure I use and why:

  • Amortisation of acquired intangibles (excluded from adjusted EPS and from the form's depreciation line; deducted for reported EPS). SEK 189m in FY2024, SEK 132m in FY2025 (SEK 36m of lower planned amortisation from a lower share of identified depreciable assets; SEK 7m of impairment). Fabri's purchase price allocation (EUR 35.7m for 27 percent on top of EUR 15m for 24 percent implies about EUR 130m to 140m of equity value) will create customer-relationship and backlog intangibles of perhaps SEK 300m to 400m amortised over five to seven years, about SEK 50m to 70m a year from FY2027; Nordic bolt-ons add about SEK 10m a year each year they run. My path: SEK 150m, 175m, 180m, 185m, 185m. I disagree with the first set's reading of the consensus depreciation line (SEK 402m, 358m, 348m) as amortisation rolling off: the consensus EBITDA-to-EBIT gap is SEK 559m, 572m and 592m, rising, and the separate line is a different broker set. Reported EPS therefore carries about SEK 0.45 to 0.50 of amortisation after a partial tax shield; adjusted EPS does not.
  • Items affecting comparability. SEK 65m plus SEK 29m (Q4 2024), SEK 64m (Q1 2025), SEK 11m (Q2 2025), SEK 75m (FY2025), none in H1 2026. I carry SEK 50m a year in the reported case (bad debt above the normal provision, small restructurings, executive changes) and zero in the adjusted case; the adjusted-to-reported gap is about SEK 0.15 from this item. A full-year without any such item would be the first since 2023 and would add SEK 0.15 to reported EPS without touching the adjusted figure the brokers quote.
  • Net financial items: SEK -225m, -220m, -210m, -210m, -215m (P-02). Components in FY2027: external interest about SEK 143m, lease interest about SEK 30m, other (earn-out discounting, fees) about SEK 40m, no FX. The reported FY2025 figure was SEK -145m (not FactSet's -167m), so the step up to FY2026 is Fabri's debt, the Riksbank and a half-year of FX loss already booked.
  • Tax. FY2025's 28.1 percent was built from a 20.6 percent Swedish starting point plus SEK 31m of losses without a deferred-tax asset, SEK 8m of non-deductible items and SEK 8m of utilised carryforwards; H1 2026 reverted to about 20 percent (SEK 57m on SEK 288m) and most quarters in 2024 and 2025 were 20 to 21 percent. Fabri's German profit (about 10 percent of group pre-tax from 2027) is taxed at about 30 percent, adding a point; unrecognised losses in weak subsidiaries recur at some level (the stock of such losses rose to SEK 149m at end-2025), adding another point or two in the years when they arise. I use 23.0 percent for FY2026, 23.5 percent for FY2027 and 24.0 percent thereafter on the adjusted pre-tax profit. This is a disagreement with the first set (26 to 28 percent): the 2025 rate was exceptional in its reconciliation, not structural, and the H1 2026 print is the evidence. Pillar Two is assessed by the company as not applicable. Each point of tax rate is about SEK 0.04 of FY2027 EPS; the difference between my 23.5 percent and DNB Carnegie's 20 percent is SEK 0.13, about a quarter of my gap to that broker.
  • Minorities (adjusted basis, before amortisation). Reported minorities were SEK 32m in FY2025 and SEK 36m in H1 2026 alone (TSM at 70 percent, other partly owned subsidiaries; the balance rose from SEK 170m to SEK 235m). Fabri at 51 percent: EBITA about SEK 167m in FY2027 less its own interest and German tax leaves about SEK 110m, of which 49 percent is SEK 55m. My path: SEK 70m (FY2026, five months of Fabri), 125m (FY2027), 135m (FY2028), 115m (FY2029, Fabri to 68 percent), 100m (FY2030, the first option tranche). Reconstructing the three brokers' FY2027 adjusted net income (SEK 811m median) from their pre-tax profit (SEK 1,027m) plus amortisation (about SEK 150m) at a 20 to 23 percent tax rate implies they already deduct about SEK 95m to 130m of minorities, so this item is in line with consensus rather than a source of my gap; it is, however, a line the forecast-inputs form does not carry, so I have folded it into the net financial items input, grossed up for tax (SEK 91m, 163m, 178m, 151m, 132m), and documented it in Part 4 of the form.
  • Share count. 268.4 million at end-FY2025 (FactSet), 268.75 million at June 2026 after the TSM treasury-share transfer; no treasury shares remain. Warrants: 2024/2027 series at SEK 44.32 (at the money at SEK 42.52), 2025/2028 series at SEK 31.40 (in the money), 2026/2029 series at SEK 44.37 (1.95 million); the 2023/2026 series at SEK 64.90 lapses. I add 0.3 million in FY2026, 1.2 million in FY2027, 2.2 million in FY2028, 1.0 million in FY2029, nil in FY2030; the cash they bring (about SEK 50m to 70m a year in 2027 to 2029) is left out of the buyback line to avoid double counting. Buybacks: the 5 percent mandate has never been used and will not be while leverage is above 2x and the engine is restarting; none modelled. Share-funded acquisitions: the record (SEK 138m for Fabri step 1 at SEK 29.70; treasury shares for TSM) says they happen; none in the base, 0.5 to 1 percent a year of dilution in the faster-engine branch.
  • Dividends. Policy about 30 percent of net profit (reported). FY2025: SEK 0.50 (39 percent of reported, 28 percent of adjusted). My path, declared for each year: SEK 0.60, 0.75, 0.90, 1.00, 1.10, which is about 29 to 31 percent of adjusted EPS and 38 to 42 percent of reported; cash cost SEK 160m to 300m a year, paid the following spring. Consensus (SEK 0.70, 0.90, 0.90) is higher in FY2026 and FY2027 and lower in FY2028. A SEK 0.70 proposal in February 2027 would be a small positive signal on cash; SEK 0.50 again would say the Q4 release disappointed.
  • Currency. Unhedged; 71 percent of sales SEK before Fabri, about 65 percent after; translation sensitivities SEK 35m of reserves per 10 percent EUR move and SEK 57m per 10 percent NOK move (the annual report's current figures; the first set's SEK 48m was the older one). I take about 0.5 percent a year off reported growth for a stronger krona (Morgan Stanley's EUR/SEK 10.40 and NOK/SEK 0.98 targets); on EPS it is about SEK 0.02 to 0.03 a year, inside the noise.
  • Fabri's cash steps. Step 2: about EUR 35.7m (about SEK 393m) in Q3 2026 from the facility. Step 3: 17 percent, 2029 at the earliest, conditional on EBITA thresholds; at DNB Carnegie's 0.9x-sales architecture on about EUR 140m of 2028 sales, about SEK 235m. Step 4: options on 32 percent over 2030 to 2033, formula undisclosed; I spread about SEK 125m a year from 2030. These sit in the acquisitions line and compete with bolt-ons for cash.

What it does to the three outputs, FY2026 to FY2030 against FY2021 to FY2025:

  • Adjusted net income (brokers' basis, after minorities): SEK 557m, 661m, 759m, 878m, 983m, from SEK 494m in FY2025 and a peak of about SEK 700m in FY2023; adjusted EPS SEK 2.07, 2.45, 2.79, 3.21, 3.59 against SEK 1.76 and the FY2023 peak of SEK 2.67. Reported net income about SEK 405m, 490m, 575m, 690m, 795m; reported EPS about SEK 1.50, 1.80, 2.10, 2.50, 2.90, from SEK 1.28. The chain takes 46 percent of adjusted EBITA in FY2026 and about 40 percent in FY2030, against 27 percent in FY2021 and 44 percent in FY2025.
  • Free cash flow after lease payments: SEK 550m, 650m, 800m, 895m, 960m, from about SEK 630m in FY2025 on the same definition (operating cash flow SEK 1,010m less capex SEK 97m less leases about SEK 280m). Lease payments rise from about SEK 280m to SEK 390m with Fabri and growth; capex stays at 0.7 to 0.8 percent of sales; cash tax runs at the book rate; cash interest excludes the non-cash FX and discounting items. Consensus free cash flow (SEK 672m, 1,101m, 1,210m) appears to be after leases in FY2026 and on an unclear basis after; the FY2027 gap is working capital.
P-09

The net likely impact on the main line items over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years

C57

Question asked After assessing the other questions/items in the set in greater detail, what is the net likely impact to the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

Judgement: the next four years are an earnings recovery on slower revenue growth, the mirror image of the last six, which were rapid acquired revenue growth on a declining margin. Revenue grows at about 8.6 percent a year compound from FY2025 to FY2030 (FY2019 to FY2025: 15.6 percent, almost all acquired), adjusted EBITA at about 13 percent (FY2019 to FY2025: 11 percent in money but from 8.5 to 6.4 percent in margin), adjusted EPS at about 15 percent (FY2019 to FY2025: flat to down, SEK 1.49 to 1.76 on the two bases, with a peak in FY2023), and free cash flow after leases at about 9 percent. The net impact is positive and front-loaded into FY2026 and FY2027 on the operating lines, where utilisation, bad-debt normalisation, legacy roll-off and Fabri's mix add about 100 basis points of margin in two years; it is offset on the lines below EBITA by a chain that has become permanently heavier (P-08) and on the cash line by a working-capital loop that consumes about 11 to 12 percent of incremental revenue; and from FY2028 the growth in every line slows to the organic rate plus a restrained acquisition engine. Against consensus the net impact is: revenue in line then above, EBITA below by about 7 percent, EPS below by 9, 19 and 18 percent, free cash flow below by about 20 to 40 percent in FY2026 to FY2027.

The lines together, history against base (SEK millions; adjusted basis; brokers' EBITDA = adjusted EBITA plus depreciation excluding acquired intangibles):

(FY2023 adjusted EBITA and EPS are the brokers' basis actuals; FY2021 acquisitions and free cash flow are approximate from FactSet's investing and operating lines.)

What drives each difference from the last four to six years, in order of size:

  1. Margin recovery (about SEK 200m of EBITA by FY2028 on FY2025 volumes): utilisation of a labour base pruned in 2024, bad debt normalising, legacy contracts rolling off, Fabri's mix. This is the same mechanism that took the margin down in 2022 to 2025, running the other way, and it is the company's own 2017 to 2021 history re-run at a slower pace (the 8 percent arrives in FY2030 in my base, against 2017 to 2021 when it was there every year).
  2. Volume (about SEK 160m of EBITA by FY2028 from organic growth at the segment margin): the Swedish cycle's turn, with 12 to 18 months of visibility and a lag of two to four quarters from starts to installation revenue, so 2027 is the strong organic year and 2028 fades. In 2019 to 2023 volume growth was mainly bought.
  3. Germany (about SEK 180m of EBITA by FY2028, of which SEK 90m is attributable after minorities): new to the model, higher-margin, 49 percent leaked, and priced in a market where private equity sets the multiple.
  4. The chain below EBITA (about SEK -130m of pre-tax drag by FY2028 against the consensus path, and about SEK -200m against the 2021 structure): interest flat at about SEK 210m rather than falling, tax 24 percent rather than 20, minorities SEK 135m rather than SEK 32m, amortisation SEK 180m rather than SEK 130m on the reported line.
  5. The acquisition engine (about SEK 60m of EBITA by FY2030 from Nordic bolt-ons at 60 percent of the old pace): restarted in H2 2027, which keeps net debt flat to rising in money and leverage falling in ratio; in 2019 to 2023 the engine was the whole story and it was funded with debt and equity at the top of the market.
  6. Working capital and leases (about SEK -100m to -170m of annual cash against a zero-build assumption): growth years consume cash at this company; lease payments rise with Fabri.

What stays the same: capex at 0.7 to 0.8 percent of sales; a dividend at about 30 percent of (reported) profit; no buybacks; a share count that drifts up 0.3 to 0.8 percent a year; recurring "non-recurring" items of about SEK 50m; the Swedish segment as 65 to 70 percent of sales and the whole of the margin story.

The net, in the Consumer's terms: the model's EPS doubles from FY2025 to FY2030 (SEK 1.76 to 3.59) and that is a positive case; but FY2027 consensus (SEK 3.01) is already my bull case (8 percent margin in 2027, financial items falling to SEK 200m, a 22 percent tax rate gives about SEK 2.96), so the lines most likely to move over the next 18 months are the brokers' below-EBITA lines and FY2027 EPS, downward, while revenue and EBITA hold. That is the asymmetry the third and fourth sets should carry.

P-10

The base case for the key operational drivers and the key financial line items over the next 18 months to four years

C55

Question asked Pulling together all the above answers, in all sets so far, what is your carefully considered base case for the key operational drivers and the key financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

Judgement: the base case is a utilisation-and-mix margin recovery to 7.4 percent in FY2027 and 8.0 percent in FY2030, on organic growth of about 8 percent in H2 2026 fading to 5 percent in FY2027 and 3 to 3.5 percent after, with Fabri adding SEK 1.45bn of sales at 11.5 percent from FY2027, a restrained bolt-on restart from H2 2027, net financial items flat at about SEK 210m to 225m, tax at 23 to 24 percent, minorities at SEK 125m to 135m, and cash conversion reaching 100 percent on the company's definition during 2027. It produces adjusted EPS of SEK 2.07, 2.45, 2.79, 3.21 and 3.59 for FY2026 to FY2030 (+18, +19, +14, +15, +12 percent), free cash flow after leases of SEK 550m to 960m, company-basis leverage of 2.2x at end-2027 and 1.8x at end-2030, and a dividend rebuilding to SEK 1.10. The cone around FY2027 EPS is SEK 1.80 (stalled recovery into the hikes, 6.5 percent margin) to SEK 3.00 (8 percent margin in 2027 with falling interest and a 22 percent tax rate); the probability mass is centred a little below my base because three of the four below-EBITA items can only disappoint relative to consensus, and the operating upside (a faster Swedish margin, a faster engine) is real but needs the Q4 2026 cash print to open it.

The operational drivers, with the figure in the base and the evidence behind it:

  • Swedish organic growth: about +12 percent in Q3 2026 and +8 percent in Q4 2026 on trough comparatives (Q3 2025 -3.6 percent); +5 percent in FY2027 (Q1 +6, Q2 +5, Q3 +5, Q4 +4 percent as comparatives harden: Q1 2026 +4.9, Q2 2026 +8.4 percent); +4 percent in FY2028, +3.5 percent after. Evidence: building starts +8.0 percent in July 2026 (non-residential +13.2 percent), backlog SEK 7,534m (+13.1 percent) and 1.21x installation sales at group level, metropolitan projects moving into execution, the Skanska 2026 to 2032 and NCC frameworks; against it the Riksbank (hike expected November 2026, 2.0 to 2.5 percent by mid-2027), residential new-build "extremely sluggish", 7 percent direct residential exposure, and the hiring constraint.
  • Swedish adjusted EBITA margin: about 6.6 percent in FY2026 (Q1 5.0, Q2 7.0, Q3 about 7.1, Q4 about 7.9 percent), 7.2 percent in FY2027 (the lead broker's figure, which I adopt because the wage round and the legacy roll-off net to about zero and utilisation and bad debt give the gain), 7.6 percent in FY2028, 7.8 and 7.9 percent after. The peak was 9.5 percent in Q4 2021; the trough 4.1 percent in Q1 2025.
  • Norway: revenue SEK 2,440m in FY2026 (+6 percent), +6 percent in FY2027, +4 percent after; margin 5.8 percent in FY2026 (Q1 4.7, Q2 6.8 percent), 6.0 percent in FY2027, 6.5 percent after. Backlog +40.9 percent on public infrastructure (Aker hospital NOK 440m, the Oslo nursing home); Norges Bank at 4.50 percent; construction-cost inflation 5.4 percent; the long-duration turnkey book is the write-down risk.
  • Finland: revenue SEK 1,803m in FY2026 (H1 +30 percent organic in Q1 on industrial work), +4 percent in FY2027, +3 percent after; margin 8.5 percent in FY2026 (Q1 12.0, Q2 8.1 percent), 8.0 percent after. Backlog -3.8 percent at Q2 2026; industrial and data-centre work, residential frozen; the segment merges with Germany from Q3 2026 with a one-month lag, so the readable figure will be a blend.
  • Germany (Fabri): consolidated from 1 August 2026 with a one-month lag, so one month in Q3 2026 and three in Q4 2026 (four reported months in FY2026, about SEK 445m); SEK 1,450m in FY2027 (EUR 117m pro forma 2025 plus about 10 percent a year from bolt-ons, at EUR/SEK about 10.9), SEK 1,595m, 1,760m, 1,935m after; margin 11.5 percent throughout (12.7 percent reported for 2025; shaded for German capacity utilisation at 68 percent, heat-pump subsidy volatility and bolt-on dilution). Minorities 49 percent to 2028, 32 percent from 2029, falling through the 2030 to 2033 options.
  • Nordic bolt-ons: SEK 150m of sales in FY2027 (bought mid-year), SEK 400m in FY2028, SEK 500m in FY2029, SEK 550m in FY2030, at a 7 percent margin and about 0.5x sales; the gate is company-basis leverage below 2.5x, which I expect in the Q1 or Q2 2027 report. The historical SEK 800m a year is the upside branch and adds about 2 points of growth and SEK 20m of EBITA a year on top.
  • Group adjusted EBITA margin: 6.9, 7.4, 7.7, 7.9, 8.0 percent. The FY2025 to FY2028 bridge, in basis points: utilisation +60, bad debt +40, legacy roll-off +30, Fabri mix +40, Norway and Finland normalisation net 0, 2027 wage round net -40, country-organisation cost and other -10; total +120 to 130.
  • Below EBITA: depreciation excluding acquired intangibles 2.6 percent of sales (SEK 390m to 535m); amortisation of acquired intangibles SEK 150m to 185m (reported line only); net financial items SEK -225m, -220m, -210m, -210m, -215m; tax 23.0, 23.5, 24.0, 24.0, 24.0 percent; minorities SEK 70m, 125m, 135m, 115m, 100m; shares 268.7, 269.9, 272.1, 273.1, 273.1 million (year-end); dividend SEK 0.60, 0.75, 0.90, 1.00, 1.10.
  • Cash: operating cash flow SEK 960m, 1,100m, 1,285m, 1,410m, 1,505m; capex SEK 110m to 155m; lease payments SEK 300m to 390m; free cash flow after leases SEK 550m, 650m, 800m, 895m, 960m; acquisitions SEK 520m, 410m, 550m, 785m, 850m; dividends paid SEK 134m (2026), 161m, 202m, 245m, 273m. Working capital (gross FactSet measure) 11.7, 11.4, 11.3, 11.2, 11.2 percent of sales at year-end. Company cash conversion about 92 percent for FY2026, about 100 percent in FY2027 and FY2028.

The financial lines, next 18 months by quarter (revenue growth, adjusted EBITA margin, brokers' EBITDA margin): Q3 2026 +12.6 percent, 6.8 percent, 9.4 percent; Q4 2026 +15.0 percent, 7.6 percent, 10.2 percent; Q1 2027 +15.5 percent, 6.5 percent, 9.1 percent; Q2 2027 +15.0 percent, 7.5 percent, 10.1 percent; Q3 2027 +14.0 percent, 7.3 percent, 9.9 percent; Q4 2027 +5.5 percent, 8.1 percent, 10.7 percent. The Fabri consolidation lifts reported growth to mid-teens from Q4 2026 to Q3 2027 and drops it to mid-single digits in Q4 2027; the Consumer should read organic and segment lines, not the headline.

Against consensus and the company: revenue SEK 15,053m, 16,860m, 17,995m against SEK 15,193m, 17,171m, 17,863m (-1, -2, +1 percent); brokers' EBITDA SEK 1,430m, 1,686m, 1,853m against SEK 1,495m, 1,770m, 1,895m (-4, -5, -2 percent); adjusted EPS SEK 2.07, 2.45, 2.79 against SEK 2.28, 3.01, 3.42 (-9, -19, -18 percent; Pareto's SEK 1.79, 2.55, 3.14 is the nearest broker to me, Nordea's SEK 2.09 for FY2026 the nearest single figure); the company's targets: 8 percent margin reached FY2030 (consensus FY2028; founder "urgent"), 10 percent growth reached only FY2026 to FY2027, 2.5x and 100 percent conversion reached in 2027.

The cone and its skew over twelve months: FY2027 adjusted EPS SEK 1.80 to 3.00, base SEK 2.45; I put about 25 percent of the mass below SEK 2.20 (a stall, a write-down, or a wage round absorbed on fixed-price work), 50 percent between SEK 2.20 and 2.70, 25 percent above SEK 2.70 (the Swedish segment prints 7.5 percent-plus in Q4 2026 and the Q4 cash release is normal, opening the faster engine). The skew against consensus is clearly negative; the skew against my base is roughly symmetric on the operating lines and negative on the below-EBITA lines.

What changes the base, with thresholds: (1) tomorrow, Swedish segment margin at or above 7.5 percent with organic growth near 12 percent, and operating cash flow near SEK 200m with receivables under SEK 1,800m, would move my FY2026 margin to 7.1 percent and FY2027 to 7.6 percent; a Swedish margin under 7.0 percent or operating cash flow under SEK 100m would move FY2027 to 7.1 percent and delay the restart to 2028. (2) The Q4 2026 report (February 2027): rolling conversion at or above 100 percent and pro forma leverage at or below 2.5x keep the H2 2027 restart; a SEK 0.70 dividend proposal would confirm it. (3) Spring 2027: the electrical and plumbing settlements; actual pay near 3 percent keeps the -40 basis points, an outcome nearer the 8 percent minimum-wage headline applied broadly would double it. (4) Fabri's first full quarter (Q4 2026 report): a margin near 12 percent and positive cash conversion keep 11.5 percent; below 10 percent would take 10 basis points off the group and raise the question of the 2029 hurdle. (5) Any Nordic customer insolvency of Northvolt's size, or a Bravida-type governance read-across, is outside the base.

Part 4. The reasoning behind the inputs

Basis of the inputs. EBITDA is on the brokers' basis (before amortisation of acquired intangibles and before items affecting comparability), which is the company's adjusted EBITA plus depreciation of property, equipment and right-of-use assets; FY2025 actual on this basis is SEK 1,229m (9.0 percent) against adjusted EBITA of SEK 875m (6.4 percent), so the depreciation line runs at about 2.6 percent of revenue. EBITDA less the Part 1 depreciation line therefore equals adjusted EBITA (SEK 1,039m, 1,248m, 1,386m, 1,516m, 1,640m for FY2026 to FY2030; margins 6.9, 7.4, 7.7, 7.9, 8.0 percent). Revenue for FY2025 is SEK 13,598m (FactSet; SEK 13,599m on the brokers' basis), so the growth inputs give revenue of SEK 15,053m, 16,860m, 17,995m, 19,195m, 20,496m.

Two things the Financial Analyst must know about the inputs.

  1. The form has no line for minority interests, and minorities are material here (SEK 32m in FY2025, SEK 36m in H1 2026 alone before Fabri; Fabri at 51 percent from 1 August 2026 adds about SEK 55m a year). I have therefore folded the minority charge into "Net financial items", grossed up at the tax rate so that the model's post-tax profit is attributable profit. The two components are: net financial items proper SEK -225m, -220m, -210m, -210m, -215m; minorities SEK -70m, -125m, -135m, -115m, -100m, grossed up at the year's tax rate to SEK -91m, -163m, -178m, -151m, -132m. If the model has its own minorities line, enter the financial items at the first set of figures and the minorities at the second (ungrossed) set instead. The model's interest cover and any cash-interest derivation should use the first set.
  2. Working capital is on the FactSet gross definition the block uses (receivables plus inventories less payables; 11.7 percent of revenue at end-FY2025 and at Q2 2026; 13.8 percent at end-FY2024; 13.1 percent at end-FY2023), not the company's net definition (1.6 percent at end-2025, 2.3 percent at Q2 2026). Holding FY2026 at 11.7 percent charges the model about SEK 170m of build, which blends the operating build I expect (about SEK 100m, after a Q4 release smaller than Q4 2025's SEK 451m) with Fabri's acquired balance (about SEK 160m, which in the accounts arrives through the acquisition line). From FY2027 the small annual improvement reflects the Instalco 2.0 working-capital controls; I do not assume SPIE-style negative working capital.
