The operating drivers behind the inputs

  • Organic growth: group about +9 percent in H2 2026 (Sweden about +12 percent in Q3 and +8 percent in Q4 on the trough comparatives of Q3 2025 -3.6 percent and Q4 2025 +4.9 percent), +5 percent in FY2027 (Q1 +6, Q2 +5, Q3 +5, Q4 +4 percent as comparatives harden from Q1 2026 +4.9 and Q2 2026 +8.4 percent), +3.5 percent in FY2028, +3 percent in FY2029 and FY2030. About 2.5 to 3 points of each year's organic growth is price and cost inflation passed through (Swedish construction-cost inflation 3.7 percent in September 2026, rising toward 4.6 percent then easing to 2.8 percent by late 2028); real volume is about 6 percent in H2 2026, 2 percent in FY2027 and 0.5 to 1 percent after, bounded by hiring. Evidence: Swedish building starts +8.0 percent in July 2026 (non-residential +13.2 percent), backlog SEK 10,937m (+17 percent, +14.8 percent organic, 1.21x installation sales), installation revenue lagging starts by two to four quarters, the Riksbank's November 2026 hike and a 2.0 to 2.5 percent policy rate by mid-2027, residential new-build still weak.
  • Acquired growth: Fabri (EUR 86m of 2025 sales, EUR 117m pro forma, 23 subsidiaries, about 800 employees) at EUR/SEK about 10.9, growing about 10 percent a year from German bolt-ons; one month in Q3 2026, three in Q4 2026, twelve from FY2027. Nordic bolt-ons restart in H2 2027 once company-basis leverage prints below 2.5x (expected in the Q1 or Q2 2027 report): SEK 300m of annual sales bought mid-2027, SEK 500m a year in 2028 and 2029, SEK 550m in 2030, at about 0.5x sales and a 7 percent margin. This is about 60 percent of the historical SEK 800m a year of consideration; the full historical pace is the upside branch.
  • Currency: unhedged; a stronger krona takes about 0.5 percent a year off reported growth in FY2026 and FY2027; nil assumed after.
  • Margin bridge from FY2025 (6.4 percent) to FY2028 (7.7 percent), in basis points: utilisation of the labour base pruned in 2024 +60 (incremental adjusted EBITA margin about 12 percent on Swedish organic revenue through H1 2027, 10 percent in H2 2027, the segment margin from 2028 as hiring catches the backlog); bad-debt normalisation +40 (other external costs back from 8.5 to 9.0 percent of sales toward 7.5 to 8.0 percent; two-thirds already in the H1 2026 run-rate); roll-off of low-margin Swedish contracts signed in 2024 and 2025 +30 (completing between Q4 2026 and Q2 2027); Fabri mix +40; spring 2027 Swedish wage round net -40 (a 3 percent actual pay rise on employee costs of 36.8 percent of sales is 1.1 percent of sales; two-thirds recovered through service repricing, cost-plus pass-through, selectivity and utilisation; one-third absorbed on the un-indexed fixed-price book, 43 percent of sales); country-organisation cost and other -10. No real price in the margin. From FY2029, +10 basis points a year from bolt-on accretion and about 15 basis points of procurement and finance standardisation across the 150 subsidiaries.
  • Seasonality in Part 3: Q1 and Q3 are the weak quarters (Q1 2026 adjusted EBITA 5.8 percent, Q3 2025 6.0 percent), Q4 the strongest (Q4 2025 7.2 percent); Fabri's consolidation lifts reported growth to the mid-teens from Q4 2026 to Q3 2027 and drops it to 5.5 percent in Q4 2027. Quarterly adjusted EBITA margins behind the EBITDA inputs: 6.8, 7.6, 6.5, 7.5, 7.3, 8.1 percent (EBITDA margin = adjusted EBITA margin plus 2.6 points).
  • Depreciation (excluding acquired intangibles): 2.6 percent of revenue, rising in money with Fabri's vehicle and premises leases and with growth. Amortisation of acquired intangibles (not in the form; reported line only): about SEK 150m, 175m, 180m, 185m, 185m, rising with Fabri's purchase price allocation and the bolt-ons.
  • Net financial items (proper): FY2026 SEK -225m (H1 -124 including a SEK 33m unrealised FX loss; H2 about -100 with Fabri's step-2 debt of about SEK 393m from August and the first Riksbank hike); FY2027 -220m (external interest about SEK 143m on average company-basis net debt of about SEK 3.1bn at about 4.6 percent, lease interest about SEK 30m, other items about SEK 40m); then -210m, -210m, -215m as deleveraging, the margin ratchet below 2.5x and the bolt-on restart offset each other. Each 100 basis points on the floating debt is about SEK 31m. Consolidated FY2025 net financial expense was SEK 145m (external interest SEK 126m, lease interest SEK 26m, FX gain SEK 33m, other); FactSet's SEK 167m is a different basis.
  • Tax: 23.0 percent in FY2026 (H1 2026 was about 20 percent; FY2025's 28.1 percent was built from SEK 31m of unrecognised losses, SEK 8m of non-deductibles and SEK 8m of utilised carryforwards on a 20.6 percent Swedish base), 23.5 percent in FY2027 and 24.0 percent after, reflecting Fabri's German profit at about 30 percent and a recurring level of unrecognised losses in weak subsidiaries.
  • Minorities: SEK 70m (FY2026), 125m, 135m, 115m (Fabri to 68 percent from 2029), 100m (first tranche of the 2030 to 2033 options); existing subsidiary minorities about SEK 65m to 75m a year plus Fabri's 49 percent of about SEK 110m of net profit.
  • Share count: 268.4 million at end-FY2025; 268.75 million at June 2026 after the TSM treasury-share transfer; warrants add 1.2 million in FY2027 (2024/2027 series at SEK 44.32, half exercised), 2.2 million in FY2028 (2025/2028 series at SEK 31.40), 1.0 million in FY2029 (2026/2029 series at SEK 44.37, half); the 2023/2026 series at SEK 64.90 lapses. The cash from warrant exercise (about SEK 50m to 70m a year in 2027 to 2029) is left out of Part 2 to avoid double counting. No buybacks (the 5 percent mandate has never been used); no share-funded acquisitions in the base.
  • Dividends: about 30 percent of reported net profit under the policy, which is 38 to 42 percent of reported and 29 to 31 percent of adjusted EPS on my figures; declared per fiscal year, paid the following May.
  • Capital spending: 0.7 to 0.8 percent of revenue (FY2025 SEK 97m including intangibles). Lease payments: SEK 300m in FY2026 (lease liabilities SEK 722m at June 2026, SEK 658m at end-2025, of which SEK 276m current; lease interest SEK 26m in FY2025), rising with Fabri and growth.
  • Acquisitions: FY2026 SEK -520m (H1 SEK 56m on FactSet's line; Fabri step 2 about SEK 393m in Q3; earn-outs and minority buy-outs about SEK 70m in H2); FY2027 -410m (Nordic bolt-ons about SEK 150m, German bolt-ons about SEK 110m, earn-outs and minority buy-outs about SEK 150m; the maximum undiscounted contingent consideration was SEK 240m at June 2026 and SEK 160m was paid for minority buy-outs in 2025); FY2028 -550m; FY2029 -785m (including Fabri step 3, 17 percent, about SEK 235m at the 0.9x-sales architecture DNB Carnegie describes); FY2030 -850m (bolt-ons plus about SEK 125m for the first tranche of the Fabri options). No disposals.
  • Resulting balance sheet (not an input): company-basis net debt about SEK 3,100m at end-2026, 3,040m end-2027, 3,000m end-2028, 3,135m end-2029, 3,290m end-2030; company-basis leverage about 2.6 to 2.7x reported at end-2026 (about 2.5x pro forma for a full year of Fabri), 2.2x end-2027, 2.0x end-2028, 1.9x end-2029, 1.8x end-2030.

The company's guidance, and how your inputs compare with it

The company gives no annual guidance. Its five through-cycle targets: sales growth of at least 10 percent a year (my inputs: 10.7 and 12.0 percent in the Fabri years, then 6.7 to 6.8 percent, so met only in FY2026 and FY2027); EBITA margin of at least 8 percent (mine reaches 7.9 percent in FY2029 and 8.0 percent in FY2030; the founder calls it urgent and has tied his tenure to reaching it sustainably; consensus places it in FY2028); net debt to EBITDA not above 2.5x (mine below 2.5x from H1 2027 on a reported basis and about 2.5x pro forma at end-2026); cash conversion of at least 100 percent (mine about 92 percent for FY2026, about 100 percent in FY2027 and FY2028); dividend payout of about 30 percent (mine about 40 percent of reported, 30 percent of adjusted, so in line in spirit; the FY2025 payout was 39 percent of reported). Management's informal commentary (Swedish legacy contracts "diminishing over the next several quarters"; the Q2 cash shortfall as timing; Finland's 12 percent "exceptionally high"; the market recovery "highly uneven") is consistent with these inputs. The lead broker's Swedish segment path (7.2 percent in 2027, 7.6 percent in 2028) is adopted as the Swedish input.

The bridge from the company's own reported figures to the basis consensus uses

FY2025, SEK millions: reported EBITA 800 (5.9 percent) plus items affecting comparability 75 (SEK 64m Northvolt receivable impairment in Q1, SEK 11m executive compensation in Q2) = adjusted EBITA 875 (6.4 percent); plus depreciation of property, equipment and right-of-use assets 354 = brokers' adjusted EBITDA 1,229 (9.0 percent), the basis of the consensus EBITDA (SEK 1,495m, 1,770m, 1,895m) and of the Part 1 EBITDA input. FactSet's operating EBITDA of 1,092 and adjusted EBIT of 558 are on FactSet's standardised basis and sit SEK 137m and SEK 110m below the company's adjusted EBITDA and reported EBIT (668 = 800 less 132 of amortisation of acquired intangibles); do not compute growth from FactSet's history to consensus. Brokers' EPS (FY2025 SEK 1.76; consensus SEK 2.28, 3.01, 3.42) is before amortisation of acquired intangibles and before items affecting comparability, after net financial items, tax and minorities: adjusted EBITA 875 less net financial items 145 = 730 pre-tax; less tax at about 28 percent and minorities 32 gives about 494, SEK 1.84 on 268.4 million shares, close to the SEK 1.76 actual on the brokers' basis. Reported basic EPS was SEK 1.28 (net income 344 after 132 of amortisation, 75 of items and the 28.1 percent tax rate). My cross-check EPS below is on the brokers' basis; the model's EPS, built from the Part 1 inputs with minorities folded into the financial line, should match it. The reported EPS corresponding to my base is about SEK 1.50, 1.80, 2.10, 2.50, 2.90 (adjusted EPS less about SEK 0.45 of amortisation after a partial tax shield and about SEK 0.15 of recurring items). Consensus's FY2026 EPS range of SEK 1.79 (Pareto) to SEK 2.30 (DNB Carnegie) is partly basis: DNB Carnegie uses a 20 percent tax rate and adds back the full amortisation.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 3 and Layer 4

Research requests for the next set

Company: Instalco AB (INSTAL-SE, Nasdaq Stockholm): a Nordic technical installation group of about 150 decentralised subsidiaries in electrical, heating and plumbing, ventilation, industrial installation, industrial scaffolding and technical consulting, reporting the segments Sweden (about 71 percent of FY2025 net sales of SEK 13,598 million), Norway and, from Q3 2026, Finland and Germany (the German platform Fabri AG, 23 subsidiaries, consolidated at 51 percent from 1 August 2026 with a one-month reporting lag), with sales about 35 percent service, 33 percent renovation and 30 percent new production, run since 1 August 2025 by returning founder Per Sjöstrand under the "Instalco 2.0" margin-over-volume programme toward long-term targets of at least 8 percent EBITA margin, at least 10 percent annual growth over a cycle, net debt below 2.5x EBITDA and 100 percent cash conversion.

Request: T-D1

Peers: Bravida Holding AB (BRAV-SE; the closest listed Nordic installer, which held a 6.7 percent Swedish EBITA margin in 2025 on falling Swedish sales and is now pivoting to data-centre growth); Assemblin Caverion Group (private, Triton-owned; the Nordic number one, 7.9 percent adjusted EBITA margin in 2025 after its 2024 merger, deleveraging from about 4.0x); Nimlas Group (private; a decentralised Swedish consolidator whose Swedish adjusted EBITA margin rose from 6.1 percent in 2024 to 7.9 percent in 2025 on about 1 percent organic growth); SPIE SA (SPIE-FR; the European multi-technical benchmark whose EBITA margin rose from 6.7 percent in 2023 to 7.6 percent in 2025 through selectivity, pricing and acquisitions); Fasadgruppen Group AB (FG-SE; the decentralised Nordic contractor whose margin recovery failed, ending in a SEK 504 million rights issue in February 2026); InstallatørGruppen (Copenhagen-listed since June 2026; a decentralised roll-up of 48 acquisitions since 2023 with a 9 to 10 percent adjusted EBITA margin); Green Landscaping Group and Eltel (the platforms Instalco's founder chaired; Eltel's margin improved for eleven consecutive quarters to Q1 2026, Green Landscaping reported SEK 79 million of one-off costs in Q3 2026); Caverion before its merger (the precedent for a listed Nordic installer restoring margin after project write-downs, 2017 to 2022). Operating conditions: a Swedish construction recovery that began in 2026 (building starts up 8.0 percent year on year in July 2026 per DNB Carnegie) running into a Riksbank hiking cycle (a November 2026 hike expected by Nordea and Barclays) and Norges Bank at 4.50 percent after its September 2026 hike; the spring 2027 Swedish collective wage round for electrical and plumbing-and-ventilation installers on contracts priced in 2026 without automatic indexation; a mid-sized Swedish tender market with elevated bidder counts and public buyers awarding on lowest price; competitors' capacity absorbed by data-centre contracts (Bravida's order intake up 44 percent in Q2 2026); the German market in contraction (construction PMI 42.1 in July 2026, capacity utilisation 68 percent in August 2026 per Deutsche Bank) with an infrastructure fund ahead; a decentralised model being tightened by the Instalco 2.0 baseline, country managers and quarterly internal-control reports. Inputs: The expected changes to test: a return of the group adjusted EBITA margin from 6.4 percent in FY2025 (5.9 percent reported) toward the company's 8 percent target, which the FactSet consensus of three brokers (DNB Carnegie, Pareto Securities, Nordea, 21 October 2026) places in FY2028, with DNB Carnegie's published Swedish segment path of 7.2 percent in 2027 and 7.6 percent in 2028 (29 September 2026); a return of cash conversion from 90 percent rolling at Q2 2026 to the 100 percent target and of company net debt to EBITDA from 2.6x to below 2.5x; the restart of bolt-on acquisitions after a pause (one acquisition in H1 2026 against a five-year average spend DNB Carnegie puts at about SEK 800 million a year); the scaling of Fabri AG (EUR 86 million of 2025 sales, 12.7 percent EBITA margin) in Germany through further bolt-ons at German multiples; and consensus EPS growth of 29, 32 and 14 percent for FY2026 to FY2028 (FactSet, 21 October 2026) from SEK 1.76 in FY2025. The record to test: adjusted EBITA margins above 8 percent every year from 2017 to 2021 with acquisitions running and volumes rising, the decline to 5.9 percent through 2022 to 2025, write-downs in Norway (2018, 2021), southern Sweden (2024) and on Northvolt (SEK 64 million, Q1 2025), cash conversion above 100 percent in most years, and the first four quarters under the returning founder (Q4 2025 margin 7.2 percent, Q2 2026 7.1 percent, organic growth from -3.6 percent in Q3 2025 to +8.4 percent in Q2 2026). The strengths to test: local relational lock-in through retained brands and founder-managers; selective bidding and the ABC customer prioritisation; a service share that rose from about 30 percent to 35 to 37 percent; a variable cost base and the capacity actions of 2024 (eight subsidiaries closed or merged, 110 employees and 100 subcontractors removed); the founder's record elsewhere; and the structured, staged German entry. The strengths it would need to acquire: pricing power at the tender stage, digital and procurement tooling of the Bravida and SPIE kind, project controls that prevent fixed-price write-downs across 150 autonomous units, a German acquisition capability against private-equity bidders (Brookfield's 15 German installer deals since 2023 per DNB Carnegie), and working-capital discipline during growth. Focus: How many decentralised Nordic or European installation groups that lost two to three points of margin in a downturn regained them, how long it took, what distinguished those that did (Bravida Denmark, SPIE, Caverion) from those that did not (Fasadgruppen, Eltel), and whether Instalco's demonstrated strengths (selectivity, service mix, a pruned cost base, a founder-operator) are the ones that decided those outcomes or whether the deciders were strengths Instalco still lacks (indexed pricing, project controls, centralised procurement, a self-funded acquisition engine).

Peers: Bravida Holding AB (BRAV-SE; the Nordic installer with the longest public record of segment margins and cash conversion through two cycles); SPIE SA (SPIE-FR; the benchmark whose margin progression came from many small drivers rather than one); Fasadgruppen Group AB (FG-SE; the case where a forecast resting on acquired growth and margin recovery failed on cash and leverage); Caverion (the precedent for repeated project-related forecast misses, with write-downs of EUR 27.1 million in 2017, EUR 9.3 million in 2018, EUR 17.1 million in 2019 and EUR 12.8 million in 2020); Eltel (restructurings in 2023 that removed about 225 positions while margins stayed near break-even); Nimlas Group and InstallatørGruppen (private and newly listed decentralised consolidators whose reported margins and cash conversion show the dispersion of the model); Green Landscaping Group (the founder's other platform, where forecasts rested on acquisitions and were set back by 2026 write-downs); Burkhalter Holding AG (BRKN-CH; a decentralised installer whose shares fell about 28 percent over six months to October 2026 without a disclosed cause).
