Layer 3 and Layer 4

Layer 3: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months
and Layer 4: Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

10 questions in 4 groups · 15,764 words · written 23 October 2026, 11:06 to 11:25 UK, Fable, ricky login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes for this layer

The Investment Analyst's title
Instalco (INSTAL-SE): third and fourth sets of questions, the predictability of the forecasts and how far our view is reflected in guidance, consensus and the narrative

Written 23 October 2026 by the Investment Analyst, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo for Richard.

Read this first: the basis of every figure below.

  • Everything here is pre-Q3. Instalco publishes its Q3 2026 report today, 23 October 2026, and none of my files contains it; the AlphaSense reports written for this work were run on 22 and 23 October and say so. Bravida reports the same day and sets the sector read. Where the print would change a judgement I name the threshold, as the first two sets did, so that the Consumer can update on the print rather than re-derive.
  • Bases, unchanged from the earlier sets: money in SEK millions; the company's adjusted EBITA (FY2025 SEK 875m, 6.4 percent); the brokers' basis for consensus (EBITDA SEK 1,229m, 9.0 percent; EPS SEK 1.76, before amortisation of acquired intangibles and items affecting comparability, after financial items, tax and minorities). Consensus FY2026 to FY2028 EPS SEK 2.28, 3.01, 3.42 rests on three brokers (DNB Carnegie Buy SEK 47, Pareto Buy SEK 48, Nordea Hold SEK 45 from 19 October 2026); I write "the three brokers" where it matters. Our base case (the second set, now the model of record after update 1): revenue SEK 15,054m, 16,861m, 17,990m; adjusted EBITA margin 6.9, 7.4, 7.7 percent; adjusted EPS SEK 2.08, 2.45, 2.79, which is 9, 19 and 18 percent below consensus. Price SEK 42.52 (21 October 2026); 24-month-forward P/E 12.7 to 12.9x on consensus EPS (the 44th to 46th percentile of the stock's own history) and 15.7x on our EPS.
  • Three things about the one-page model summary that the Consumer must know before using it for topics #6, #11 and #12; the detail is in D-01 and in the gap explanation that follows it. (1) The model's FY2025 EPS of SEK 1.37 is the Viewforth definition, which is after amortisation of acquired intangibles, while FY2026 onward is on the brokers' basis, before it. The "+51.4 percent" FY2026 EPS growth in the summary, and part of the "+48.4 percent from earnings" in the 18-month return, are therefore a basis artefact; like-for-like FY2026 EPS growth is +18 percent from SEK 1.76. (2) The summary's "EBIT (Viewforth: EBITA) against consensus: +11.1, +4.0, +6.1 percent" sets our EBITA, before amortisation, against consensus EBIT, after about SEK 130m to 150m of it; on the same basis we are about 3, 5 to 8 and 1 to 5 percent below consensus, the range depending on whose amortisation is added back. (3) The free-cash-flow gap the summary flags is explained after D-01 and leads to one input change (working capital); a second change (acquisitions) follows from new evidence on earn-outs and minority buy-outs. Both are in inputs-changed.md.

The third set: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months

Layer 3: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months

Risks

❌ D467 questions
D-0168D-0252D-0344D-0438D-0547D-0650D-0752

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The predictability of Instalco's forecasts over the next 18 months divides cleanly by line. Revenue and adjusted EBITA are predictable to within about 2 percent and about 30 basis points of margin, because two independent passes built them from the same demonstrated mechanism (volume returning to a labour base pruned in 2024, bad debt normalising, legacy work rolling off, Fabri's mix), because three of the five building blocks are already in the H1 2026 run-rate, because the company reports what is needed to track them (segment organic growth and margin, backlog, headcount), and because the precedents say decentralised installers with selectivity and a 37 percent service share regain two to three points over two to three years. Adjusted EPS and cash are not: the EPS forecast depends on four below-EBITA lines that disclosure does not let anyone forecast within SEK 0.10 each, that the three brokers model so differently that their EPS ranking is the inverse of their EBITDA ranking, and that can only disappoint against consensus; cash depends on a seasonal pattern that will be judged on two prints and on a working-capital loop that consumes 11 to 12 percent of every krona of growth. The drivers are concentrated where they are strongest (Sweden, utilisation, one macro variable) and diffuse where they are weakest (the chain below EBITA), the cone for FY2027 EPS is SEK 1.70 to 3.00 around SEK 2.45 with a fat lower half against consensus, and the multiple that prices all this is company-driven (88 percent idiosyncratic), which means our view transmits to the price and so do the write-downs and the cash misses. The earlier sets are congruent on everything that decides direction; the corrections I make (the working-capital double count, the earn-out floor, the EPS basis break and the EBIT comparison in the model summary) are to the mechanics, not to the case. For the memo: the forecasts are predictable enough on the operating lines to be stated with confidence, predictable enough on EPS to say that consensus is more likely to be cut than met, and not predictable enough on cash to rest the case on the engine restarting before the February 2027 print shows it.

D-01

How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets?

C68

Question asked How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets: to what extent do they support and align with each other, rather than contradict or jar?

Judgement: highly congruent on the shape of the case and on every direction that matters; the differences between the first and second sets are differences of pace and of basis, not of direction, and the one region where they jar with each other, the chain below EBITA (tax, amortisation of acquired intangibles, minorities), is the same region where both jar with consensus. That is itself the most useful finding: the parts of the case that two independent passes agree on (volume returning to a pruned base, no price, utilisation fading from 2028, cash as the gate, the engine off until leverage prints below 2.5x) are the predictable parts, and the parts they disagree on are small in EPS (SEK 0.05 to 0.15 each) but are exactly the lines the three brokers also disagree on. I find two internal inconsistencies that need correcting (one in the model, one in the second set's cash cross-check), and five points on which the new AlphaSense evidence lets me adjudicate or lean against an earlier answer.

Where the sets support each other, in the order the memo will use them:

  • External forces to the revenue line: the first set's "high-single-digit organic for two quarters, mid-single in 2027, low-to-mid in 2028" is the second set's 9, 5 and 3.5 percent, built on the same mechanism (trough comparatives, a two-to-four-quarter lag from starts, a Riksbank hiking cycle setting the ceiling, hiring as the binding constraint). The AlphaSense monitoring report adds the distributor confirmation (Rexel's Swedish same-day sales +4.8 percent in Q3 2026, Solar +6.5 percent organic, Onninen +12.9 percent in August) and the Konjunkturinstitutet upticks in September 2026, all consistent with the volume leg.
  • Internal forces to the margin line: the first set's "the company's own 2017 to 2021 history re-run under the same man at a slower pace than his urgency implies" is the second set's 6.9, 7.4, 7.7, 7.9, 8.0 percent with the 8 percent in FY2030, two years after consensus; both adopt the lead broker's Swedish path (7.2 percent in 2027, 7.6 percent in 2028) as the spine. Both attribute the recovery to utilisation, bad-debt normalisation, legacy roll-off and Fabri's mix, and both give price nothing.
  • Industry and competitive advantage to the ceiling: both sets cap the margin at the company's own history, not at SPIE's or Burkhalter's, because the moat is relational and carries no tender pricing power; the second set quantified what the first set asserted (price is 2.5 to 3 points of nominal growth and zero of margin; the 2026 relief is one-third own, two-thirds borrowed from data-centre capacity).
  • Economic strength to the chain below EBITA: both say consensus is most generous below EBITA; the second set turned the first set's "hold net financial items flat rather than falling" into SEK 225m, 220m, 210m against the brokers' 234m, 171m, 133m, which is the single largest reason our FY2028 EPS is 18 percent below consensus.
  • Management and risks to the gates: both treat the Q4 2026 cash release and the 2.5x leverage print as the events that restart the engine, both carry a one-in-three chance that cash conversion stays below 100 percent through 2027, both carry SEK 50m to 75m of recurring "non-recurring" items in the reported case, and both place the dilutive-raise tail at low-to-medium probability (the second set says under 15 percent over 18 months).
  • Guidance, sell-side and narrative to the fourth set: both say there is nothing in company guidance to build from, that the operating consensus lines are credible and the financing lines are not, and that the market has paid for the first flight of the staircase.

Where they jar, with my adjudication:

  1. The tax rate (first set 26 to 28 percent; second set 23 to 24 percent). I side with the second set. The lead broker publishes 21.0 percent for 2026 and 22.0 percent for 2027 (DNB Carnegie, 29 September 2026); H1 2026 printed about 20 percent; FY2025's 28.1 percent was built from SEK 31m of unrecognised losses, SEK 8m of non-deductibles and SEK 8m of utilised carryforwards on a 20.6 percent base. The second set's 23 to 24 percent already sits 1.5 to 2 points above the lead broker to carry Fabri's German profit at about 30 percent and a recurring level of unrecognised losses (the stock of such losses was SEK 149m at end-2025). The residual risk is that weak Swedish subsidiaries generate unrecognised losses again in a soft quarter, which would push a quarter's rate above 25 percent; it is a quarterly-volatility point, not a structural one.
  2. Amortisation of acquired intangibles (first set: the consensus depreciation line of SEK 402m, 358m, 348m shows it rolling off; second set: it rises with Fabri's purchase price allocation to SEK 175m to 185m). The new evidence sides with the first set's reading of the broker: DNB Carnegie's own acquisition-related amortisation line is SEK 130m in 2026, SEK 110m in 2027 and SEK 100m in 2028, falling even after Fabri (AlphaSense T-I2, citing the 29 September 2026 preview). The second set's rise is a hypothesis about Fabri's purchase price allocation, which is still preliminary. My view sits between: Instalco's recent allocations have identified a lower share of depreciable assets than its older deals (FY2025's amortisation fell SEK 36m for that reason), so Fabri's allocation plausibly adds SEK 30m to 50m a year rather than SEK 50m to 70m, while the Nordic book rolls off SEK 20m to 30m a year; the reported line in FY2027 and FY2028 is then about SEK 150m to 160m rather than SEK 175m to 180m. This is not a forecast input (the form's depreciation line excludes acquired intangibles, as adjusted EPS does), but it moves reported EPS, on which the 30 percent dividend policy is applied, up by about SEK 0.05 to 0.10 against the second set's SEK 1.80 and 2.10, and it narrows the reported-to-adjusted gap the market sees to about SEK 0.40 to 0.45.
  3. Minorities (second set SEK 70m, 125m, 135m for FY2026 to FY2028; the lead broker SEK 62m and 86m for 2026 and 2027). The second set reconstructed consensus as already deducting SEK 95m to 130m of minorities; the lead broker's published figure is SEK 86m for 2027, so about SEK 0.14 of our SEK 0.56 gap to the lead broker's FY2027 EPS is minorities rather than margin. I keep the second set's figure, because the evidence on the existing minorities is hard (the non-controlling-interest charge was SEK 22m in Q2 2026 alone, SEK 14m in Q1 2026 and SEK 7m in Q2 2025, and the balance rose from SEK 170m to SEK 235m in six months) and Fabri's 49 percent of about SEK 110m of net profit adds SEK 55m. But the Consumer should carry this as the line where the lead broker is lightest and where the H2 2026 prints decide (threshold: a minority charge above SEK 50m for H2 2026 confirms our run-rate; below SEK 35m confirms the broker's).
  4. The interest-rate sensitivity. The annual report puts a 100 basis point move at SEK 31m on profit after tax (AlphaSense own-3 and own-5, citing the 2025 annual report); the second set carried SEK 31m before tax. The pre-tax figure is therefore about SEK 40m, and the EPS sensitivity about SEK 0.11 per 100 basis points (4.5 percent of FY2027 EPS), a quarter larger than modelled. It is a sensitivity, not an input, and the second set's rate path (Riksbank +50 basis points by mid-2027 on the SEK book) already carries the hike, so I do not change the net financial items line; I do raise the weight of the rate path in the cone (D-04).
  5. Leverage definitions. The first set said the company's 2.6x excludes leases and earn-outs; the second set said net debt excluding leases over EBITDA before IFRS 16; the AlphaSense cash report says the company's interest-bearing net debt (SEK 3,561m) includes SEK 722m of leases and that adding earn-outs takes the lead broker's figure to 2.8x. The 2.6x ratio is arithmetically consistent only with the second set's reading (about SEK 2,840m excluding leases over about SEK 1,090m of EBITDA before IFRS 16), and the gearing figure the company reports (102.2 percent) uses the SEK 3,561m. So three definitions are in play: the company's KPI at 2.6x, the lead broker's with earn-outs at 2.8x, FactSet's with leases at 2.82x. The gate that matters (the 2.5x target, the acquisition restart) will be judged on the company's KPI as the company prints it; the Consumer should insist on that print and not re-derive it.
  6. The consensus margin against the lead broker's segment path. The second set's claim (that the group consensus margin and the lead broker's Swedish path cannot both be right unless the other segments average about 9.5 percent) is confirmed with a number. DNB Carnegie's published segment paths for 2027 (Sweden 7.2 percent, Norway 6.6 percent, Finland and Germany 9.8 percent, AlphaSense T-D1 and T-I1) sum to about 7.5 percent at group level after central costs, while its 2027 EBITDA (SEK 1,762m, 10.5 percent) less 2.6 points of depreciation implies about 7.9 percent. The 40 basis points, about SEK 65m, is either a lower depreciation ratio in the broker's model or an unreconciled gap; either way our 7.4 percent is 10 basis points below the lead broker's own segment arithmetic, not 50 basis points below its headline. The memo can say that on the operating lines we are within the broker's own error band and that the whole of our EPS gap is the chain below EBITA.
  7. The acquisition cash floor. The AlphaSense inventory of minorities and earn-outs shows that in FY2024 the group paid SEK 151m of contingent consideration and SEK 200m of minority buy-outs (SEK 351m), and in FY2025 SEK 131m and SEK 160m (SEK 291m); nearly all of FactSet's SEK 316m of FY2025 "acquisitions" was the settling of old obligations, not new deals. The second set carried SEK 70m for H2 2026 and SEK 150m for FY2027. With the maximum undiscounted contingent consideration at SEK 240m against a SEK 50m carrying value and a minority balance of SEK 235m before Fabri, I raise the floor; the input change is after this answer.
  8. The second set's cash cross-check against the model. The two differ by SEK 185m in FY2026 and by SEK 135m to 170m thereafter. The explanation and the one input change are after this answer.

Two smaller points where I lean against the earlier sets: the first set's "92 percent variable cost base" was already withdrawn by the second set (about 42 percent fully flexible, 47 percent semi-flexible); and the first set's reading that the Fabri majority step is "broadly leverage-neutral" is right on the ratio and wrong on the cash, as the second set said, because the step adds SEK 393m of debt now and SEK 600m to 650m of minority equity that is bought out in 2029 to 2033.

What the congruence means for predictability: the dependable core of the forecast is the part both sets built the same way from the same mechanism, and it is the operating line. The undependable part is the chain below EBITA, on which the two sets differ by SEK 0.10 to 0.15 of EPS and on which the three brokers differ by SEK 0.46 (FY2027 EPS SEK 2.55 to 3.01 on EBITDA of SEK 1,620m to 1,833m that is not even ranked the same way, see I-02). The Consumer should therefore treat revenue and adjusted EBITA as forecastable to within about 2 percent and 30 basis points respectively over 18 months, and adjusted EPS as forecastable only to within about 10 percent, with the error concentrated in financial items, minorities and tax.

The model summary's flagged gap: the cross-check free cash flow against the model's, and the model's own artefacts

The summary flags FY2026 (the Analyst's SEK 550m against the model's SEK 365m); the same pattern runs through every year (SEK 650m against 485m, 800m against 630m, 895m against 745m, 960m against 825m). I reconstruct the model's figure as net income after the grossed-up minority charge, plus depreciation, less capital spending, less lease payments, less the change in working capital at the input percentage; for FY2026 that is 558 + 390 − 110 − 300 − 170 = SEK 368m, within rounding of the model's 365. The Analyst's cross-check is operating cash flow after tax and interest (SEK 960m) less capital spending (110) less lease payments (300) = SEK 550m. The SEK 185m gap has three parts.

  1. Minorities, about SEK 70m in FY2026 and SEK 125m, 135m, 115m and 100m after. The Analyst folded the minority charge into net financial items, grossed up for tax, so the model's net income is attributable profit and its free cash flow is attributable free cash flow; the cross-check is group free cash flow before minorities. The model's treatment is the right one for a per-share valuation and for the dividend, and no input is wrong. The Consumer should know two things: the cash that actually leaves the group in the year is smaller than the charge (dividends to minority holders plus, later, the buy-outs that the acquisitions line carries), so the model's figure slightly understates the cash available for deleveraging within a year; and the charge and the later buy-outs are two views of the same leakage, so the model is mildly conservative on net debt over the five years, by perhaps SEK 100m to 150m cumulatively.
  2. Non-cash financial items, about SEK 45m in FY2026 (the SEK 33m unrealised currency loss booked in H1 2026 and part of the roughly SEK 40m of earn-out discounting and fees) and about SEK 35m to 40m a year after. The net financial items input carries them because the profit and loss needs them; the model treats the whole line as cash. No input is wrong; the model's free cash flow runs SEK 35m to 45m a year below the true cash figure on this point, and any cash-interest cover the model derives is slightly too tight.
  3. Working capital, about SEK 70m in FY2026 only. The 11.7 percent input, applied to revenue that includes four months of Fabri, generates a SEK 170m year-end build; the Analyst's own text expects an operating build of about SEK 100m and attributes the rest to Fabri's acquired balance, which "in the accounts arrives through the acquisition line". That is the point: the acquired balance is paid for inside the SEK 393m step-2 consideration that the acquisitions input already carries, so charging it again through the working-capital input double counts it. This is an input error of about SEK 70m, and I correct it by lowering the working-capital percentage so that the model's annual builds equal the operating builds the second set intended (about SEK 100m in FY2026, then SEK 170m, 105m, 110m and 140m), which means a path of 11.2, 11.0, 10.9, 10.8 and 10.8 percent instead of 11.7, 11.4, 11.3, 11.2 and 11.2. The balance-sheet working capital the model shows will then sit about SEK 70m below the balance the accounts will show; the cash flow, which is what the leverage gate and the return depend on, will be right. The change raises FY2026 attributable free cash flow from SEK 365m to about SEK 435m and leaves later years unchanged within SEK 10m.

Three further artefacts in the summary, none of which is an input:

  • The EPS basis break. FY2025 EPS of SEK 1.37 is the Viewforth definition (after amortisation of acquired intangibles, from FactSet's operating EBIT); FY2026 onward is the brokers' basis, before it. Hence the "+51.4 percent" FY2026 growth. The like-for-like growth is +18 percent from SEK 1.76, and the Consumer should re-base the earnings component of the 18-month and three-year returns before using them in topic #12; on the brokers' basis the earnings growth from FY2025 to the 18-month column (FY2027, SEK 2.45) is +39 percent, not the +48 percent the split shows, and to the three-year column (FY2029 on a June 2029 blend, about SEK 3.0) about +70 percent rather than +82 percent.
  • The EBIT comparison. "Viewforth against consensus +11.1, +4.0, +6.1 percent" compares our EBITA with consensus EBIT after amortisation. Adding about SEK 135m, 150m and 150m back to consensus EBIT gives consensus EBITA of about SEK 1,070m, 1,350m and 1,450m, against which our SEK 1,040m, 1,246m and 1,383m are 3, 8 and 5 percent below (2, 5 and 1 percent on the lead broker's lower amortisation of SEK 130m, 110m and 100m). The memo should not say we are above consensus on operating profit.
  • Year-end net debt. The model's end-FY2026 net debt of SEK 4,206m (its basis includes leases) is about SEK 480m more than the inputs' own arithmetic gives (end-2025 SEK 3,437m plus SEK 520m of acquisitions plus SEK 134m of dividends less SEK 365m of equity free cash flow is SEK 3,726m), and about SEK 390m more than the second set's text implies (SEK 3,100m excluding leases, about SEK 3,820m with them). The model may be adding lease-liability growth or Fabri's consolidated debt, or charging lease payments twice; the Financial Analyst should reconcile it before the leverage path or the three-year return is read, because on the model's figure leverage prints 2.9x at end-2026 and on the second set's 2.6 to 2.7x reported and about 2.5x pro forma. The guideline fair multiple of 19.93x two-year-forward in the return calculation is the dashboard's ten-year 90th percentile band; D-07 and the fourth set bear on whether a company-driven multiple with the engine off can carry it, and the memo Analyst's topic #11 view should start from that.
D-02

To what extent does past and present delivery make the expected change plausible, and how far does the thesis rely on strengths the business demonstrably has rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

C52

Question asked To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

Judgement: the change our model expects over 18 months (adjusted EBITA margin from 6.4 to 7.4 percent, cash conversion back to 100 percent, leverage below 2.5x during 2027) is plausible on strengths the business has demonstrated, because it is the first leg of a recovery the company has executed before and because four of its five building blocks are already visible in the numbers; the change the company and consensus expect beyond that (8 percent in FY2028, the acquisition engine at its old pace, Germany scaling at Nordic multiples) relies on three strengths the business has not demonstrated and would need to acquire: pricing at the tender stage, project controls that hold across 150 autonomous units in a downturn, and a German acquisition capability against private equity. The precedents say that decentralised installers that regained two to three points of margin did so in two to three years and that the deciders were controls, mix and indexation; Instalco has the mix, is building the controls and lacks the indexation. So: the first leg is well founded and the second is a hypothesis, and our model places the boundary between them in the right place.

What delivery has demonstrated, with the evidence that counts:

  • The margin mechanism has run before, in both directions. Adjusted EBITA above 8 percent every year from 2017 to 2021 with volumes rising and acquisitions running; 8.2 percent to 5.9 percent through 2022 to 2025 as revenue per employee fell 11 percent from its Q3 2023 peak while personnel cost per employee rose 18 percent since 2019. The recovery we model is that mechanism in reverse, and the first four quarters under the returning founder show it running: adjusted EBITA margin 6.0 percent (Q3 2025), 7.2 (Q4 2025), 5.8 (Q1 2026, seasonal), 7.1 (Q2 2026); organic growth from −3.6 to +8.4 percent; Swedish segment 7.0 percent in Q2 2026 against 6.6 a year earlier; backlog per employee the highest since Q1 2023. Two of the four quarters beat consensus at group level; the one miss (Q3 2025) was utilisation, not write-downs (the lead broker's own reading), which is the cyclical kind of miss, not the structural kind.
  • Three of the five building blocks are in the run-rate. Bad-debt normalisation: no new impairment in H1 2026, overdue receivables flat through the Q2 build, provisions back from the SEK 72m end-2024 peak; roughly two-thirds of the 40 basis points is already in H1 2026. The 2024 capacity cut (110 employees, 100 subcontractors, eight subsidiaries, SEK 30m of losses removed) is in the base. Fabri's margin is contractual and reported (12.7 percent in 2025), and the majority step is done. The fourth block, legacy roll-off, is management's dated qualitative commitment ("diminishing over the next several quarters", April 2026) and is unobservable from outside; the fifth, utilisation, is the only one that depends on the market continuing to supply volume.
  • Cash and leverage have been delivered before under this CFO: cash conversion 108 percent in FY2025, 112 percent at Q3 2025, 100 percent at Q1 2026; leverage from 3.3x to 2.6x in three quarters. The 90 percent reading at Q2 2026 is one quarter old and seasonal in shape; it is the one output that has gone the wrong way and it is the one tomorrow's print and February's print will decide.
  • The founder's playbook has worked elsewhere: Eltel has twelve consecutive quarters of year-on-year margin expansion to Q2 2026 under his chairmanship; Green Landscaping compounded at 26 percent a year at 4 to 6x. The same chairmanship also produced Green Landscaping's SEK 79m of write-downs and restructuring in Q3 2026 and 28 percent free-cash-flow conversion over 2020 to 2025, which is the honest other half of the record: his platforms deliver margin and growth and are late to discover project problems.

What the thesis would need the business to acquire, and whether the precedents say it must:

  • Tender pricing power. Not demonstrated in the last cycle (margin 8.2 to 5.9 percent through the inflation), not demonstrated now (management cannot separate price from volume across 6,000 projects; pricing sentiment in specialised construction only turned positive in late 2025; public buyers award on lowest price). The precedent that regained margin through pricing is SPIE, which has indexation in all contracts, daily pricing tools and 70 to 75 percent of revenue from servicing existing assets; Instalco has none of the three. Our model does not need this strength for 7.4 percent and does not carry it; consensus's 8 percent in FY2028 implicitly does, because it needs the Swedish segment above the lead broker's own 7.6 percent or the other segments above 9.5 percent.
  • Project controls across 150 autonomous units. Being built (the baseline completed in spring 2026, country COOs, quarterly internal-control reports, "fewer major negative project deviations"); not yet tested in a downturn, which is when Caverion (EUR 27m, 9m, 17m and 13m of write-downs in 2017 to 2020), Eltel, Instalco Norway (2018, 2021), southern Sweden (2024) and Green Landscaping (2026) discovered theirs. The precedents that succeeded (Caverion 2017 to 2022, Bravida Denmark 2024 to 2026, SPIE) each installed a standardised way of working and a central risk function; the two that failed in the cohort (Fasadgruppen, Green Landscaping) left subsidiaries unmonitored. Instalco 2.0 is the right design and is eighteen months old; the test it has not yet passed is a soft quarter with 43 percent of the book on fixed price and a wage round landing on it.
  • A German acquisition capability. Fabri bought 11 companies after November 2024 on the founders' own deal flow and at a structure priced at about 0.9x sales; the market it now competes in has had 104 technical-installation deals since 2023 (44 percent of all since 1995), with Brookfield (15 deals), VDK (six, plus the 51-company Builtech combination), SPIE (ROFA, SGS at a high-single-digit multiple), Eiffage and Nordic Climate Group bidding, and disclosed multiples of 7 to 9x EBITA against the Nordic 3 to 6x. This strength is not demonstrated at Instalco's level; it is demonstrated at Fabri's level on small local deals, which is the only level our model uses (10 percent a year of German bolt-on growth, no multiple arbitrage assumed).
  • Working-capital discipline during growth. Demonstrated in flat years (2024, 2025) and not in growth years (2021: 82 percent on FactSet's measure; Q2 2026: 90 percent rolling). The peers say the same (Bravida 77 percent in H1 2026 on data-centre advances; InstallatørGruppen 71 percent; Nimlas 78 percent for 2025). Our model needs 92 percent for FY2026 and about 100 percent in FY2027 to FY2028, which is the company's demonstrated range; it does not need SPIE's negative working capital.

The precedent count, because the question asks for it: of the nine named decentralised or multi-technical groups that lost two to three points of margin in a downturn, five regained them (SPIE, 90 basis points in two years; Caverion, five years to 4.5 percent from heavy write-downs, via services mix and 2,000 fewer project staff; Bravida Denmark, 0.1 to 5.1 percent in two years under a standard operating model; Nimlas Sweden, 6.1 to 7.9 percent in one year on 1 to 2 percent organic growth, the closest decentralised precedent and the most encouraging; Instalco itself, 2017 to 2021); two are mid-way (Eltel, twelve quarters of improvement and still under 5 percent; Bravida Sweden, restructuring in 2026); two failed on cash and leverage (Fasadgruppen, a SEK 504m rights issue; Green Landscaping, a 2026 transition year). The distinguishing features of the successes were a control system, a service or maintenance mix above 45 percent, and indexation or selectivity, in that order; volume returning helped all of them and decided none. Instalco has selectivity and a 37 percent service share, is installing the control system, and lacks indexation; that profile fits the "regained over two to three years" group better than the "failed" group, provided the balance sheet holds, which is why the first set's one-in-three on cash is the right weight to keep.

Implication for predictability: our 18-month forecast rests on demonstrated strengths to the extent of about three-quarters of its margin bridge (bad debt, capacity, Fabri mix, and the utilisation that the backlog already supports) and on management's word for the rest (legacy roll-off); consensus's 2028 rests on strengths not yet shown. The dates that test the acquired strengths are the Q1 2027 report (the first soft quarter under the baseline, with comparatives hardening) and the Q2 to Q3 2027 reports (the wage round on fixed-price work).

D-03

In relation to the differences between the forecasts (next 18 months to four years) and the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers?

❌ D44

Question asked In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?

Judgement: concentrated at the point that matters and diffuse in the way that hurts. On the revenue line the drivers are diffuse (Swedish organic volume, Norwegian public work, Finnish industrial work, Fabri's consolidation, a restrained Nordic restart) and can fail independently without sinking the forecast. On the EBITA line the drivers are concentrated: one segment (Sweden, 71 percent of sales before Fabri) and one mechanism (volume returning to a labour base pruned in 2024) produce about 45 percent of the SEK 510m EBITA uplift from FY2025 to FY2028, and that mechanism depends on one external variable, Swedish starts into a Riksbank hiking cycle. On the EPS line the drivers are diffuse in the worst sense: four below-EBITA lines (financial items, tax, minorities, amortisation on the reported line) that are each small, each independent of the operating recovery, and each able to disappoint relative to consensus without any of the others compensating. The difference from the last six years is the mirror image: 2019 to 2025 was growth bought with debt and equity on a declining margin, driven by one engine (acquisitions, SEK 800m a year) that is now off; 2026 to 2030 is margin and mix on slower growth, with the engine returning at 60 percent of its old pace from H2 2027.

The bridge, and what each piece depends on (SEK millions of adjusted EBITA, FY2025 to FY2028, from the second set's build; the total uplift is SEK 875m to 1,383m):

  1. Swedish utilisation, about SEK 90m (60 basis points), plus Swedish organic volume at the segment margin, about SEK 120m: together roughly 40 to 45 percent of the uplift. Depends on Swedish starts (Byggstartsindikatorn +8.0 percent in July 2026, non-residential +13.2 percent), on the two-to-four-quarter lag running as it has in past cycles, on hiring lagging backlog (backlog per employee SEK 1.74m at Q2 2026), and on the Riksbank path (a November 2026 hike to 2.00 percent and February 2027 to 2.25 percent is the Nordea and Morgan Stanley call; Deutsche Bank has 2.50 percent by mid-2027). One macro variable, one segment, one mechanism. This is the concentration.
  2. Bad-debt normalisation, about SEK 55m (40 basis points): depends on no second Northvolt or Serneke (construction companies 48 percent of sales; Swedish construction bankruptcies at the prior year's high in mid-2026). Independent of (1) in good times, correlated with it in a stall.
  3. Legacy roll-off, about SEK 45m (30 basis points): depends on management's April 2026 statement and on no new low-priced work having been signed in 2026, which cannot be observed (award margins and the fixed-price share of backlog are not disclosed).
  4. Fabri's mix, about SEK 180m of consolidated EBITA at 11.5 percent on SEK 1.6bn, of which SEK 90m is attributable after the 49 percent leak: depends on German demand (construction capacity utilisation 68 percent in August 2026, residential permits just turning positive, non-residential permits down 6 percent in July, the EUR 500bn fund running about 20 percent below plan on Morgan Stanley's reading), on founder retention under majority ownership, and on bolt-on pricing against private equity. Independent of the Nordic drivers; concentrated in one platform of 23 subsidiaries reported with a one-month lag inside a merged segment.
  5. The wage round, about −SEK 70m net (−40 basis points) in FY2027: depends on the spring 2027 electrical and plumbing settlements (the Riksbank and Nordea expect 3.3 percent wage growth; SEB's 8 percent is for negotiated minimums), on the undisclosed fixed-price share of the 2026 backlog and on the undisclosed share with indexation. Independent of the others and dated.
  6. Norway and Finland, net about zero; the Nordic bolt-on restart, about SEK 35m a year from FY2028: depends on the Q4 2026 cash release and the leverage print, so correlated with (1) through cash.

Below EBITA (the EPS bridge against consensus rather than against history): net financial items flat at SEK 210m to 225m against the brokers' path to SEK 133m (SEK 0.22 to 0.25 of FY2028 EPS); tax 23 to 24 percent against the lead broker's 21 to 22 (SEK 0.06 to 0.08); minorities SEK 125m to 135m against the lead broker's 86m (SEK 0.14); amortisation on the reported line only. Each depends on a different thing (the Riksbank and the facility's undisclosed fixed-floating mix and margin grid; the mix of German and loss-making Swedish profit; Fabri's net profit and the existing minority pool; the purchase price allocation), none depends on the operating recovery, and the only one that can surprise favourably against consensus is tax. This is the diffuse-and-skewed part: no single below-EBITA item is large, but the four of them sum to the whole of our 18 percent gap to FY2028 consensus EPS.

How the concentration compares with the last four to six years: in 2019 to 2023 one driver, acquisitions at SEK 800m a year, produced 25 to 36 percent revenue growth and all of the EBITA growth in money, while margin fell every year from 2021; the forecast error in those years was almost entirely on the margin, which the brokers kept above 8 percent until it was 6 percent, and on leverage, which reached 3.3x against a 2.5x target. The next four years reverse it: revenue growth of 10.7, 12.0 and 6.7 percent is mostly Fabri and recovery rather than an engine, the EBITA growth is mostly margin, and the forecast error is most likely to sit below EBITA and in cash rather than in the margin, because the margin is being modelled a year slower than the founder and half a year slower than the brokers.

Dispersion evidence for this cohort at this point of a recovery, which the question asks for: the sell-side's own record on Instalco says the 18-month consensus EPS cone has been about plus or minus 30 percent (FY2026 consensus ranged SEK 2.07 to 3.24 over twelve months; FY2025 SEK 1.51 to 2.69), and the events that widened it were the ones listed above in exactly this order: utilisation misses (Q3 2025, an 11 percent cut), customer insolvencies (Q1 2025, SEK 64m), leverage (mid-2025, the target from SEK 50 to 40), working-capital reversals (Q2 2026, cash well below). Rate rises and wage rounds have not yet widened it, because the last three years had falling rates and the 2023 and 2025 wage rounds landed on a shrinking book; both are ahead of us, which is why the cone in D-04 is wider than the second set's and why its lower half is fatter.

What would make the drivers less concentrated: a Swedish segment margin printed above 7.5 percent in a quarter in which organic growth is below 5 percent (price or mix, not volume); a disclosed fixed-price share of backlog below 40 percent with indexation on long contracts; a first Nordic bolt-on in H1 2027, which adds a second margin driver; Fabri printing above 11 percent on its own for two quarters inside the merged segment.

D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

❌ D38

Judgement: wide for an 18-month horizon and negatively skewed against consensus, roughly symmetric around our own base on the operating lines and negatively skewed below EBITA. For FY2027 adjusted EPS I put the cone at SEK 1.70 to 3.00 around a base of SEK 2.45, with about 30 percent of the probability below SEK 2.20, 45 percent between SEK 2.20 and 2.70, and 25 percent above SEK 2.70; against the consensus SEK 3.01 the whole of the cone except its top decile sits below. For FY2026, three-quarters of which is already reported or in the comparatives, the cone is SEK 1.95 to 2.25 around SEK 2.08, and the only thing that moves it is today's and February's below-EBITA lines. The case is therefore predictable in direction (margin up, leverage down, volume up: I hold that at 70 to 75 percent), moderately predictable on the operating lines (adjusted EBITA within about 7 percent of our base at two-thirds probability), and poorly predictable on EPS and cash, where the error is not symmetric. The practical meaning for the memo: the forecasts are more likely to be met on revenue and EBITA than the market's are, and consensus EPS is more likely to be cut than met.

How I build the cone, and why it is wider than the second set's SEK 1.80 to 3.00:

  • The operating half. Organic growth of 5 percent in FY2027 against a range of 1 to 8 percent; Swedish margin of 7.2 percent against 6.5 to 7.8; incremental margin on organic revenue of 12 percent against 8 to 15. The two-point swing in Swedish organic growth is worth about SEK 18m of EBITA, 1.5 percent of EPS; the swing in the Swedish margin is worth about SEK 75m, 6 percent of EPS. So the operating cone on FY2027 EPS is roughly plus or minus SEK 0.25 around SEK 2.45, and it is close to symmetric: the upside case (Sweden prints 7.5 percent-plus in Q4 2026 and the Q4 cash release is normal) is as plausible as the downside (a stall in starts into the hikes shows in H2 2027 with a two-quarter lag). I widen the second set's lower bound to SEK 1.70 because the two events it did not weight, a rate rise of 100 basis points (SEK 0.11 of EPS on the annual report's after-tax sensitivity, not the SEK 0.09 modelled) and a wage round at the SEB minimum applied broadly (up to 100 basis points before offsets, SEK 0.25 of EPS), are both dated inside the window and both more likely than not to land in part.
  • The below-EBITA half. Financial items SEK 200m to 260m (consensus 171m); tax 21 to 26 percent; minorities SEK 90m to 150m; share count 269m to 271m. The combinations are worth about SEK 0.30 of EPS from the top to the bottom of their ranges, and relative to consensus only one of the four (tax) can surprise favourably. This is the skew: around our base the below-EBITA cone is about −SEK 0.20 to +SEK 0.10; around consensus it is −SEK 0.60 to +SEK 0.05.
  • The tails. Downside: a SEK 100m to 150m write-down cluster or a second customer insolvency (5 to 15 percent of a year's EBITA, 15 to 40 percent of a quarter's) with cash conversion below 90 percent at Q4 2026 would put FY2027 EPS at SEK 1.70 to 1.90 and open the dilutive-raise branch; I put it at 10 to 12 percent, consistent with the second set's "under 15 percent" and the first set's "low-to-medium". Upside: the founder's urgency proving right, 8 percent in 2027 with falling interest and a 22 percent tax rate, gives SEK 2.95 to 3.00, which is consensus; 10 percent.
  • The lead broker's own scenarios bracket the same cone from the outside: bear 0 percent organic growth and a 6.5 percent margin, bull 8 percent organic growth and an 8.0 percent margin; the market multiple they attach (9x and 15x EV/EBITA) says the price cone is wider than the earnings cone, which is D-07's point.

Why the skew is negative against consensus and how the Consumer should read it: consensus FY2027 EPS of SEK 3.01 is a two-of-three number. Nordea has SEK 3.01 on the lowest revenue and EBITDA of the three (SEK 16,414m and 1,620m) and Pareto SEK 2.55 on the highest (SEK 17,348m and 1,833m), so the EPS ranking of the brokers is the inverse of their operating ranking, which can only be true if the chain below EBITA is modelled very differently (or on different bases) by each house. The median therefore sits where two houses with light below-EBITA chains sit, and the broker whose chain resembles ours (Pareto, SEK 2.55) is within 4 percent of our base. The cone around consensus is not symmetric because consensus is not the centre of the brokers' own distribution of operating assumptions; it is the top of their below-EBITA distribution.

Predictability, stated in the terms the memo needs: revenue for FY2027 within plus or minus 3 percent of SEK 16.9bn at about 70 percent; adjusted EBITA within plus or minus 7 percent of SEK 1.25bn at about 65 percent; adjusted EPS within plus or minus 10 percent of SEK 2.45 at about 50 percent; cash conversion at or above 100 percent for FY2027 at about 50 percent; company leverage below 2.5x by the Q2 2027 report at about 60 percent. The width comes from the structure of the business (150 units, 43 percent fixed price, percentage-of-completion accounting, no award-margin disclosure); the skew comes from the regime (hiking cycle, wage round) and from consensus having been built from the segment optimism upward rather than from an authored path.

The events that narrow the cone, in order: today's Swedish segment margin (at or above 7.5 percent with organic growth near 12 percent moves our FY2027 margin to 7.6 percent and shifts the cone up by about SEK 0.10; below 7.0 percent moves it to 7.1 percent and shifts it down by the same), today's and February's cash and receivables (SEK 200m of operating cash flow and receivables under SEK 1,800m take the write-down and raise tails from 12 to about 6 percent), the February 2027 leverage print on both definitions and the dividend proposal (SEK 0.70 confirms the cash leg), the spring 2027 settlements, and Fabri's first full quarter.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and the key drivers of the thesis for monitoring the case over about the next six months?

❌ D47

Question asked How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

Judgement: helpful for the drivers the market watches and unhelpful for the drivers that decide our variance. What the company reports each quarter (organic, acquired and currency growth by segment; backlog with organic and currency splits; segment EBITA; rolling cash conversion on its own definition; leverage on its own definition; the minority charge; contract assets and liabilities; receivables; the components of net financial items) is enough to track volume, the Swedish margin, cash and the leverage gate, which are the four things the next two prints will be judged on. What it does not report (backlog duration and its fixed-price share; award margins; subsidiary-level write-downs until they become closures; revenue per employee as a series; the effect of the ABC prioritisation; Fabri's standalone figures, now inside a merged Finland and Germany segment with a one-month lag; the facility's fixed-floating mix, margin grid and covenant definitions; the Fabri option hurdles and formulas) is the list of things that decide whether 7.4 percent becomes 8 percent and whether EPS reaches consensus. The external series fill about half the gap, and the lead broker's monitoring technique (backlog per employee) fills some of the rest. Net: the Consumer can monitor the case's direction well and its second derivative poorly, which matches the predictability profile in D-04.

What can be monitored, where, and with what lead, over October 2026 to April 2027:

  • Volume and utilisation. The company's organic growth by segment and backlog per employee (derivable from the reported backlog and headcount; the lead broker uses it as the proxy for award margins and utilisation) are the best internal series. Externally, Byggstartsindikatorn (monthly; +8.0 percent year on year in July 2026) leads installation revenue by two to four quarters; the distributor reports (Rexel's Swedish same-day sales +4.8 percent in Q3 2026; Solar +6.5 percent organic in Q2 with double-digit Swedish installation growth; Onninen +12.9 percent comparable in August) lead invoicing by one to two quarters because installers buy materials shortly before starting; Konjunkturinstitutet's builder confidence, order-book and price expectations (all up in September 2026) lead pricing by a quarter or two. Bravida's quarterly commentary on Swedish pricing, published the same morning, is the sector read. This leg is well covered.
  • Pricing. Not reported by the company and not separable by it; the only proxies are Konjunkturinstitutet's price expectations, wholesaler commentary (Brødrene A&O Johansen reported fiercer-than-expected project price competition in Denmark and Sweden in October 2025; Rexel passed 3.6 percent of price in Q2 2026) and Bravida's and Assemblin Caverion's statements. A Swedish segment margin above 7.5 percent in a quarter with organic growth under 5 percent is the only internal signal of price, and no such quarter is due before Q1 2027.
  • Cash. Reported quarterly: operating cash flow, receivables, contract assets and liabilities, rolling conversion. The thresholds stand (today: operating cash flow toward SEK 200m, receivables under SEK 1,800m, contract assets not above SEK 800m; February: conversion at or above 100 percent, a Q4 release of the Q4 2025 order of size). The weakness is definitional: the company's conversion is operating cash flow over EBITDA, not over net income, and its leverage KPI is one of three in use (D-01), so the Consumer should take the company's own print and not a derived one.
  • Costs. Employee costs as a share of sales (reported) will show the wage round from Q2 2027; the settlements themselves (electrical and plumbing agreements, spring 2027) are public when signed and are the single most important external event in the window after the Riksbank's November decision. The fixed-price share is annual (43 percent in the 2025 annual report) and the indexed share is not disclosed at all, so the wage round's margin effect cannot be estimated from disclosure; it can only be read after the fact.
  • Germany. Fabri reports one month in Q3 2026 and three in Q4, inside a segment with Finland. The lead broker expects the merged segment at 9.5 percent in Q3 and 9.8 percent for 2026 and 2027; a segment margin below 9 percent with Finland flat would say Fabri is under 11 percent, which is the threshold for our 11.5 percent. Backlog (EUR 69m at end-Q1 2026) may be given; cash conversion and net debt of Fabri will not be. The minority charge in the income statement is the cleanest external read on Fabri's net profit (49 percent of it), which is a useful inversion the Consumer can use.
  • Financial items. Reported with components (external interest, lease interest, other, currency), which is better than most peers; the structure (floating STIBOR-linked base, a policy of at least 50 percent of net debt on short interest terms, no swaps disclosed, a leverage-linked margin step-up whose grid is undisclosed) means the Riksbank decision on 20 November 2026 maps to the line with a lag of one to two quarters at about SEK 10m a quarter per 100 basis points.
  • Minorities and options. The minority charge is reported quarterly; the balance, the contingent consideration (carrying value against maximum) and the minority buy-outs are reported half-yearly or annually; the Fabri hurdles and formulas are not reported and will not be before the 2026 annual report in March 2027, if then.

How this compares with peers: Bravida gives a sensitivity table for interest rates and a service-installation split; SPIE gives annual numerical guidance and hedging detail; InstallatørGruppen gives a pro forma organic growth outlook and reiterates it; Instalco gives none of these but gives restated segment history promptly and the cleanest organic-acquired-currency split in the cohort. The company's disclosure is therefore average for the cohort on the operating lines and below average on the lines where our view differs from consensus, which is a structural reason the below-EBITA gap can persist in consensus for several quarters: nobody can see it until it prints.

For monitoring the next six months the Consumer should run five series: Swedish segment margin and organic growth (quarterly); backlog per employee (quarterly); operating cash flow, receivables and contract assets (quarterly, read against the seasonal pattern of a Q2 to Q3 build and a Q4 release); the minority charge and net financial items by component (quarterly); and the three external leads (Byggstartsindikatorn monthly, the distributor prints, Bravida's Swedish commentary). The gaps that no series fills are award margins, the fixed-price and indexed shares, and Fabri standalone; the research request on the wage round in requests.md is aimed at the second.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines, and through to EPS; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to EPS?

C50

Question asked How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

Judgement: the structure of the transmission is clear and most of its links are observable; two links in the middle are opaque and they are the links that carry the thesis. What the company says it will achieve (an EBITA margin of at least 8 percent, growth of at least 10 percent over a cycle, leverage not above 2.5x, cash conversion of 100 percent, a dividend of about 30 percent) traces as follows: the margin into the Swedish segment (reported), inside which sit utilisation (observable through revenue per employee and backlog per employee), bad debt (observable through other external costs and the provision), legacy roll-off (not observable) and award margins (not observable), plus Fabri's mix (half observable, inside a merged segment); the growth target into organic growth by segment (reported) and acquired growth (reported, with the acquisitions line); leverage and cash into KPIs the company defines itself. From EBITA to EPS the chain is fully disclosed in components (depreciation, amortisation of acquired intangibles, external and lease interest, other financial items, currency, tax, minorities) but not forecastable from disclosure, because the facility terms, the tax mix and the Fabri economics are undisclosed. The external transmission is clear at the demand end (starts to installation revenue with a two-to-four-quarter lag; distributors to invoicing with a one-to-two-quarter lead) and clear at the financing end (the Riksbank to interest with a one-to-two-quarter lag), and opaque at the pricing link in the middle (competitors' capacity to tender prices to award margins), which is the link the whole industry's margin runs through and which nobody, including the company, can measure.

The internal chain, link by link, with the observability of each:

  1. Target to segment. The 8 percent group margin is about 7.6 to 7.9 percent in Sweden plus 6 to 7 percent in Norway plus 9.5 to 10 percent in Finland and Germany, on the lead broker's and our arithmetic. Sweden's segment margin is reported quarterly with restated history; this link is clear and the market reads it as the thesis (D-05, I-03).
  2. Segment margin to its drivers. Utilisation is the largest driver and the most observable (headcount and revenue are reported; the incremental margin on organic revenue can be computed each quarter, 13.5 percent at group level and 10.4 percent in Sweden in Q2 2026); bad debt is observable in other external costs (8.5 to 9.0 percent of sales in late 2024 and 2025 against 7.5 to 8.0 before) and in the provision; legacy roll-off is a management statement with a stated timing and no number; award margins and the ABC programme's effect are not reported. Half the margin bridge is therefore visible and half is taken on trust until it shows in the segment line; the memo should say so.
  3. Drivers to cost lines. Employee costs (36.8 percent of sales in FY2025) carry utilisation and the wage round; other external costs carry bad debt and the country-organisation cost; materials and purchased services (46.9 percent) carry input prices through the 57 percent cost-plus and 43 percent fixed-price mix. All three are reported annually and the first two quarterly in substance; the fixed-price and indexed shares are not.
  4. EBITA to EPS. Depreciation (about 2.6 percent of sales on the brokers' basis), amortisation of acquired intangibles (reported quarterly, SEK 63m in H1 2026), external loan interest (SEK 29m in Q2 2026, falling with rates in 2025 to 2026 and about to rise), lease interest (about SEK 7m a quarter), other financial items (earn-out discounting, fees), currency on intra-group loans (SEK −33m in H1 2026, non-cash and unforecastable), tax (20 percent in H1 2026, 28.1 percent in FY2025), minorities (SEK 36m in H1 2026). Every component is disclosed after the fact; three of them (interest, tax, minorities) cannot be forecast from disclosure within about SEK 0.10 of EPS each because their drivers (the facility's fixed-floating mix and margin grid, the tax mix by subsidiary, Fabri's net profit) are not given. This is why the chain below EBITA is where the brokers disagree most (D-04) and where the narrative is thinnest (I-03).
  5. EPS to the dividend and the balance sheet. Clear: 30 percent of reported net profit, applied with discretion (39 percent in FY2025); leverage on the company's KPI; both reported.

The external chain, with the strength of each link:

  • Demand: construction starts (Byggstartsindikatorn) to installation revenue, lag two to four quarters, well evidenced across cycles; public budgets (defence, health, prisons, schools) to the public share of backlog (Norway +40.9 percent in Q2 2026 on public infrastructure), firm and dated; the renovation directive to renovation volumes, slow and beyond 2027. Clear.
  • Pricing: competitors' capacity (Bravida's SEK 7bn-plus of data-centre backlog absorbing technicians; distressed small firms exiting) to tender prices to award margins. The direction is agreed by every source (the lead broker's "second-order effects from a tighter subcontracting market", the company's "stabilised the pricing environment"), the magnitude is unmeasurable (no award-margin disclosure, no national bidder-count series; the fifteen-bidder figure is a southern-Swedish and public-tender symptom), and the reversibility (data-centre slippage returning capacity within two quarters) is unarticulated. Opaque, and it carries the difference between 7.4 and 8 percent.
  • Costs: the wage round to employee costs (clear in timing, unclear in magnitude for the reasons in D-05); metals and wholesaler pricing to materials (visible in distributor and OEM reports; neutral now).
  • Financing: the Riksbank and Norges Bank to net financial items (clear: floating base, SEK 31m after tax per 100 basis points per the annual report, one-to-two-quarter lag) and to starts (clear in sign, unquantified in elasticity; the AlphaSense rates report could not find a demand elasticity for Nordic project starts).
  • Valuation: not part of EPS, but the external transmission from Bravida's narrative and the Swedish small-cap flow to Instalco's multiple is in D-07.

The three milestones the Consumer can hold the company to, because they are both stated by the company and observable: Swedish legacy contracts "diminishing over the next several quarters" from April 2026 (test: the Swedish margin in Q1 2027, the seasonally weak quarter, printing above Q1 2026's 5.0 percent by at least 70 basis points); the Q2 2026 cash shortfall being timing with overdue receivables unchanged (test: today's and February's receivables and conversion); margin before acquisitions (test: no Nordic bolt-on announced before the 2.5x print, and one within two quarters after it). Milestones the company has not given and the memo should not invent: a date for 8 percent, a restructuring number, a Fabri bolt-on budget.

D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (rolling six months), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

C52

Question asked To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

Judgement: the daily price movement is overwhelmingly idiosyncratic and the multiple is company-driven. Over the latest six-month window (27 April to 22 October 2026) the equal-weighted basket of the ten dashboard peers explains 12 percent of Instalco's daily variance (correlation 0.35, beta 0.80), and over the last 24 month-ends the explained share has ranged between 8 and 31 percent with a median of 13; the stock returned +19.1 percent in the window against the basket's 0.0 percent. No single peer explains more than 13 percent (Bravida, correlation 0.35), and the European multi-technical names explain nothing (Burkhalter 0 percent, Arcadis 0 percent, Ashtead 0 percent). The multiple's own five-year range (7.86x to 34.13x 24-month-forward P/E; today 12.7 to 12.9x) and the lead broker's own framing (a 21 percent average premium to European peers over eight years that became a 65 percent discount in June 2025 and a 35 percent discount in October 2025, then closed) say the same thing: the multiple is set by what the market believes about this company's leverage, its founder and whether its acquisition engine is on, not by the sector's multiple. The qualification is that the level over a year has tracked the one peer that shares its market: Instalco +63 percent and Bravida +61 percent over twelve months, +59 and +62 percent year to date, both on Swedish recovery, both re-rated ahead of earnings; the daily paths are different because the catalysts are different (Bravida's data-centre orders, Instalco's founder and cash prints). So: the direction of the re-rating was sector-and-country-driven; its size, its timing and its vulnerability are company-driven.

What the co-movement table shows, read for what it means:

  • The idiosyncratic share has been 69 to 92 percent in every window for two years. The two windows where the basket explained more (26 percent to January 2026, 31 percent to August 2026) coincide with sector-wide moves (the Nordic installer re-rating around the Q4 2025 prints; the summer 2026 data-centre narrative after Bravida's Q2) and the share fell back to 12 percent within two months each time. The stock joins the sector on sector news and leaves it on its own news.
  • Beta to the basket of 0.80 with a correlation of 0.35 means the stock moves less than the sector on sector days and far more on its own days; the price's variance is about three times what the sector would give it. For a position, the sector hedge removes little of the risk; the risk is the prints.
  • Bravida is the only peer with a meaningful correlation (0.35) and it is the anchor the market uses for the "compounder" multiple branch (16.5x P/E, 71st percentile of its own history, pinned valuation D on the dashboard against Instalco's C at the 46th percentile). The first set's perception risk (a Bravida de-rating on data-centre principal-contractor risk or on the artificial-intelligence financing crack removing the 16x branch) remains the one sector channel into Instalco's multiple; the co-movement data say it would act through sentiment and the valuation anchor rather than through daily correlation.

What set the multiple, event by event, from the AlphaSense valuation report and the dashboard: the Q3 2025 miss and the mid-2025 leverage spike (the target cut from SEK 50 to 40, a 50 percent fall in the share over the preceding twelve months to a 65 percent discount against European peers, the multiple at its 7.86x low in early 2026); the founder's return on 1 August 2025 and the Q4 2025 beat (the "founder back with a bang" re-rating, +63 percent over twelve months); the Q2 2026 beat (the stock at the 90th percentile of its two-year multiple range); the Fabri majority step (a positive, read as the M&A runway); Nordea's move to Hold on 19 October 2026 (the first dissent, as yet without a visible price effect in the data). None of these is a sector event. The lead broker's own valuation language ("engine-off" at 10 to 12x EV/EBITA when leverage is above target and the acquisition pace is paused, "engine-on" at 13 to 14.5x when it resumes; the historical average 14.0 to 14.5x; the stock at about 13x on 2027 earnings today) is the clearest statement that the multiple is company-driven: it is a function of leverage and deal flow, two variables the company controls, rather than of the sector's growth.

What this means for predictability and for the memo's multiple (topics #10 and #11):

  • Favourable: a company-driven multiple transmits our fundamental view to the price. If the cash release, the leverage print and a first bolt-on arrive on our timetable (H1 2027), the multiple can move from the engine-off band to the engine-on band without any help from the sector; the stock does not need Bravida or the Swedish small-cap index to do anything.
  • Unfavourable: the same mechanism makes the multiple hostage to the least predictable parts of the forecast (cash, write-downs, the lead broker's call) and gives it a five-year range of 4x, which is wider than any sector range. The stock has already moved from the bottom of that range to its own ten-year median (46th percentile) on the first leg of delivery; it sits at the 90th percentile of its two-year range. The "mania" band (19.9x, the ten-year 90th percentile) that the model uses as the guideline fair multiple was reached only in the engine-on, low-rate years of 2020 to 2021, when the multiple ran from 20x to its 34x peak; with the engine at 60 percent of its old pace from H2 2027 and the Riksbank hiking, I would treat the "defendable" band (16.5x, the 75th percentile) as the top of the plausible range for the 18-month horizon and the "prudent" median (13.3x) as the centre, and the memo Analyst should say so in topic #11. On our FY2029 EPS of SEK 3.22, those bands give SEK 43 to 53 against SEK 42.52 today, before dividends.
  • Second-order: the stock's own multiple was 18x on the lead broker's 2026 EPS and 13.6x on 2027 against Bravida's 20.8x and 16.4x and SPIE's 14.0x and 12.5x (DNB Carnegie and Bernstein, September and October 2026); the discount to Bravida is about 15 percent and is explained by service mix, scale and leverage. If Bravida de-rates, the peer "anchor" argument weakens but Instalco's absolute level is set by its own delivery; if Bravida continues to re-rate on data centres, the sector argument gives Instalco nothing because it has no data-centre exposure.

For the Consumer's monitoring: the rolling six-month R-squared is a useful tell in itself. A rise above 25 to 30 percent (as in January and August 2026) says the stock is trading on the sector narrative and the next company print will be judged against sector expectations; a fall toward 10 percent says the next print is the whole story.

Back to contents

Layer 4: Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

The Investment Analyst's heading The fourth set: Assessing the extent to which our assessment is reflected in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative

The fourth set as a whole ❌ D37 written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The market has priced the part of our assessment that is predictable and has not priced the part that is not, and the gap between the two works against the case over the next 18 months and for it beyond. Company guidance is five undated targets and a dividend; it will be reiterated at every event, raised only by an act the founder has declined so far (a date, a capital markets day, a deal) and only after the February 2027 cash and leverage print, and reduced only by a write-down; the one number that moves is the dividend, most likely to SEK 0.60 to 0.70. Sell-side consensus has the operating recovery about right (revenue and EBITDA within 2 to 5 percent of our base, the margin at or a little above the lead broker's own segment arithmetic) and the chain below EBITA wrong in the one direction it can be wrong, so that FY2027 EPS of SEK 3.01, a two-of-three median built on a financial line that assumes rate cuts in a hiking cycle and a minority charge below the H1 2026 run-rate, is more likely to be cut by 10 to 15 percent than held, with the cuts landing at the Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 reports while sales, EBITDA and targets are held and the buy share drifts from 86 percent toward 60 percent on a second Hold. The narrative, written by one house and read by a register that watches the Swedish margin, the backlog and the founder, articulates well the dynamics we also believe in and not at all the four that produce our variance (the utilisation fade, the wage round on un-indexed fixed-price work, the reversibility of borrowed pricing relief, the below-EBITA chain), so the next negative surprises arrive without a frame. The favourable side of the gap is real but later: nobody carries the acquisition restart in a revenue line, nobody carries the balance sheet at 1.9x by 2029, and the multiple is company-driven, so if the February 2027 print passes the cash and leverage gates the re-rating from the engine-off band to the engine-on band needs no help from the sector. For the memo: the case for the next six months is "operating beats, EPS cuts, guidance unchanged, narrative intact"; for months six to eighteen it is "the below-EBITA cuts complete, the wage round prints, the restart begins"; and the stock's phase is turning-up-to-broadening with the broadening conditional on February. Richard should read the current sell-side momentum rating of A and the 24 percent rise in target prices as priced delivery, not as unrecognised upside, and should look for the unrecognised upside in 2028 and beyond.

Guidance

1 question
I-0144
I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

❌ D44

Question asked How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

Judgement: Instalco gives no annual guidance, so "guidance" over the next six reports (Q3 2026 today; Q4 2026 on 11 February 2027; Q1 2027 on 28 April; Q2 2027 in July; Q3 2027 in October; Q4 2027 in February 2028) means four things: the five through-cycle targets, the dividend proposal, the qualitative statements on margin, cash and acquisitions, and any formal event (a dated 8 percent commitment or a capital markets day). On that definition the most likely outcome at every one of the six events is that the targets are reiterated unchanged and met only in part: I put "reiterated and partly met" at about 70 percent per event, a formal raising (a dated 8 percent, a capital markets day, or a restart announcement framed as a return to the 10 percent growth target) at about 20 percent cumulatively over the 18 months, most plausibly at or after the Q4 2026 report if the Swedish margin has printed above 7.5 percent and the cash release is normal, and a reduction (a second dividend cut, a profit warning, a quiet re-wording of the 8 percent as a through-cycle average) at about 10 percent cumulatively, concentrated at the Q1 and Q2 2027 reports when the comparatives harden and the wage round lands. The dividend is the only numerical guidance and it is the one I expect to move: SEK 0.60 to 0.70 for FY2026 against SEK 0.50 (SEK 0.70 at 40 percent, SEK 0.60 at 45 percent, SEK 0.50 at 15 percent), consensus at SEK 0.70. Secondarily, over 18 months to three years: the 8 percent target will be kept (it is the founder's exit condition) and reached in FY2028 on consensus, in FY2030 on our base, so "met late" at about 55 percent, "met on consensus's date" at about 25 percent, and "de-emphasised" at about 20 percent; the 10 percent growth target will not be met organically and will be reinterpreted to include Germany; raised targets (for instance a margin above 8 percent with Fabri in the mix) are possible only from 2028 and I put them at about 15 percent.

What each outcome would need, event by event:

  • Today, Q3 2026. The sell-side's thresholds are the lead broker's: about 10 percent group organic growth (12 percent in Sweden) on the −3.6 percent comparative, a 90 basis point year-on-year rise in the adjusted EBITA margin to about 6.8 percent (SEK 226m of EBITA), operating cash flow recovering toward SEK 200m, leverage toward 2.5x, Fabri's first month above 10 percent inside a merged segment at about 9.5 percent. "Met" is a print at or above those on the operating lines with a seasonal cash build explained as timing; it does not change guidance. "Raised" would be management dating the 8 percent or naming a restart; I put that at under 10 percent today, because the founder has refused to time the target at every call since October 2025 and the cash leg has not yet been shown. "Reduced" would be a Swedish margin below 7.0 percent or a write-down; one in four to one in three on the first set's reading of the price's sensitivity, but even then the targets stay, which is the point: this company does not reduce guidance, it misses it.
  • February 2027, Q4 2026. The decisive event. Needs for "raised": rolling cash conversion at or above 100 percent, a Q4 working-capital release of the order of Q4 2025's SEK 451m, company leverage at or below 2.5x on at least the pro forma basis, a dividend proposal of SEK 0.70 or more, and a statement that acquisitions resume. If all four arrive, the probability of a capital markets day or a dated plan during 2027 rises to about 40 percent, because the founder's commitment to stay until 8 percent is "sustainably" reached makes a dated plan the natural next communication once Sweden is above 7.5 percent. Needs for "reduced": conversion below 90 percent, leverage above 2.7x reported, a SEK 0.50 dividend; that combination is the "false friend" path and I put it at 15 to 20 percent.
  • April 2027, Q1 2027. The first quarter with hard comparatives (+4.9 percent organic in Q1 2026) and the seasonally weakest margin (5.0 percent in Sweden in Q1 2026); the legacy roll-off claim is tested here. Guidance is most at risk of a qualitative softening at this event, not of a formal change.
  • July and October 2027, Q2 and Q3 2027. The wage round shows in employee costs; Fabri's first full year is readable; a first Nordic bolt-on is most likely in this window (55 percent in H1 2027 on our base). A restart would be presented as delivery of the 10 percent growth target rather than as new guidance.
  • The May 2027 AGM. The 2026/2029 warrant programme; no change to dividend policy expected; a buyback mandate exists and will not be used.

Why the company behaves this way, which decides the probabilities: the five targets have stood for years through a margin of 8.2 percent and of 5.9 percent; under the previous CEO the 8 percent was a through-cycle average, under the founder it is "a matter of corporate urgency" and his tenure condition; the only numerical signal in two years was the dividend cut, taken promptly and framed as deleveraging. The peer practice is the same (Bravida and Fasadgruppen guide by targets; SPIE, Burkhalter and InstallatørGruppen give numbers), and the cohort precedent for a formal downgrade is a profit warning from a position of stress (Green Landscaping, September 2026, under the same chairman), which Instalco's reiterated-targets style would only produce after a write-down. So the asymmetry is: guidance cannot be cut in the ordinary way because there is none, and it can only be raised by an act (a date, a day, a deal) the founder has so far declined; what moves is the dividend and the restart.

How this sits against our assessment, which is what the score measures: the targets sit above our base on margin (8 percent urgent against our FY2030), roughly on our base on leverage and cash (below 2.5x and 100 percent during 2027), and above it on growth (10 percent against our 6.7 percent from FY2028). The company's informal commentary (legacy contracts diminishing over several quarters, the cash shortfall as timing, Finland's 12 percent exceptional, the market uneven) is consistent with our inputs and gives no licence to model 8 percent before FY2028. The gap is therefore mildly unfavourable: there is no conservative guidance to be beaten, the only upward events are conditional on the cash print, and the 8 percent is in consensus for 2028 already, so delivering it on time earns nothing and delivering it late costs.
