Sell-side estimates

1 question
I-0232
I-02

How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (18 months), and why? Secondarily, for 18 months to three years out?

❌ D32

Question asked How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months): to be upgraded, cut or held, and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

Judgement: revenue and EBITDA consensus will be held or nudged up on the next two prints and EPS consensus will be cut, because the line the brokers have got right (the operating recovery) is the line that beats on easy comparatives and the lines they have got wrong (financial items, minorities, tax) are the lines that print below their models every quarter from now. For FY2026 EPS (median SEK 2.28, mean SEK 2.17, range SEK 1.79 to 2.30) I expect a further 3 to 7 percent cut by February 2027 to about SEK 2.10 to 2.20, mostly the lead broker (SEK 2.30 on a 21 percent tax rate and SEK 62m of minorities) coming down. For FY2027 EPS (median SEK 3.01, range SEK 2.55 to 3.01, our SEK 2.45) I put the probabilities over the next three events (to April 2027) at: cut by more than 5 percent, 55 percent; held within 5 percent, 30 percent; raised by more than 5 percent, 15 percent; and over the full 18 months (to February 2028): cut, 60 percent; held, 25 percent; raised, 15 percent, with the cut most likely to be 10 to 15 percent, to about SEK 2.60 to 2.70, concentrated at the Q4 2026 report (when the brokers roll forward with the full below-EBITA run-rate and the dividend) and the Q1 2027 report (hardening comparatives, wage round). For FY2027 revenue (SEK 17,171m against our 16,861m) and EBITDA (SEK 1,770m against our 1,686m) I expect small cuts of 2 to 5 percent, partly offset by Fabri bolt-ons; so the net breadth of revisions turns from positive on every line today (EPS +2, +4, +8 percent over one, three and six months; EBITDA and sales +11 percent over six months; targets +24 percent) to negative on EPS and flat on sales and EBITDA by mid-2027, while target prices are held because they are multiple-driven. Secondarily, FY2028 EPS (SEK 3.42, our SEK 2.79) is cut by 10 to 15 percent over 18 months; FY2029, when it is first published in early 2027, will be created at about SEK 3.6 to 3.8 and then cut toward our SEK 3.22, with the one upward force being the acquisition restart, which no broker carries in the revenue line and which, if it comes at the historical pace, adds 2 to 3 percent to revenue and 3 to 4 percent to EPS a year.

The evidence that fixes the direction, in order of weight:

  1. The revision data already show the pattern. For FY2027, EBITDA consensus is up 12 percent and sales 11 percent over twelve months (Fabri's consolidation, about 8 to 9 percent of sales, plus the Q2 beat), but EPS is up only 3 percent over twelve months and 8 percent over six. The EBITDA upgrade did not reach EPS because the brokers raised interest, minorities and amortisation at the same time (the lead broker trimmed FY2026 EPS 5 percent in July 2026 while raising EBITA); that is our chain-below-EBITA argument visible in the sell-side's own numbers before any print we expect has landed.
  2. The three brokers' dispersion is below EBITA, not above it. Nordea: the lowest FY2027 revenue (SEK 16,414m) and EBITDA (SEK 1,620m) and the highest EPS (SEK 3.01, identical to the median and to its FY2028 SEK 3.42). Pareto: the highest revenue (SEK 17,348m) and EBITDA (SEK 1,833m) and the lowest EPS (SEK 2.55). DNB Carnegie in the middle on both (SEK 16,829m, 1,762m, 2.99). A broker with EBITDA SEK 213m lower than another cannot have EPS SEK 0.46 higher on the same basis unless its chain below EBITA is about SEK 330m lighter after tax, which is implausible; so either Nordea's EPS is on a different basis (before minorities or amortisation treated differently) or it is stale against its own operating cuts. The median SEK 3.01 is therefore the figure of the two houses with the lightest chains, and the house whose chain resembles ours (Pareto) is within 4 percent of us on EPS while 3 percent above us on revenue. When Nordea's EPS is reconciled to its own EBITDA, or when FactSet drops it, the median moves toward SEK 2.75 without any operating change; that is the single most likely mechanism for the first FY2027 cut.
  3. The lead broker's financial line assumes cuts in a hiking cycle. DNB Carnegie's net financial items fall from SEK 238m (2026) to 159m (2027) and 135m (2028) "under expected Riksbank rate cuts" (AlphaSense T-I2), while the macro houses (Nordea, Morgan Stanley, Deutsche Bank) expect hikes to 2.00 to 2.50 percent by mid-2027 and Norges Bank at 4.50 percent to late 2027; the facility is floating (STIBOR-linked, at least 50 percent short interest terms, no swaps disclosed) and SEK 393m of Fabri debt was added in August. The SEK 79m gap between the broker's 2027 financial line and ours is SEK 0.22 of EPS and it will print quarter by quarter from Q3 2026 (external interest SEK 29m in Q2 2026 before Fabri's debt; about SEK 35m to 40m a quarter from Q4). This is the line on which cuts are near certain and only timing is in doubt.
  4. Minorities. The lead broker deducts SEK 62m for 2026 and 86m for 2027; the charge was SEK 36m in H1 2026 before Fabri and SEK 22m in Q2 alone; Fabri adds about SEK 14m a quarter. The H2 2026 prints will show SEK 45m to 60m on our reading, which annualises above the broker's 2027 figure; another SEK 0.10 to 0.14 of EPS.
  5. The operating lines will beat for two quarters and then meet. Q3 2026 and Q4 2026 face trough comparatives (−3.6 and +4.9 percent organic); the lead broker expects 12 percent Swedish organic growth today; Bravida's Swedish margin is expected up 70 basis points to 6.0 percent, which sets a benign sector read. Beats on volume will hold sales and EBITDA consensus and may nudge FY2026 EBITDA up 1 to 2 percent; they will not lift FY2027 EPS, because the brokers already have the margin at 7.8 to 7.9 percent for 2027 (above the lead broker's own segment arithmetic of about 7.5 percent, D-01), and Q1 2027 is where the comparatives harden (+4.9 percent) and the Swedish seasonal low (5.0 percent in Q1 2026) is tested against the legacy roll-off claim. If Q1 2027 prints Sweden at 5.5 to 6.0 percent, FY2027 EBITDA is held; below 5.5 percent it is cut 3 to 5 percent and the EPS cut compounds to 15 to 20 percent.
  6. The cut cycle for FY2026 ended in Q1 2026 (the first set's C-74), so the downside from here is from new information, not old; the new information is the run-rate below EBITA, which is new to the models but not to the accounts.

What would produce upgrades instead, and their likelihood: a Swedish segment margin at or above 7.5 percent today and again in February (about 30 percent), which would lift FY2027 EBITDA 2 to 4 percent and offset the below-EBITA cut on EPS to leave FY2027 EPS roughly held at SEK 2.9 to 3.0; a first Nordic bolt-on in H1 2027 with Fabri's first full quarter above 11 percent, which adds to revenue and EBITDA for 2028 (about 35 percent); a Riksbank reversal to cuts in 2027 (under 20 percent on the macro houses' paths). Combined, the "raised" branch for FY2027 EPS is about 15 percent, as stated.

The breadth, ratings and targets: the buy share is 86 percent after Nordea's move to Hold on 19 October 2026 (the FactSet count for the share is larger than the three named estimators), target prices SEK 45 to 48 against SEK 42.52, mean SEK 49.7, up 24 percent over six months on multiple rather than on estimates. Targets are set at 13x 2027 EV/EBITA by the lead broker and will be held through EPS cuts because the brokers will cut the chain below EBITA, not EBITA; a second downgrade to Hold (Pareto is the candidate, being the house closest to our EPS) is more likely than an upgrade from Nordea over the next two events (35 against 15 percent), which would take the buy share toward 60 percent. The dashboard's sell-side momentum rating of A is propped by targets and ratings; the Wisdom Library's warning in the first set applies and the Consumer should expect that letter to fall to B or C by mid-2027 on the EPS line alone.

Secondarily, 18 months to three years: consensus for FY2028 carries the full 8 percent, financial items at SEK 133m and no bolt-ons; our FY2028 is 18 percent below on EPS and 1 percent above on revenue. The cut on EPS is the mirror of the FY2027 cut; the upgrade on revenue (the restart) is the one line where we are above consensus and where the market will be surprised favourably if the engine runs. FY2029 consensus will be created from FY2028 plus 8 to 10 percent, which is our SEK 3.22 plus 15 percent, and will be cut as the FY2028 base is cut.

How this sits against our assessment: unfavourable on EPS (consensus is 19 percent above our base and more likely to come down than we are to go up), neutral to slightly favourable on revenue and EBITDA (within 2 to 5 percent, and the operating lines are where the next two prints beat), and favourable only on the one line nobody models (acquired growth from 2028). The score reflects a gap that works against the case over the next 18 months and for it beyond.

Stock market narrative

1 question
I-0340
I-03

How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary; how well known is each to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years?

❌ D40

Question asked How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary (for example on Twitter): how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

Judgement: the narrative is a Swedish-margin, backlog and founder story, and it is well articulated on exactly the dynamics that we judge most predictable and silent on the ones we judge least predictable. Of the fifteen dynamics tested, six are widely known and discussed by the lead broker and, on the evidence available, by investors (the source of the margin recovery as utilisation plus selectivity; bad-debt normalisation; the legacy roll-off; the data-centre absorption of competitors' capacity; the Q4 working-capital release; the acquisition restart as the re-rating catalyst); five are known to the lead broker in its model and not in its narrative, and not to investors at all (net financial items and the undisclosed facility structure; minorities after Fabri; the tax rate; amortisation after the purchase price allocation; the founder's external chairmanships, which the broker raised in January 2026 and has since treated as mitigated); and four are articulated by no one (the fade of the utilisation gain once the backlog is staffed; the reversibility of the data-centre pricing relief; the two-definition leverage print at year-end 2026 and the pro forma against reported reading; the Fabri option hurdles and formulas, which are undisclosed and therefore unarticulated by construction). The relationship between the group consensus margin and the lead broker's Swedish path is visible in the broker's own tables and discussed by nobody; the assumptions under the FY2027 consensus EPS of SEK 3.01 are discussed by the lead broker alone and only as model lines. The important qualification is on the evidence: the AlphaSense coverage check found research for only one of the three houses (DNB Carnegie; analysts Jakob Söderblom and Johan Sundén), so Nordea's and Pareto's narratives are unobserved, and the report's statement that retail and institutional forums ignore the chain below EBITA is an inference from the lead broker's description of what the market watches rather than a sampled finding. The conclusion survives the caveat, because the one house we can read is the one whose model the market uses.

Dynamic by dynamic, who says what, since when, and with what view (the numbering is the research brief's):

  1. Source of the margin recovery. Utilisation and the 2024 capacity cut: DNB Carnegie since December 2024 (35 basis points from the cut), with backlog per employee as its monitoring proxy since July 2026; the company's "value-added pricing" since February 2026. View: self-help first, pricing secondary. The fade of utilisation once hiring catches the backlog: nobody; the broker treats the incremental margin as persistent. Known: widely, except the fade.
  2. Bad debt and legacy roll-off. DNB Carnegie since January 2026 (40 basis points), the company since April 2025 (legacy pressure) and April 2026 (diminishing over several quarters). View: a step, with the broker treating it as a trend. Known: widely.
  3. The spring 2027 wage round and the fixed-price share. DNB Carnegie mentions labour shortages and wage inflation (January 2026) and the Riksbank's 3.3 percent; nobody quantifies the fixed-price exposure of the 2026 backlog or the indexed share, and the SPIE indexation contrast is made only in SPIE's own coverage (Morgan Stanley, March 2026). Known: as a risk word, not as a number; our −40 basis points net is not in any model we can see.
  4. The data-centre absorption and its reversibility. DNB Carnegie (September 2026) and the company (Q2 2026 report) articulate the second-order benefit; nobody articulates that Bravida's SEK 7bn-plus of backlog is externally financed and that the capacity returns within two quarters if it slips. Known: the benefit widely, the reversibility not at all.
  5. Net financial items. DNB Carnegie models SEK 238m, 159m, 135m and names the fixed-floating mix as undisclosed; its path assumes cuts against its own macro peers' hikes; the FactSet consensus path (SEK 234m to 133m) is the broker's. Investors: no commentary found. Known: to the lead broker as a line, to nobody as a problem.
  6. Minorities. DNB Carnegie SEK 62m and 86m, with the SEK 600m to 650m balance named in January 2026; the July 2026 trim of FY2026 EPS for "updated minority assumptions". Investors: nothing. Known: to one house, understated.
  7. Tax. DNB Carnegie 21.0 and 22.0 percent; the 28.1 percent of 2025 and the 20 percent of H1 2026 are in the accounts; no narrative. Known: to one house.
  8. Amortisation after Fabri. DNB Carnegie SEK 130m, 110m, 100m (falling); the purchase price allocation is preliminary. Known: to one house, with a view (falling) that differs from the second set's (rising) and sits nearer mine.
  9. Working capital in growth and the Q4 release. Widely discussed: the company (late invoicing, overdue receivables unchanged), DNB Carnegie (the Q4 release pattern, the Bravida 77 percent read-across, "a precursor to higher revenue"), and the peers' own disclosures. Known: widely; the structural residue in Norwegian long projects (the first set's composite reading) is not articulated.
  10. Year-end leverage on two definitions. DNB Carnegie notes the covenant basis (adjusted EBITDA, write-downs added back) and the 2.6x against 2.8x with earn-outs; nobody frames the February 2027 print as pro forma against reported, which is where a 2.6x headline could be misread as a failure. Known: the covenant comfort widely, the reading of the gate not at all.
  11. The restart's timing and scale. DNB Carnegie's SEK 800m five-year average and its "gradual restart in H2 2026", then "as leverage falls"; the company's "margin, debt, cash, then acquisitions". Known: widely, and the sell-side's timing is earlier and larger than ours (we have H2 2027 at 60 percent).
  12. The Fabri options. The 0.9x sales architecture and the 2029 and 2030 to 2033 steps are disclosed in outline; the hurdles and formulas are not; DNB Carnegie calls the structure protective. Known: as a structure; the cash call is unmodelled by anyone.
  13. The founder's chairmanships and succession. DNB Carnegie, January 2026 (criticised in industry media as overboarded; mitigated by delegation to Larsson and Persson); the tenure-until-8-percent condition is widely cited; the permanent succession is unaddressed. Known: moderately, as a governance footnote.
  14. The group consensus margin against the Swedish path. The lead broker publishes both (Sweden 7.2 and 7.6; group EBITDA 10.5 percent) and does not reconcile them; nobody else comments. Known: visible, undiscussed.
  15. The assumptions under the FY2027 EPS of SEK 3.01. Discussed only as the lead broker's model lines (SEK 2.99 on a 22 percent tax rate, SEK 159m of financial items, SEK 86m of minorities); the two-of-three structure of the median (Nordea's EPS against its own EBITDA) is discussed by nobody. Known: not.

For the business model in general and the peers: the sector narrative is Bravida's (data centres, +61 percent, the "compounder" multiple), SPIE's (indexation, recurring revenue, de-rated on French politics), Fasadgruppen's (the rights-issue cautionary tale) and Burkhalter's (an unexplained 28 percent fall). Instalco is told as the founder's restoration inside Bravida's sector story. What the sector narrative does not carry is that Instalco has no data-centre exposure and no indexation, so it borrows the sector's pricing relief and none of its structural growth; the dashboard's thematic momentum rating of D for the whole sector says the theme is not what is driving these stocks.

Secondarily, for 18 months to three years: the German platform is articulated as a runway (DNB Carnegie's "prolongs the M&A runway", the company's "five times the Nordics"), with the private-equity competition and the 7 to 9x multiples known to the lead broker and the German macro (68 percent capacity utilisation, the infrastructure fund running 20 percent below plan) known to the macro houses but not connected to Fabri by anyone except the lead broker's "more selective in Germany" remark. The 8 percent in 2028 is consensus; the 2030 we model is nobody's narrative. The founder's departure once 8 percent is reached is articulated as a condition and not as a risk.

How this sits against our assessment, which the score measures: the dynamics the narrative knows well are the ones on which we agree with it (the operating recovery and its sources), so there is no favourable gap there; the dynamics it does not know are the ones where our assessment is below consensus (the chain below EBITA, the utilisation fade, the wage round, the reversibility of pricing relief), so the gap is unfavourable: when they print, the surprise is negative and the narrative has no frame for it. The two places where the gap runs the other way, and the memo should name them as the positive side, are the acquisition restart's scale after 2028 (we are above consensus on revenue from FY2028 and the market's narrative has the engine as a catalyst without a revenue line for it) and the balance sheet by 2028 to 2029 (1.9x with SEK 500m a year of bolt-ons on the second set's build), which nobody articulates because everyone is watching the 2.5x print.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Instalco AB (INSTAL-SE, Nasdaq Stockholm), a Nordic technical installation group of about 150 decentralised subsidiaries in electrical, heating and plumbing, ventilation, industrial installation, industrial scaffolding and technical consulting, reporting the segments Sweden (about 71 percent of FY2025 net sales of SEK 13,598 million), Norway and, from Q3 2026, Finland and Germany (the German platform Fabri AG, consolidated at 51 percent from 1 August 2026 with a one-month reporting lag).

Request: OWN-1

Question: For Instalco AB (INSTAL-SE), what did the Q3 2026 interim report published on 23 October 2026 and the same-day Q3 2026 report of Bravida Holding AB (BRAV-SE) show, line by line, against what the sell-side had previewed (DNB Carnegie's 29 September 2026 preview looked for about 10 percent group organic growth with about 12 percent in Sweden, adjusted EBITA of about SEK 226 million, a recovery in operating cash flow, leverage moving toward the company's 2.5x target and the first month of Fabri AG inside the renamed Finland and Germany segment): specifically the Swedish segment's organic growth and EBITA margin against Q3 2025 (6.0 percent group adjusted EBITA margin) and Q2 2026 (Sweden 7.0 percent); group operating cash flow, trade receivables (SEK 2,009 million at 30 June 2026), contract assets (SEK 808 million) and rolling twelve-month cash conversion (90 percent at Q2 2026); net debt to EBITDA on the company's own definition and any pro forma figure for Fabri; the non-controlling-interest charge (SEK 36 million for H1 2026); net financial items by component (external loan interest was SEK 29 million in Q2 2026); the Finland and Germany segment's revenue and margin and any Fabri standalone figures; management's statements on the timing of the 8 percent EBITA margin target, the resumption of acquisitions, the FY2026 dividend, the spring 2027 Swedish wage round and the Riksbank; and how each of the three covering brokers (DNB Carnegie, Pareto Securities, Nordea) changed its estimates, rating and target price in the days after the report, with the share-price reaction on the day for Instalco and for Bravida?

Request: OWN-2

Question: For Instalco AB (INSTAL-SE), what do the published models of the three covering brokers (DNB Carnegie, Buy, target SEK 47 on 29 September 2026; Pareto Securities, Buy, target SEK 48 on 28 September 2026; Nordea Equity Research, Hold, target SEK 45 on 19 October 2026, per FactSet) assume line by line for FY2026 to FY2028 below adjusted EBITA, namely depreciation, amortisation of acquired intangibles, net financial items and the interest-rate path behind them, the effective tax rate, the minority-interest charge after the Fabri AG consolidation and the share count, and on what EPS basis each submits its estimate to FactSet, so as to explain why FactSet shows Pareto with the highest FY2027 revenue and EBITDA (SEK 17,348 million and SEK 1,833 million) but the lowest EPS (SEK 2.55) and Nordea with the lowest FY2027 revenue and EBITDA (SEK 16,414 million and SEK 1,620 million) but the highest EPS (SEK 3.01); what Nordea's 19 October 2026 note gave as the reasons for its move to Hold and what it expects for the Swedish segment margin, cash conversion and the acquisition restart; and how each broker's Swedish segment margin path for 2027 and 2028 reconciles with its group EBITDA margin for the same years?

Request: OWN-3

Question: For Instalco AB (INSTAL-SE) and the Swedish technical installation industry, what is known about the spring 2027 collective wage round for the electrical installation agreement (Installatörsföretagen with Svenska Elektrikerförbundet) and the plumbing and ventilation agreement (Installatörsföretagen with Byggnads), including the negotiation timetable, the parties' opening positions and demands as published, the outcome of the 2023 and 2025 rounds for the same agreements, the Riksbank's and the Swedish Mediation Office's expectations for 2027 wage growth, and the SEB and Nordea forecasts cited in 2026 for negotiated minimum and actual wage increases; how Swedish installation contracts under AB 04, ABT 06 and partnering forms typically treat wage and material cost indexation (the Entreprenadindex and other index clauses, their coverage and lag) compared with the Danish AB 18 indexation above twelve months that InstallatørGruppen describes; what Instalco, Bravida, Assemblin Caverion and Nimlas have disclosed about the share of their revenue or backlog on fixed-price contracts and on indexed terms; and how the 2021 to 2023 inflation period affected the margins of Swedish installers on fixed-price work signed before the cost increases, as reported by the companies and by DNB Carnegie, Deutsche Bank and ABG Sundal Collier at the time?
